# DIVERGENCES.md — wire divergences & unconfirmed inferences

The single registry of (a) **deliberate divergences** from the Anthropic Claude
Managed Agents reference and (b) **inferences** about reference behavior made where
neither the SDK types nor the `ant` CLI can reveal it — each to be confirmed against a
real managed-agents recording. The verifier's wire-compat rung resolves intentional
mismatches against this file; an intentional mismatch missing here is a defect. New
divergences land here in the same PR that introduces them.

Format: **surface** — behavior. *Evidence.* `*Tracked: #N*` names the **open** issue that will settle the entry; a closed issue may appear only as provenance — `(delivered)`, or a trailing `landed for #N` clause (the oldest entries spell it as a standalone *Landed for #N.*) — never as the live tracker. Where that issue is the live tracker of more than one entry — #78, the recording tracker, above all — it carries a parenthetical naming what *this* entry leaves open, because the issue alone cannot. `tools/registrycheck` holds the file to all of this: its shape rules run in `make verify` as that package's own test, and `make registry-check` adds the issue-state rules a credential-free gate cannot. Three sections: architecture/compatibility notes (not mismatches), CONFIRMED deliberate divergences, and INFERRED assumptions (each cross-linked to the issue tracking its confirmation).

## Architecture & compatibility notes (not divergences)

Facts that read like divergences but are not mismatches with the managed-agents wire —
recorded here so the allowlist stays honest. Honesty sometimes means naming a neighbouring
behavior that *is* a mismatch, so that a reviewer does not clear it on this entry's authority;
the mismatch itself belongs to the open bug that owns it, never to this section.

- **Session resource (all session endpoints) — end-user binding** — Sessions carry no user_id field at all. Scoping keys are org/workspace/project (reserved, single-tenant defaults in v1); end-user-to-session ownership is an application-layer concern via session metadata and the audit-only created_by. This is a deliberate architectural divergence (explicitly from adk-go's AppName+UserID pattern; the platform stays user-agnostic). *Evidence: CLAUDE.md 'Non-negotiable design principles' #5; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations" (sessions schema: 'audit-only created_by, no user_id'); CLAUDE.md standing product decisions*
- **BYOC worker (POST …/work/{work_id}/heartbeat) — a stalled run stops beating** — A worker whose run reports no progress for `WORKER_STALL_TIMEOUT` (default 30m) cancels the run and stops heartbeating, so the lease lapses server-side and the control plane re-offers the item; it never force-stops such an item, since another worker may hold it by then (#383). No request or response shape changes — the worker simply stops calling a lane the protocol already handles the absence of, which is exactly what a dead worker's lease expiry is for — so this is client-side behavior, not a wire mismatch. The reference worker has no analogue: the SDK's poller bounds only its Stop call (`stopTimeout`, 10s) and has no stall, deadline or max-runtime bound on the run itself, and the `ant` CLI's only worker-lifetime flag (`--max-idle`) stops the *worker* once its session goes idle on `end_turn`, which says nothing about a run that never returns — so nothing here contradicts observed reference behavior; the platform executor carries the same guard through `queue.KeepLease`, which is entirely internal. *Evidence: stopTimeout is the poller's only run-side bound, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller.go stopTimeout; internal/worker/lease.go (progress, hbExitStalled), internal/queue/keeper.go (stall budget, ErrWorkStalled); docs/plan/33_bounding-a-wedged-work-item.md*
- **BYOC worker (GET …/work/poll) — a backoff after an item refused before its lease was extended** — When the control plane refuses an item's heartbeat before any beat has extended its lease — a `4xx` retrying will not fix, other than `412`, or `lease_extended: false` beside a live state — the worker force-stops the item as the reference worker does, then sleeps its poll-error backoff (up to 1 s, doubling to 60 s) before the next poll, where the reference worker polls again at once. The reason is ours: this control plane's stop re-queues the session's unanswered calls at once, so a server refusing every beat would otherwise spin the worker at round-trip speed (#813). A stop the control plane asks for is not a refusal and is not followed by a backoff. Only the poll's timing changes, never a request or response shape, so this is client-side behavior, not a wire mismatch. *Evidence: the reference loop polls again at once, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — worker.go EnvironmentWorker.Run, and its poller backs off only after an item it could not ack, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller.go WorkPoller.discardUnprocessable; internal/worker/lease.go (Run, hbExit.refused), internal/api/workapi.go (stopWork, rearm); internal/worker/lease_test.go TestWorkerBacksOffAfterItemsRefusedFromTheirClaim and TestWorkerStopBeforeTheClaimWindsDown*
- **GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events/stream — SSE preview delta frames** — Streaming preview deltas use type 'content_delta', never the Messages API's 'content_block_delta'. event_start/event_delta frames are stream-only, never persisted, and reconcile under the buffered event's pre-allocated id. *Evidence: CLAUDE.md 'Wire-compatibility rules'; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (events/preview.go: 'delta type is literally content_delta, NOT the Messages API's content_block_delta')*
- **External tool waits and ordered recovery (#375)** — Custom calls and self-hosted worker-executed built-ins advertise `idle/requires_action` with their tool-use event IDs. Receipt is distinct from processing: a later reply or confirmation can be saved before an earlier call settles, while both blocking IDs remain advertised. Processing follows each thread's generated call order. Cloud execution may run between two custom calls and publish its result before the final custom reply; worker authorization permits early execution, but approval alone does not settle its result wait. Model continuation requires the thread's complete result set. This is a local implementation rule supported by observable behavior, not a claim about the reference service's internals. Platform Web/MCP and inline delegation remain platform-owned. *Evidence: [mixed-tool recordings at 146c57d734e935f7c7f1d681a3ba2612d5c1dd82](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agents-wire-recordings/blob/146c57d734e935f7c7f1d681a3ba2612d5c1dd82/2026-09-19-custom-mixed-tools/FINDINGS.md), [order follow-up at 16d24be5acdb0ee4729b8051b5363ce17991ed06](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agents-wire-recordings/blob/16d24be5acdb0ee4729b8051b5363ce17991ed06/2026-09-19-custom-order-followup/FINDINGS.md): ask-first allow/deny, worker-first, alpha/bash/beta, and reversed custom replies; internal/events/orderedtools.go and the API/worker/executor ordered-flow tests.*
- **session.status_running emission cadence** — Emitted once per idle->running flip, not once per tool result while a turn is in flight. This matches the reference's own reconciliation rule — "a turn opens with a single `session.status_running` event", after which the model-request frames repeat per request — so uninterrupted platform-tool execution produces no status transition per result. An external wait that becomes runnable emits the actual idle-to-running transition described above; a partial reply that remains blocked does not. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/events-and-streaming § "Accumulate and reconcile" (fetched 2026-08-22); docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/brain` + `internal/queue` + state machine — the orchestration loop (slice 5)"*
- **agent_toolset default permission policy — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-08-08, plan 29 slice 1)** — Defaults to always_allow (`toolset.DefaultAgentToolsetPolicy`). This entry was opened because the value rested on the plan alone: "the SDK wire types carry no resolved default to corroborate it, and one SDK create-agent example resolves a kitchen-sink agent to always_ask", flagged for a recording against a real endpoint. The public permission-policies guide states it outright, twice — "Each toolset kind has its own default: the agent toolset defaults to `always_allow`, and MCP toolsets default to `always_ask`" and "`default_config` is optional. If you omit it, the agent toolset is enabled with the default permission policy, `always_allow`" — and public docs outrank the SDK in this repo's resolution order, so the platform's value is the reference's and no recording is needed to settle it. The always_ask example that prompted the doubt is no longer present in the SDK checkout (the checkout carries no `AlwaysAsk` reference outside generated code — the union boilerplate in betaagent.go and betasession.go and two type-index links in api.md, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy, exactly as when checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.61.0 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — api.md BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicyParam and BetaManagedAgentsAlwaysAskPolicy; the tool-config union split, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolConfigUnion, multiplies the betaagent.go boilerplate without adding a hand-written use), so what it actually demonstrated cannot be re-read; it is left recorded rather than deleted because the doubt it caused is part of this entry's history. #59, which asked for that recording, is closed as answered by the public guide rather than by an endpoint. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/permission-policies (both sentences, fetched 2026-08-08); internal/toolset/definitions.go (DefaultAgentToolsetPolicy / DefaultMCPToolsetPolicy); docs/history/2026-07.md § "permission policies + the confirmation round-trip (slice 7)" (the original doubt)*
- **Every keyset-paged list — `next_page` is a `k1|…` token, not a `page_…` value** — The reference describes its list cursor as "an opaque pagination cursor (a `page_...` value)"; this platform mints `base64url("k1|<dir>|t|<unixnano>|<id>")` keyset positions instead, and has since the first list shipped (the memory-store list, plan 36 slice 1, is simply the newest to use them). Plan 36 slice 2 adds the grammar's second key kind — `k1|<dir>|m|<base64url(path)>`, for the memories list, which orders by path rather than by time; the inner encoding is what keeps a path's own `|` out of the separator the grammar splits on. Not a mismatch, because the value is opaque on both sides: a client passes back exactly what `next_page` handed it, and the SDK's own pagination carries `next_page` as a plain string it never parses or constructs. What the keyset buys is the property an offset cannot have — a page that neither duplicates nor skips a row when writes land between requests. Only a client that hardcoded the `page_` shape could tell the difference, and neither the SDK nor the CLI is one. **The key's presence on a terminal page is a different matter, and the reference answers it two ways, by route.** Five lists send an explicit `"next_page": null` when no page follows — files, skills, skill versions, environments and an environment's work. Every other list in the archive omits the key on its last page; the five of those recorded with a page still to come sent it then, and the sessions list never sent `prev_page` on a first page. This platform matches the key's presence per route (#676): `pageJSON` and `biPageJSON` omit a cursor they do not have, `nullPageJSON` and `filePageJSON` send the null, and `TestListTerminalPageEnvelope` pins every list's last page exactly and walks each one cursor by cursor. Beyond that key, the environments list answers one envelope on every beta lane, a divergence below (`The environments list — one envelope on every beta lane`), and the memories list renders the recorded `"prefixes": []` (`The memories list — the recorded prefixes key`). The lists never recorded with a page to come are the INFERRED entry `Lists recorded only on their last page`. Until #676 every list sent the null, and this entry claimed the reference agreed — generalising from #544, which had fixed the files list in the other direction (it omitted a key the reference sends as null). The typed SDK's pagers and the console end a walk on an absent key and a null alike, but a raw client that tests for the key's presence must accept both shapes. The files list now mints a `k1|…` token like the rest, so the `page_` mismatch above covers it too. *Recording: every 200 list body in managed-agents-wire-recordings, 2026-09-02 through 2026-09-24-mcp-sse-selfhosted (2,887 bodies after de-duplicating by request-id), classed absent / null / cursor; no list mixed absent and null on one lane. **Null**: files 441 null, never absent (2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 386 and 391, 2026-09-03/batch2.json idx 266 and 276 among them); skills 35 null, 6 cursors (2026-09-05/batch1.json idx 39's cursor answered by idx 41's null); skill versions 19 null, 4 cursors (2026-09-04/batch1.json idx 57 null over four versions, idx 58 a cursor at limit=1); environments 20 null on the `managed-agents-2026-04-01`, console `environments-2026-03-01` and header-less lanes, its 9 absent bodies all on the dated `environments-2025-11-01` lane, whose envelope has no `next_page` at all (both dated envelopes are the environments divergence below); work 4 null (2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 247, 296, 301, 347). **Omitted, proven by a cursor on the same list**: memory stores 24 absent, 3 cursors (2026-09-05/batch6.json idx 16 omits it over three stores at limit=100, idx 17–19 carry one at limit 1 and 2); memories 46 and 2 (2026-09-05/batch4.json idx 7 and 12 carry one over a store past 2,000 memories, batch6.json idx 7–13 omit it over five; all 48 also carry `"prefixes": []`, rendered here as the memories entry below says); memory versions 2 and 3 (2026-09-02/free_batch1.json `memver.list.nolimit` and `memver.list.limit5` carry one, `memver.list.Notes` omits it); sessions 18 and 1 (2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 172 carries one at limit=2, 2026-09-03/batch2.json idx 179 omits it over 28 sessions; none of the 19 carries `prev_page`, all of them first pages); session events 307 and 3 (2026-09-12-console-141/ui-network.json idx 238 carries one at limit=40 over a 41-event session, 2026-09-12-archived-threads/batch1.json idx 5 omits it over the same 41 at limit=100).* *Evidence: PageCursor's `next_page` is a bare string, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — pagination.go PageCursor.NextPage, and the documented "`page_...` value", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreListParams.Page; the pagers stop on an empty `next_page`, which an absent key and a null both decode to, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — pagination.go PageCursor.GetNextPage and BidirectionalPageCursor.GetNextPage; internal/api/page.go encodeTimeCursor / decodeCursor and the five envelopes; internal/api/page_test.go TestListTerminalPageEnvelope, TestListRoutesAreClassified and TestSDKPagersEndOnEitherTerminalEnvelope.*
- **No DELETE on /v1/deployments, mirroring the reference's own absence (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference publishes a deployment.deleted webhook but exposes no delete path: enumerating the spec's paths shows post and get on /v1/deployments, get and post on /v1/deployments/{deployment_id}, and post alone on archive, pause, unpause and run — no delete method anywhere — and BetaDeploymentService has no Delete. Archive is the terminal verb here as it is there, and unarchive exists in neither. *Evidence: the six deployment paths and their methods, one generated service method per path and verb, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.New and BetaDeploymentService.Get and BetaDeploymentService.Update and BetaDeploymentService.List and BetaDeploymentService.Archive and BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Run and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, and no Delete, absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Delete; internal/api/server.go (the seven routes registered, none of them DELETE).*
- **Daylight-saving semantics: a nonexistent wall clock is skipped, a doubled one fires twice (plan 37 slice 1)** — Confirmed against the public docs page, which states both outcomes outright; this entry was opened as an inference because neither machine-readable source carries any DST text — a spec-wide grep for daylight / DST / non-existent / ambiguous returns one unrelated hit, and the two cron schedule schemas say only "Literal wall-clock matching in the configured timezone", which decides neither case. The docs page decides both, and this platform matches it: a 02:30 that does not exist on the spring-forward day is skipped; a 01:30 that occurs twice on the fall-back day fires twice, as two rows with distinct `scheduled_at` instants — which is why that column is `timestamptz` rather than `timestamp`, since a wall-clock column would collide the pair on the occurrence index and swallow the second fire. Not a divergence, then, but a behavior worth keeping registered: the two rules are invisible in every typed source, and the second is the kind a reimplementation silently gets wrong. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-29 — "Wall-clock times that do not exist on a spring-forward day (such as 2 AM) are not triggered. Wall-clock times that occur twice on a fall-back day fire twice."); no DST text on any field of the two cron schedule schemas, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule; internal/cron/cron.go instantsFor and walk, internal/cron/cron_test.go TestDSTSpringForwardSkips / TestDSTFallBackFiresTwice / TestDSTHalfHourZone; internal/store/migrations/0031_deployments.sql (the scheduled_at comment).*
- **A fired session's metadata is empty, not the deployment's — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02 and 2026-09-05, #78) (plan 37 slice 4)** — A deployment fire creates its session with `metadata: {}` whatever the deployment's own bag holds, and so does the reference: two recording waves each read a fired session back, resolved its `deployment_id` to the deployment's create body, and found `{}`. The argument made before either wave stands: CLAUDE.md principle 5 names session metadata as the application layer's ownership hook, so a schedule that wrote into it would overwrite the one seam apps are told to use. That argument was registered jointly with the fired session's title, under one "no source says" hedge, but it never covered the title — which the same probes showed this platform had wrong, and which is now the converged `A fired session is titled with the deployment's name` entry among the divergences. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`deployment.run.session.get`, `deployment.create.cron`) and the 2026-09-05 recording (`rec84.session.get.before-delete`, `rec84.adjacency.two-adjacent`); deployment metadata and session metadata — two separate bags with no stated relationship, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeployment.properties.metadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateSessionParams.properties.metadata; internal/api/deploymentruns.go deploymentSessionIn.*
- **Work API auth — a Bearer key naming the wrong environment is 403 `permission_error` (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — A recording of the reference answered a live environment key used against another environment's `/work/poll` with **403** `{"type":"permission_error","message":"Token not authorized for this environment"}`, and answered an *unknown* environment id byte-identically — so not 404 either. This platform now returns exactly that, from the single `workScope` check all eight work routes share. The recording reached only the poll route, so the uniformity across the other seven is an extrapolation and is registered as its own INFERRED entry rather than claimed here. The entry this replaces asserted **401 `authentication_error`**, argued partly on the claim that "no permission_error type exists in the envelope"; that premise was false of the reference and, since #56/plan 31, of this platform too. The same recording refutes it twice over: the reference also answers an environment key whose OAuth scope does not cover a route with `permission_error` ("OAuth token does not meet scope requirement any_of(…)"), so `permission_error` is not confined to the human lane — the comment on `errTypePermission` said it was, and no longer does. The reversal is recorded rather than deleted because the old status was pinned by two tests, and the second of them was pinning a **property this change gives up**: `TestEnvironmentKeyValueBindsToOneEnvironment` required a wrong-environment refusal to be indistinguishable from an unknown key, so that a refusal never revealed a token was genuine. The reference distinguishes them deliberately — a garbage bearer is 401 `authentication_error` "Invalid bearer token", a live key aimed elsewhere is this 403 — so the property is dropped in favour of matching it. The trade is narrow and worth stating: the reference reveals that a token is genuine but never *which* environment it covers, because a real-but-uncovered id and a nonexistent one answer with the identical message (now pinned verbatim, precisely so that indistinguishability cannot regress unnoticed), and a caller holding a live key already learns it is genuine from the environment it does cover. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.wrong-env.cloud` and the unknown-env probe beside it); internal/api/workapi.go workScope, internal/api/errors.go errTypePermission/errForbidden; internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkPollRequiresEnvironmentKey and TestWorkLifecycleRoutesScopeAndMethod, internal/api/worklist_test.go TestWorkListAuthAndEmpty.*
- **GET /v1/environments/{id}/work/poll — an empty queue really is 200 with a null body (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — Recorded: an empty poll answers 200 with a bare `null`, not 204, which is what this platform already did. The entry this replaces chose `null` for status uniformity and flagged 204 as "equally plausible server-side, to confirm against a recording"; it is confirmed. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.empty.block_ms=500`, `work.poll.after-force-stop`); internal/api/workapi.go pollWork.*
- **POST /v1/environments/{id}/work/poll (wrong method on the poll path) — the update route's 400, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-12, settling #63; aligned for #841)** — A POST to the poll path folds into the `{work_id}` update route as work_id="poll", which the SDK's route table implies (POST v1/environments/{id}/work/{work_id} is the Update method, beside a separate GET …/work/poll, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Update and BetaEnvironmentWorkService.Poll), and the reference answers neither of the two outcomes this entry used to weigh, the 404 on the nonexistent item this platform gave and a method-restricted 405: with `{}` it refused the body, `metadata: Field required`, and with a valid patch it refused the id — 400 `invalid_request_error`, `Invalid work ID format: expected session_*, cse_*, or work_* prefix, got "poll".` — while a PUT there was the 405 (2026-09-12 batch1 #58, #59 and #60, `rec91.work.poll.*`, under the console's cookie lane, which reached the route where an environment key is refused on the reference's update: *Work API auth — whether the wrong-environment 403 is uniform across the work routes*). This platform answers all three as recorded, in the recorded order — the body, then the id (checkWorkID), then the item — under the environment key its work routes take. The id check reaches the other four `{work_id}` routes too, unrecorded (*The reference's recorded error sentences, answered where no recording reaches*, below). *Evidence: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-12/batch1.json idx 58–60 and 2026-09-12/FINDINGS.md § 6; the assumption it settles, docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — the work-item metadata update (slice 8, PR C-meta)"; internal/api/wire.go checkWorkID, internal/api/workapi.go updateWork; internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkPollRejectsWrongMethodAndPath.* *Landed for #841.*
- **BetaSelfHostedWork — unreached lifecycle timestamps render null, and `started_at` marks the item's entry to the queue (confirmed 2026-09-02 and 2026-09-19)** — Recorded: `acknowledged_at`, `stop_requested_at`, `stopped_at` and `latest_heartbeat_at` are `null` before their transitions, as this platform renders them. `started_at` is no transition stamp: the reference sets it on a freshly enqueued item still in `state: "queued"`, about 100–150 ms after `created_at`, re-stamps it when a stopped item re-enters the queue under the same id, and leaves it alone through poll, ack, heartbeat and stop — so it reads "the item is queued", not "a worker has it". This platform stamps it at enqueue too, equal to `created_at` (one statement's `now()`) where the reference's lands a beat later. A re-queue here is a new row (*Work items (poll / get / list / stats) — when one is enqueued*), which carries its own stamp; a reclaim re-stamps the row it returns to the queue (*Work-item identity*). *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.poll.empty`, a queued item carrying `started_at`); the 2026-09-19 self-hosted recordings — 11 first enqueues and 2 same-id re-queues across `2026-09-19-self-hosted-docker`, `-self-hosted-latest-cli`, `-custom-mixed-tools` and `-custom-order-followup` (ant 1.10.0 and 1.34.0), and 37 ack and stop bodies carrying the `started_at` their poll showed; internal/queue/queue.go EnqueueThread and PollOn; internal/api/workapi.go toWire.* *Landed for #542.*
- **POST …/work/{work_id}/stop — response shape: 200 with the work object, a repeat stop included (confirmed 2026-09-02 and 2026-09-19, #804)** — Recorded: each of the 27 distinct stops the recordings hold answers **200 with the `BetaSelfHostedWork` after the transition**, `Content-Type: application/json` — 3 in state `stopping`, 24 in `stopped` — never a bodiless 204, and none is refused. That is the spec's declared return, and this platform answers it from the one renderer `GET …/work/{work_id}` uses: the recorded body's 14 keys, the typed schema's 13 and an `actor` that is always null (*BetaSelfHostedWork — the recorded `actor` key, rendered null*), and an `id` of our own (*Work item `id` — a `work_` id here, the session's id there*). The recordings settle the conflicting stops too: all 13 stops of work already `stopped`, 11 graceful and 2 forced, answer 200 with the item unchanged, `stop_requested_at` and `stopped_at` included, where this platform used to refuse them with a 409; the one forced stop of `stopping` work moves it to `stopped`, stamping `stopped_at` and keeping the first `stop_requested_at`, as it always did here. So a stop that moves nothing now answers the item as it stands, moves no timestamp and re-arms nothing, and past validation and auth the one error left is the 404 for an item the work API cannot see. A graceful stop of `stopping` work, the one repeat no recording reaches, is answered the same way, and is registered as an inference: *POST …/work/{work_id}/stop — a graceful stop of work already stopping answers it unchanged*. The 3 `stopping` answers come from graceful stops of acked `starting` work, which this platform, since #810, parks in `stopping` too, answering the claim that follows such a stop as recorded: *POST …/work/{work_id}/stop — graceful vs force semantics*. **This is the route's second reversal, and the chain is kept so that it stays auditable.** It first answered 200 with the updated object, on the grounds that a 204 makes the SDK's typed decoder error. #27 moved it to a bodiless 204 with no Content-Type, reading the SDK work poller's present-tense comment, "Today the server returns 204 with no body / no Content-Type", and its `WithResponseBodyInto` workaround as a description of the deployed service that outranked the spec (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller.go stopWork); that entry said only a recording could close the spec-vs-runtime gap. The recordings close it the other way: the service answers what the spec declares, and the 204 reading is falsified. Our BYOC worker keeps the poller's bypass, which serves this 200 and an older server's 204 alike, and still ignores an older server's 409; the real `ant beta:environments:work stop` reads the body as raw bytes and prints the object (checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betaenvironmentwork.go handleBetaEnvironmentsWorkStop). *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`work.stop.graceful`, `work.stop.force` and `work.stop.again`: one graceful and two forced stops of already-stopped work, each a 200 `stopped` with the item unchanged); the 2026-09-19 recordings' `worker-network.json` across `-self-hosted-latest-cli`, `-self-hosted-docker`, `-custom-mixed-tools` and `-custom-order-followup`, 24 stops: 3 graceful stops of acked `starting` work answered `stopping` (latest-cli #2 and #5, mixed-tools #23), 10 forced stops of active work and 1 of `stopping` work (mixed-tools #47) answered `stopped`, and 10 graceful stops of `stopped` work answered it unchanged (the sets' `worker-wire/` files repeat these records and add none); internal/api/workapi.go stopWork; internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkStopAnswersTheWorkObject, internal/api/threadexec_test.go TestWorkRepeatStopDoesNotReArm and TestWorkStopLosingARaceDoesNotReArm, internal/worker/lease_test.go TestWorkerForceStopAcceptsTheWorkObject, TestWorkerForceStopAcceptsNoContent, TestWorkerForceStopIgnoresAnOlderServersConflict and TestSDKTypedStopDecodesTheWorkObject; docs/plan/57_work-stop-200.md, and docs/plan/04_work-stop-204.md with CHANGELOG.md § [0.2.0] → Fixed for the 204 reversal.* *Landed for #804; the 204 reversal it undoes landed for #27.*
- **POST /v1/agents/{id} — an explicit null `version`, and a field-less update, are both 200 no-ops (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78)** — A recording settled both cases this platform had decided the other way, and the code now matches. (1) `{"version": null}` answers **200** with `version` and `updated_at` unchanged — not the 400 this platform returned, which had been argued from `param.Opt` representing null and omitted as distinct states. What the recording pins is `{"version": null}` *alone*, so what it proves is that the null form is not a 400; reading it as full equivalence with omission — that `{"version": null, "name": "x"}` applies the rename unconditionally rather than 400ing — is ours, and is the more client-visible half of the two. It is a safe inference (no reading of the recorded 200 makes the null-plus-field body a 400) but it is an inference, and `internal/api/agents_test.go` labels the two halves apart for exactly that reason. (2) A body naming no mutating field — `{}` — answers **200** with `version`, `updated_at` and the `/versions` list all unchanged, where this platform used to bump the version and write an `agent_versions` snapshot. A version-only body carrying a matching integer is treated the same way here, by the same reasoning; the recording pins only the null form of it, so that much is ours. The precondition still applies to a no-op that carries one: a stale version-only body is still 409. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`agent.update.version-null`, `agent.update.empty-body`, and `agent.versions.list` taken after both, which grew no snapshot); internal/api/agents.go updateAgent (the no-op short-circuit, placed after the existence/archived/optimistic checks); internal/api/agents_test.go TestAgentUpdateOptimisticVersioning.*
- **Session archive/delete — the running-session refusal, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02) (plan 24 slice 1)** — The reference docs pin the behavior ("A `running` session cannot be archived; to archive one, send a `user.interrupt` event by itself and wait for the session to become `idle`", and the same sentence for delete) but not the refusal's wire shape. Ours is a 400 `invalid_request_error` in the standard envelope. **The status is recorded now, and it matches** (2026-09-02): the reference refused both with 400 `invalid_request_error` in the standard envelope, and since #540 so does this platform in its words — "Session sesn_… cannot be archived while its status is \"running\". Only pending or idle sessions may be archived." and "Cannot delete session while it is running. Send an interrupt event or wait for the session to complete." — where it used to name the event to post (`user.interrupt`) and keep that as the more actionable sentence. The archive sentence's "only pending or idle" overstates this platform's rule, which refuses `running` alone (below); it is only ever said of a running session. The check runs under the session row lock in the same transaction as the mutation, so a confirmation flipping the session to running cannot slip between the check and the archive/delete; whether the reference closes the same window is unobservable from outside. Statuses other than `running` do not refuse, since the docs name only running; that this admits a session at `rescheduling` or one already `terminated`, to either verb, is an inference recorded in the session-threads entry under INFERRED, beside the thread archive's opposite rule. No gate-token work rides along: `gatetoken.Authenticate` already fails closed on archived sessions. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/session-operations ("Archiving a session", "Deleting a session", fetched 2026-09-16); internal/api/sessions.go (requireNotRunning); internal/api/sessions_test.go TestRunningSessionArchiveAndDeleteRejected* A second recording, 2026-09-03, re-drew that same 400 and that same sentence on `DELETE /v1/sessions/{id}` — so the refusal is now twice-observed rather than newly settled — and added the fact the first wave had not: `{"type":"user.interrupt"}` — whose event carries only `{id, type}` — is accepted immediately but **does not preempt an in-flight model request**: the session stayed `running` through thirty seconds of polling and settled at the next work-cycle boundary, so the advice that message gives is advice about the *next* boundary, not an immediate stop. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, the `sweep*` and interrupt probes, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md § "Incidental wire facts".* What remains open is not an inference and never will be: whether the reference closes the same check-to-mutation window under a row lock is invisible from outside, so no recording can settle it and none is waited on. *Landed for #64.*
- **Permission gating — mixed always_ask / always_allow turns, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03)** — A turn calling both an always_ask and an always_allow tool gates the whole turn: even the allow tools wait for confirmation (no partial execution while a confirmation is outstanding). requires_action.event_ids still lists only the ask tools. Filed as a deliberate simplicity/safety decision while reference behavior for mixed turns was unspecified; **it is the reference's behavior too.** A recorded agent with `bash` at `always_ask` and `write` at `always_allow`, asked for both in one response, produced two `agent.tool_use` events in one turn — `bash` stamped `evaluated_permission: "ask"`, `write` stamped `"allow"` — and parked on `session.thread_status_idle` and `session.status_idle`, both carrying `stop_reason.type: "requires_action"` whose `event_ids` names the `bash` call alone. A listing taken thirty-nine seconds into that park holds no `agent.tool_result` at all, so the allow call is not answered while the ask confirmation stands. Both results appear only after the confirmation, `bash` first and `write` 260 ms later — the spacing of sequential execution after release rather than of a result computed during the gate and flushed at resume, though what the wire shows is the absence of the *event*, the file's creation time not being on it. The decision therefore stands as the reason the code reads this way, not as a divergence. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "permission policies + the confirmation round-trip (slice 7)" ('Design decision — a mixed turn gates everything, not just its ask tools'); internal/brain/brain.go (the askIDs gate, which enqueues no tool_exec, and the allow/ask stamp beside it); internal/api/events.go (the confirmation that clears the last ask, then enqueues the exec that drains both); internal/brain/brain_test.go TestMixedPolicyTurnGatesEverythingButNamesOnlyAsk* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `agent.create.mixed-perm`, `session.create.mixed-perm`, `session.events.send.mixed-perm`, `session.events.list.mixed-perm` (the gate snapshot), `session.events.send.tool-confirmation-allow`, `session.events.list.mixed-perm-after-confirm` and `session.events.list.mixed-perm-final`; all three listings are forward-chronological and each is an ordered prefix of the next, so the empty gate window is not an ordering artefact.*
- **A dream's `session_id` and `outputs[]` appear in the same commit as `running` (plan 41 slice 2)** — The guide tells a client to expect a gap: the output store id "appears in the dream's `outputs[]` shortly after the dream starts `running`, once the workflow has cloned the input store", so a `running` dream can briefly report an empty `outputs[]`. This platform opens no such gap. Its start arm clones the store, inserts the transcript file rows, creates the pipeline session and sets `status`, `session_id` and `outputs[]` in one transaction, so a poller sees `pending` with both empty and then `running` with both filled, and never the state between them. That is not a mismatch — a client written for the reference's gap is served correctly by a platform that never opens one, and nothing on the wire says the gap must exist. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (the "shortly after" sentence); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.2 and §8.2 item 4 (the poll that would say whether the reference's gap is observable at all); internal/api StartDreamRunner (the start arm's write transaction)*
- **An archived memory store refuses new content with a 400 and admits erasure — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 1, decision 3)** — The reference says an archived store is "read-only" and that archiving is one-way, and stops there: no status, no error type, no statement of which routes the word covers. A recording answers all three, and this platform matches it. On an archived store `POST /v1/memory_stores/{id}` and the memory create and update routes answer **400** `invalid_request_error` — the vault rule this platform already had, worded since #540 as the reference words it, `cannot modify archived resource: memory store …`, where it used to say `memory store … is archived` — while a memory delete answers **200** `memory_deleted`, and so does the redaction of a version no memory heads: the line runs between new content and erasure, not between reads and writes. The delete drew the 400 here until #685 moved it to the permissive lock redaction already took; it still writes its tombstone version. Retrieve and list keep serving the store, which the reference documents ("Returns the store, including archived stores") and the recording shows. The first archive moves `updated_at` to `archived_at`, as the reference's did on both recorded stores, though the spec defines the field as when name, description or metadata last changed; this platform left it alone by that definition until #685 chose the recording, which every other archive route here already followed. Migration 0044 backfills the stores archived before then, setting `updated_at` to `archived_at`, the value the route would have written: an archived store refuses every update, so nothing moved the column after its archive. A second archive returns 200 with both timestamps unchanged, as recorded. A head's redaction stays refused on an archived store, a content rule the head-redaction entry records. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `store.update.archived.rename`, `mem.create.on-archived-store`, `mem.update.archived-store`, `mem.delete.archived-store`, `memver.redact.archived.non-head`, `store.get.archived`, `store.list.include_archived`, `store.archive`, `store.archive.again` and `store.archive.Notes`); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystore.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStore.ArchivedAt, and the spec's `archived_at` ("Set once and never cleared; archiving is one-way. Archived stores are read-only and cannot be attached to new sessions"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStore.properties.archived_at; internal/api/memories.go lockMemoryStoreForWrite / deleteMemory; internal/api/memorystores.go updateMemoryStore / archiveMemoryStore; internal/api/vaults.go updateVault (the rule this mirrors); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryArchivedStoreRefusesNewContentAdmitsDelete; internal/api/memorystores_test.go TestMemoryStoreCRUD; internal/store/migrations/0044_memory_stores_archived_updated_at.sql and internal/store/memorystores_backfill_test.go TestArchivedMemoryStoresBackfillUpdatedAt; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 3* *Landed for #685.*
- **A memory delete's `expected_content_sha256` — a malformed value is a 400, a well-formed mismatch a 409 `memory_precondition_failed_error` — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — Delete's precondition rides the query string instead of a body, and how it fails is stated nowhere — not in the SDK, whose param carries only the doc string "Query parameter for expected_content_sha256", and not in the guides; the reference's own worker treats 409 and 412 as the same outcome when its delete is refused. A recording separates shape from mismatch. Sixty-four zeros, a well-formed digest that does not match, draw **409** `memory_precondition_failed_error` — the update precondition's spec-stated status and type, which this platform already answered, and since #540 in the recorded words, "precondition content_sha256 failed: content has changed", the supplied and stored digests going to the operator's log. `nothex` draws **400** `invalid_request_error` ("expected_content_sha256: must be 64 characters (and 1 more validation errors)" — the sentence this platform answers since #540 for a value failing both the length and the hex rule, while one failing a single rule keeps ours, `… must be 64 lowercase hex characters`), where this platform let any storable text fall through to the 409 until #684; it now refuses anything but contentDigest's own shape, 64 lowercase hex characters, before the comparison, so a well-formed digest's 200 or 409 does not move, and only an absent parameter is no precondition at all. Three readings here are ours, since the recording probed one malformed value. That a 64-character value outside lowercase hex is a 400 too: `nothex` is both too short and not hex, and its second validation error is unnamed, so it does not say which alphabet the reference checks. And that a parameter supplied empty is a 400 like any other shape rather than no precondition — the SDK sends one for `param.NewOpt("")`, whose `Valid()` holds for the empty string (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — packages/param/option.go Opt.Valid), and which this platform used to read as no precondition, deleting unconditionally. And that a query string which does not parse (`%zz`, an unescaped `;`) is a 400 too, since Go's query parser drops such a pair and a corrupted precondition would otherwise read as absent. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `mem.delete.malformed-sha` and `mem.delete.wrong-sha`); BetaMemoryStoreMemoryDeleteParams, the query-side `expected_content_sha256` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryDeleteParams.ExpectedContentSha256), and the update precondition's documented 409 (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsPreconditionParam); deleteRemote, whose `case isStatus(err, 409), isStatus(err, 412)` collapses the two (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go SessionMemoryStores.deleteRemote); internal/api/memories.go deleteMemory; internal/memsync/tree.go IsDigest; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryDeletePrecondition* *Landed for #684.*
- **Redacting a memory's current head is a 400 `invalid_request_error`, on an archived store too — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02, #78) (plan 36 slice 2, decisions 3 and 6)** — The reference states the rule and not its status: "A version that is the current head of a live memory cannot be redacted." A recording answers **400** `invalid_request_error` ("version is the current content of mem_…; write a new version first") on a live store and on an archived one alike, and this platform answers the same status and type, and since #540 the same words, naming the memory the version still heads. The archived store is where the two differed until #686: this platform read "live" as "not archived" and allowed the head's redaction there, emptying the `memories` row to `""`, on the argument that nothing can supersede a head in a store that takes no new content. The reference keeps the rule unconditional, and so does this platform now. A head's bytes on an archived store are reached instead by deleting the memory, which an archived store admits (the archived-store entry), after which the version heads nothing and redacts like any other — a sequence the recording did not probe on the reference. A second redaction of an already-redacted version returns it unchanged, `redacted_at` included, as the recording shows the reference doing. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `memver.redact.head`, `memver.redact.archived.head`, `memver.redact.non-head`, `memver.redact.non-head.again` and `mem.get.archived-store.after-redact`, the last still serving the head's bytes); platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the head rule, and "Archiving makes a store read-only… Archiving is one-way"); Redact, a bodiless POST, and its params (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionService.Redact and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionRedactParams) and the four nulled fields (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryVersion); internal/api/memoryversions.go redactMemoryVersion; internal/api/memoryversions_test.go TestMemoryVersionRedact / TestMemoryVersionRedactOnAnArchivedStore; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decisions 3 and 6* *Landed for #686.*
- **Memory-store update — an explicit JSON null on `name`, `description` or `metadata` — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03, #78) (plan 36 slice 1)** — The update body's schema admits null on all three fields and gives it no meaning, and the SDK's `omitzero` never sends one, so this platform read each null by the rule its sibling resources apply until a recording answered all three. It now matches each answer. A null `name` is a **400** `invalid_request_error` worded `name cannot be empty`; it was already a 400 here, worded `name cannot be cleared`. A null `description` is a **200** that clears it to `""`, exactly as the documented `""` does, which is what this platform already did. A null `metadata` bag is a **400** `invalid_request_error` worded `Failed to parse request: params: field "metadata": null is not a valid value for a map field; omit the field to preserve, or set individual keys to null to delete them`, where this platform answered 200 and kept the bag; a null *inside* the bag still deletes its key, as the same recording shows. Each refusal leaves the store as it was, `updated_at` included. Only the memory-store update changed: the other updates that take a metadata patch (agents, environments, sessions, deployments, vaults and vault credentials) still read a null bag as "keep it", and no recording sent them one. What the recordings leave open on this route is the entry *Memory-store update — an empty body is the recorded 400; an empty metadata bag, an empty name's wording and the order of the refusals are ours*. *Evidence: the 2026-09-03 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch1.json, probes `store.update.name-null`, `store.update.description-null`, `store.update.metadata-null` and `store.update.metadata-key-null`, each read back by the `store.get.after-*` probe beside it); the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryStoreRequestBody`, each field `anyOf [T, null]`, no stated meaning (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryStoreRequestBody), and the SDK's `param.Opt` fields, omitted when unset (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreUpdateParams); internal/api/memorystores.go updateMemoryStore; internal/api/memorystores_test.go TestMemoryStoreUpdateSemantics* *Landed for #817.*
- **Memory create and update — an explicit JSON null on `content`, `path` or the precondition's hash — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03, #78) (plan 36 slice 2)** — The create schema lists `content` as required yet types it `anyOf [string, null]`, and the update schema admits null on both `content` and `path` with no stated meaning; the SDK never sends one. The reference was recorded answering each, and this platform now gives its answers word for word: a create with a null `content` is a **400** `content: value is required` (an absent one, unrecorded, takes the same words); an update whose `content`, or `content` and `path`, are null is the update with neither field, a **400** `v1_update_memory_params: at least one of content or path must be provided`, as is an empty body; and a precondition whose `content_sha256` is null is a **400** `precondition.content_sha256: Value is not nullable` (an absent or empty hash takes, since #540, the reference's recorded `precondition.content_sha256: must be 64 characters (and 1 more validation errors)` — *A memory-update precondition without its `content_sha256` is a 400*). Each refusal leaves the memory as it was. These were 400s here before, worded `content is required` and `update requires content or path`. *Evidence: the 2026-09-03 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch1.json, probes `memory.create.content-null`, `memory.update.content-null`, `memory.update.content-and-path-null`, `memory.update.empty-body`, `memory.update.precondition-sha-null`, read back by `memory.get.after-null-updates`); the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams` and `BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams; internal/api/memories.go createMemory, updateMemory and parsePrecondition; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryNullsTakeTheRecordedWords* *Landed for #817.*
- **Renaming a memory onto an ancestor or a descendant of another memory's path — the same 409, no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02) (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The occupancy rule itself is spec-stated and mirrored verbatim: a create's path "must be unoccupied: if a memory already exists at the path, or the path is an ancestor or descendant of an existing memory's path, the request returns `memory_path_conflict_error` (HTTP 409)", and renaming "onto a path occupied by a different memory" returns the same error. What no source states is the third case — a *rename* onto an ancestor or a descendant, `/a/b` existing and `/notes.md` renamed to `/a`. This platform runs one predicate for both operations, so a rename answers exactly what a create at that path would, `conflicting_memory_id` and `conflicting_path` included — and the reference was since recorded answering the third case the same way: renames onto an ancestor and onto a descendant of another memory were each a 409 `memory_path_conflict_error` with both members (2026-09-02 free_batch1 `mem.rename.onto-ancestor`, `mem.rename.onto-descendant-of-other`). Since #540 the messages are its too, which word an exact-path conflict apart from a prefix one and a create's apart from a rename's: "path `…` is already used by `mem_…`; use update to modify it" and "…; delete it first to rename-and-replace", and "path `…` conflicts with existing memory at `…`: a memory and a path prefix of it cannot coexist. Delete or rename the other memory first." The predicate is a literal `left()` comparison and never `LIKE`: `_` and `%` are legal path bytes, and a pattern would make `/acb/x` occupy `/a_b`. *Evidence: the spec's BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams ("must be unoccupied…"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams, and BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams ("Rename never overwrites; delete or rename the blocking memory first"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUpdateMemoryParams; internal/api/memories.go occupiedBy; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryPathOccupancy; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Landed for #540.*
- **A memory-update precondition without its `content_sha256` is a 400 — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03) (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The precondition object's schema requires only `type`; `content_sha256` is optional in both the spec and the SDK (`param.Opt`), and what the reference does with `{"precondition": {"type": "content_sha256"}}` is unobserved. This platform refuses it with a 400 rather than treating a precondition with nothing to compare against as an unconditional write — the silent alternative is the one a sync client would never notice — and so does the reference: `{"type": "content_sha256"}` answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "precondition.content_sha256: must be 64 characters (and 1 more validation errors)" (2026-09-03 batch1 `memory.update.precondition-type-only`), the words this platform answers since #540, an empty hash taking them too. A precondition with no `type` at all answered "Failed to parse request: precondition.type: Field required" (`memory.update.precondition-no-type`), likewise matched. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsContentSha256Precondition`, `required: ["type"]` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsContentSha256Precondition.required); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsPreconditionParam; internal/api/memories.go updateMemory; internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryUpdateSemantics ("a bodyless precondition")* *Landed for #540.*
- **A missing memory store at fire time settles session_resource_not_found_error — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-03) (plan 37 slice 3)** — The run-error union carries memory_store_archived_error but no store-specific not-found arm, so a store deleted between deployment create and the fire settles the generic session_resource_not_found_error, §5.2's "other resource gone" row. A recording settled which arm the reference uses: a deployment whose store was deleted ran to `session_resource_not_found_error`, "session creation rejected: a referenced resource was not found; check deployment configuration" (2026-09-03 batch1 idx 116 `deployment.run.store-deleted`), naming neither the kind nor the store, and its archived twin ran to `memory_store_archived_error`, "session creation rejected: a referenced memory store is archived; check deployment resources" (idx 119 `deployment.run.store-archived`). Both run messages are the reference's here since #540 — the run's own sentences, not the session create's 404 and 400, which a POST /v1/sessions keeps — and a dream's start, never recorded, words both refusals in ours. *Evidence: the 2026-09-03 recording (batch1 idx 116 and 119); the spec's BetaManagedAgentsRunError union (sixteen arms: an archived-store arm and the generic session_resource_not_found_error, no store-not-found arm), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsRunError; internal/api/sessionresources.go snapshotMemoryStore, internal/api/deploymentruns.go runWording; internal/api/deploymentscheduler_test.go TestDeletedMemoryStoreFailsAFireAsClassified, internal/api/deploymentruns_test.go TestDeploymentRunRecordsAClassifiedFailure, internal/api/sessioncaller_internal_test.go TestSessionCreateRefusalsAreWordedPerCaller.* *Landed for #540.*
- **web_fetch / web_search — the model-facing definitions are the recorded reference's — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02)** — This platform's definitions for the two web tools began as inferences, the reference's being unobservable in every source; a 2026-09-02 session, asked to print every tool it was given, echoed both, and ours now match them. **Schemas**, keyword for keyword: `web_fetch` is `{"type":"object","properties":{"url":{"type":"string","format":"uri","description":"The URL to fetch content from"}},"required":["url"],"additionalProperties":false}` and `web_search` the same shape over `"query":{"type":"string","minLength":2,"description":"The search query to use"}`. The recording's two web `agent.tool_use` events carry exactly `{"url":"https://example.com/"}` and `{"query":"example domain"}`, so the inferences this platform was built on held — the field names, a required string each, and no tuning knob (no max-results count, timeout, `max_content_tokens` or `user_location`). **Descriptions**, word for word since an owner decision of 2026-10-01: `web_fetch`'s usage notes, and `web_search`'s `<when_to_use_web_search>` / `<query_guidelines>` / `<response_guidelines>` brief, whose last query guideline is a date line rendered per request — the INFERRED entry on that line below says how, and what it costs. The text is the reference's guidance about the reference's tools and is not adjusted for our backends: `web_fetch`'s "a web page or a PDF" holds only as far as the configured Reader converts PDFs, and its promise that the tool "can only fetch EXACT URLs" the user gave or a search or fetch returned is enforced by the executor as the entry on it below describes (#823). The executor holds `web_search`'s `minLength: 2` itself — code points of the query as sent, untrimmed, with a blank query refused as it was before. **A neighbouring mismatch this entry does not clear:** on an OpenAI-protocol route the built-in web tools' three schema keywords are stripped, a deliberate divergence entered below. **The evidence is a model-mediated echo**, not a captured provider request: consistent with the recorded tool inputs, but not the wire itself, so the byte-level description text rests on the echoing model's fidelity. *Evidence: internal/toolset/definitions.go (the web_fetch/web_search entries) and internal/toolset/webdescriptions.go; TestWebToolSchemasMatchTheRecording, TestWebToolDescriptionsMatchTheRecording; TestGenerateBuiltinToolSchemaConstraintsReachTheWire (the anthropic adapter sends the keywords on); internal/executor/webwork.go runWebTool (TestWebSearchHoldsTheSchemasMinimumQueryLength)* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json, probe `sessA.events.after-turn2` (the echo), and the two web `agent.tool_use` events, resolved by `name`.* *Landed for #47, #682.*
- **Environment kind mutability (cloud <-> self_hosted) — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-12)** — Kind is immutable after creation: a config update that flips cloud<->self_hosted is refused 400 `invalid_request_error`, carrying `details.error_code: invalid_config_type_change`, because a kind change would re-home a session's compute and re-route its work queue between the executor and a BYOC worker mid-flight. The entry was opened as a conservative safety divergence awaiting a recording; the reference refuses the same change in both directions with the same type and code ("Cannot change environment type from Cloud to BYOC" and "… from BYOC to Cloud"), so the rule is the reference's. Since #540 the message is too, byte for byte: the kinds are named as the reference's sentence names them, "Cloud" and "BYOC", not as the wire spells them. Config updates within a kind are unaffected. *Evidence: the 2026-09-12 recording, batch1.json `rec91.kind.cloud-to-self-hosted` and `rec91.kind.self-hosted-to-cloud`; internal/api/environments.go (updateEnvironment's kind check, environmentTypeName); internal/api/environments_test.go TestEnvironmentKindIsImmutable; docs/history/2026-07.md § "work API — environment-key auth + `/work/poll` (slice 8, first part)" (the queue-split rationale)* *Landed for #61; landed for #540.*
- **POST /v1/deployments/{id}/run on an archived deployment is a 400 — no longer an inference (confirmed 2026-09-02) (plan 37 slice 3)** — The docs call archive terminal, but publish no status code for a manual run against one, and the spec attaches no operation-specific errors to any deployment endpoint — every path carries the same boilerplate error block. The entry was opened as an inference; the reference was since recorded answering the run 400 `invalid_request_error`, "Cannot modify archived deployment" (2026-09-02 `deployment.run.on-archived`), and this platform answers exactly that, in those words since #540, matching how it already refuses to act on an archived resource; the refusal precedes the run-row insert, so no run is recorded. Running while merely paused is explicitly allowed and we allow it, without unpausing. *Evidence: the spec's deployment run operation, no archived-specific response arm, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Run, its every error status answering the one shared error body, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaErrorResponse; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — "Archive, unlike pause, is terminal"; "Manual runs through the run endpoint are still allowed while paused"); internal/api/deploymentruns.go runDeployment, internal/api/deploymentruns_test.go TestDeploymentRunArchivedRefusesAndPausedAllows.* *Landed for #540.*
- **agent.thinking — content-free on the wire, replayed from internal storage (plan 60, landed for #67)** — The event keeps the reference's shape, `{id, processed_at, type}` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentThinkingEvent), and nothing that serves events reads the block behind it. The brain keeps a response's leading signed `thinking` and `redacted_thinking` blocks in `thinking_blocks` only when its turn also commits text or a tool call — a turn that settles on thinking alone keeps none — and sends them back on later requests only to the model id that produced them and only under the prefix they were produced under. That guard is this platform's policy, stricter than the API's: Anthropic's model binding drops without error a block the requested model cannot read, but a signature from another vendor's model is undecryptable, always a 400; and its prefix check refuses a block under a changed prefix — by default on accounts created on or after 2026-08-31 — unless the request sets `thinking.block_binding.prefix_mismatch_behavior` to `"drop_block"`. Sending a block only under its own prefix makes a changed system prompt, tool set, model or route drop earlier reasoning without a 400 under either setting. DeepSeek refuses a tool loop that lost its thinking, so a block whose answer carried the model gateway's `X-MAP-Thinking-Prefix: unchecked` is stored under its route alone and goes back under any prefix, still only to its model over its route; a route straight to DeepSeek keeps the gap (#883, plan 61). How the reference replays thinking server-side is not observable on its wire. The neighbouring mismatch is the openai adapter's, which still drops thinking (its entry below). *Evidence: docs/plan/60_thinking-replay.md; docs/plan/61_thinking-replay-via-gateway.md; docs/HISTORY.md § "Thinking persistence and replay (plan 60, #67) — archived 2026-10-04, delivered in one PR" and § "Thinking replay through the model gateway (plan 61, #883) — archived 2026-10-09, delivered in one PR"*

## Deliberate divergences (CONFIRMED)

Mismatches with the managed-agents wire taken by choice, each argued. A **converged** entry —
one that opens with its divergence already closed — stays here rather than moving to the notes
above: those notes are for behavior that never was a mismatch, and what once was one is worth
the record. Such an entry says what survives the convergence, as `write / edit built-in tools —
atomicity` does; where nothing survives, the closure is itself the record. An entry here may
also carry a live inference without becoming one — its pointer names the recording that would
settle it, as the `block_ms` entry's does — because the section follows the entry's divergence,
not the loose ends around it.

- **All endpoints — anthropic-version / anthropic-beta headers and ?beta=true** — anthropic-version and anthropic-beta request headers are accepted and ignored; ?beta=true is accepted where the reference honors it. No version negotiation is performed — and with Files and Skills promoted to GA (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.65.0 — client.go Client.Files and Client.Skills) that is a shape difference, not only a routing one — a narrower one than this entry claimed until #651. **Both** file shapes carry `expires_at` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — file.go FileMetadata.ExpiresAt; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata.ExpiresAt); only `scope` separates them, and `BetaFileMetadata` is the shape that has it. This platform renders that full field set on the bare path and the `?beta=true` path alike, whatever headers the request carries (plan 36 slice 0), so a session-scoped file always comes back with `scope`. The reference gates that key on the `managed-agents-2026-04-01` beta header, not on the path: every recorded file read went to `?beta=true`, and session-scoped copies came back carrying `scope` under that header and without the key on the two reads sent without it — the rule the docs state for the list ("Filtering by `scope_id` requires the `managed-agents-2026-04-01` beta header"). Accept-and-ignore stands, so a client reading a scoped file without that header gets `scope` here and not there; what the bare path does is unrecorded, since no recorded file read went to it. The bytes also settle that the key follows the file's scope, which is why `scope` carries `omitempty` here and `expires_at` does not. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 396/408 `file.get.session-snapshot-id` (scoped, carrying `scope`; sent through the console proxy, which that session's FINDINGS.md says added `?beta=true` and the managed-agents header to every call, though its capture keeps neither) and 2026-09-12-console-141/ui-network.json idx 243–300 (`?scope_id=` lists under the header, every element scoped and carrying `scope`), against ui-network idx 236 and 269 (session-scoped copies read by id on `?beta=true` without the header, in a capture that records it wherever it was sent — no `scope` key); all 24 recorded file objects spell out `expires_at`, and the 8 of unscoped files omit `scope` outright — a recorder that dropped nulls would have taken `expires_at` too, so the absence is the reference's.* The memory-store surface is a second, narrower case of the same rule rather than an exception to it: the reference documents `agent-memory-2026-07-22` as the beta value those endpoints take *instead of* `managed-agents-2026-04-01`, and that sending both returns a 400. This platform returns no such 400 — it ignores the header whatever it carries, on `/v1/memory_stores` exactly as everywhere else (plan 36 slice 1) — so a client sending both values is served rather than refused, and one sending neither is served too. Dreams are a third case of the same shape (plan 41): the dreams guide says the `managed-agents-2026-04-01` header alone "doesn't grant access to dreams", and both the SDK and the `ant` CLI — which drives the same SDK methods — prepend `anthropic-beta: dreaming-2026-04-21` to all five dream calls; this platform serves `/v1/dreams` with that header, without it, and with any other value it carries, because there is no grant here to withhold. **The Skills surface is now recorded on both counts, and it splits them (plan 39).** `?beta=true` selects no shape there: five read pairs are byte-identical across the bare and `?beta=true` paths — the list, the skill object, a version, `latest`, and the `/content` refusal — so ignoring the parameter is exactly what the reference does with it, and the write pairs behave identically too. The dated `anthropic-beta: skills-2025-10-02` header is the opposite: a full pre-migration lane, fields **and** behavior. The skill object returns `display_title`, a bare-string `source` and a numeric `latest_version`; the version object returns `version` and `directory` under an id prefixed `skill_version_`; the `{version}` slot takes the numeric form alone, `Invalid version format: 'skver_…'. Version must be a numeric timestamp (e.g., '1759178010641129').`; and `DELETE /v1/skills/{id}` refuses with `Cannot delete skill with existing versions. Delete all versions first.` where the same verb without the header cascades. This platform serves the GA shape to every caller and ignores that header, so a client still sending `skills-2025-10-02` is served GA here and the beta lane there. That is a divergence from the reference and **not** from this rule — the rule is what produces it, and plan 39 decision 11 argues the dual shape down: selecting a response shape from a request header is the one thing accept-and-ignore forbids, it doubles the wire surface permanently, and the single consumer it would spare is one workshop example a two-line edit also fixes. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [0/1, 16/17, 30/31, 32/33, 43/44] (the byte-identical `?beta=true` pairs), [18, 19] (the dated skill object), [142] (the dated version object and its `skill_version_` id), [36, 37] (the dated `{version}` slot), [79] vs [87] (cascade vs refusal); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decisions 1 and 11; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — file.go FileMetadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata, where the split was real; the same structs, where only `scope` separates them, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — file.go FileMetadata and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betafile.go BetaFileMetadata; the header every store method prepends, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystore.go BetaMemoryStoreService.New; platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the two beta values and the 400 for both); the `dreaming-2026-04-21` header prepended to all five dream calls, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadream.go BetaDreamService.New and BetaDreamService.Get and BetaDreamService.List and BetaDreamService.Archive and BetaDreamService.Cancel, and the CLI driving those same SDK methods, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betadream.go handleBetaDreamsCreate and handleBetaDreamsRetrieve and handleBetaDreamsList and handleBetaDreamsArchive and handleBetaDreamsCancel; platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide ("doesn't grant access to dreams"); internal/api/files.go fileJSON, internal/api/skills.go (skillJSON, one shape on every lane); CLAUDE.md 'Wire-compatibility rules'; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (api/server.go: '?beta=true and anthropic-* headers accepted and ignored')*
- **All endpoints — no `anthropic-workspace-id` response header, and the request header of the same name is accepted and ignored (plan 36 slice 0)** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — apierror.go Error.WorkspaceID and Error.Error, the SDK reads an `anthropic-workspace-id` response header on the error paths, unary and SSE alike, and appends it to the error string as `(Workspace-ID: …)`. This platform emits `request-id` on every response, success and error, and no workspace header — v1 is single-tenant and the workspace scoping key is reserved, not populated, so there is no id to name. The cost is one missing parenthetical in an SDK error message; the field decodes as empty and nothing branches on it. **The request header is the same posture seen from the other side.** The reference reads it to choose the workspace a request acts in — "Required with a multi-workspace API key. Optional for other API keys." — refusing a malformed value with 400 `invalid_request_error` ("anthropic-workspace-id header must be a valid workspace ID.") and an unknown or inaccessible workspace with 404 `not_found_error` ("Workspace `<id>` not found."), and the `ant` CLI sends it on the management lane beside `--api-key` and on the worker lane beside a Bearer environment key. **A 2026-09-05 multi-workspace recording puts all four arms on the wire** and adds three things the pages do not say: a workspace-scoped key naming a *foreign* workspace is refused 404 rather than served (the reading that made this arm inferable at all), the literal `default` is refused 400 like any other invalid value, and the 404's message echoes a **decoded UUID** rather than the id it was sent — it prints one even for a `wrkspc_` id belonging to no workspace, so it is decoding the input, not looking it up. When plan 42 slice 1 honors this header it will echo the id it was given instead, which keeps existence equally hidden without publishing a value the caller did not send; that half becomes its own entry when it lands. With one workspace there is nothing to select, so this platform reads the header nowhere — the string occurs in no Go file — and a malformed or foreign value is served rather than refused. Both halves end together: plan 42 slice 1 emits this header and `anthropic-organization-id` wherever a scope resolves, and honors the request header under one rule, that it may only narrow to a workspace the credential already covers. *Evidence: the field and the message suffix, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — apierror.go Error.WorkspaceID and Error.Error, and the two read points, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — requestconfig.go RequestConfig.Execute and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — ssestream.go richErrorDecoder.newAPIError; platform.claude.com api/overview (the request-header table) and the authentication doc (the two rejection bodies), fetched 2026-09-03; the header sent with `--api-key` and with a Bearer environment key, checked against anthropic-cli v1.26.1 — workspace_test.go TestWorkspaceIDHeader and TestWorkspaceIDHeaderOnWorker; internal/api/server.go (withRequestID, the platform's only stamped response header), internal/api/auth_test.go (both the success and error assertions); a grep for the header name over this repository's Go files returns nothing; the 2026-09-05 multi-workspace recording (`2026-09-05/batch9.curl.txt` probes `item4.C.*`, and `batch8.json` for the response-header schedule), which also fixes `anthropic-organization-id`'s live shape as a bare UUID and shows the reference deciding both headers on two axes rather than one schedule — whether a route stamps at all comes first (`/v1/environments`, the work-poll lane and the token routes stamp neither, even at 200), and only within a stamping route does how far the request got decide which appear.* *Tracked: #56 (both halves — plan 42 slice 1 emits the response headers and honors the request header).*
- **Model backend (brain -> provider) — pluggable, config-driven providers** — The model backend is constructed purely from config (protocol anthropic|openai, model, base_url, api_key, optional headers) and routed via a model_providers registry. base_url is required for both adapters — there is no silent api.anthropic.com fallback — and an OpenAI-compatible gateway is a first-class backend. The reference has a fixed Anthropic backend; this whole seam is a deliberate self-hosting divergence. *Evidence: CLAUDE.md principle #4; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/provider` — config-driven model access (slice 4)" (provider/provider.go, provider/anthropic: 'base_url is required (no silent api.anthropic.com fallback)'); docs/history/2026-07.md § "OpenAI-compatible adapter (`internal/provider/openai`)"*
- **Model turn timeout (brain -> provider) — bounded by the endpoint's silence, per route** — A turn is abandoned once the endpoint has sent nothing at all for that route's `stall_timeout` (default 10 minutes; any byte received, keepalives included, buys the budget back), and surfaces through the session.error(model_request_failed_error) shape a failed model request already uses — no new wire surface — settling exactly as one does: idle with retries_exhausted, or a chained turn with retry_status retrying when input arrived mid-turn (see the session.error retry-semantics inference below). The reference's own timeout policy is unobservable: the SDK types carry none and no recording shows one. This is therefore a platform-side operational bound rather than a claim about the reference — the hosted reference bounds its own fixed backend however it likes, while a self-hosted deployment points at arbitrary endpoints and must not let one wedged proxy hold a brain replica out of service (#121). *Evidence: docs/plan/17_model-endpoint-stall-bound.md; internal/provider/stall.go (StallGuard); docs/HISTORY.md § "Model endpoint stall bound (plan 17)"; see also the session.error retry-semantics inference below (#78)*
- **Observability — OTel alongside span.* events; traceparent across processes** — OTel spans and Anthropic span.* wire events are emitted from the same instrumentation point; W3C traceparent/tracestate propagates through every cross-process call including through work items to BYOC workers. Additive platform surface (deliberate divergence goal: OTel built in). *Evidence: CLAUDE.md principle #3; docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (events/span.go), docs/history/2026-07.md § "BYOC worker — `traceparent` propagation across the process boundary (slice 8, PR C2b-2)" (PR C2b-2, one OTel trace across the process boundary)*
- **Session resource field stats** — Rendered as its zeroed wire shape only — no backing computation exists. The wire shape is satisfied; the functionality is deferred.
