(outcome_evaluations left this entry with plan 21 slice 2, and deployment_id with plan 37 slice 3 — both are now real, stored state.) *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations" (wire-drift note, resolved by slice 2); reference shape checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.Stats and BetaManagedAgentsSessionStats*
- **Environment scope field** — scope is accepted and rendered only as the constant "organization"; "account" is rejected with a clear error (single-tenant v1). *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/store` — Postgres schema + migrations", docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" ('scope rendered as the constant "organization"'), docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (deliberate v1 rejections)*
- **Environment `description` — a string that is never null (plan 36 slice 0)** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, the SDK spelled out semantics the earlier pins left unstated: the response field is "null when unset", and on update "Omit to preserve; null clears to null; an empty string is stored as an empty string". This platform's column is a non-null `text` and the field renders as a plain string, so an unset description is `""` rather than `null` and an update sending `null` clears to `""` rather than to `null`; omit-to-preserve and empty-string-stored-verbatim both match. The reference's own Go type is a bare `string` too, so the SDK decodes either shape and no client breaks — the divergence is what a byte-comparing recording would see. *Evidence: the response doc and the update doc, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, both sentences absent from the text checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaEnvironment.Description and BetaEnvironmentUpdateParams.Description, which read only "User-provided description for the environment" / "Updated description of the environment"; internal/api/environments.go createEnvironment and updateEnvironment (the two `if null { description = "" }` branches), renderEnvironment (the plain-string field)*
- **POST /v1/sessions (create) — multiagent config, resources** — Deliberate v1 rejections with clear errors. **The multiagent rejection is lifted by plan 35 slice 1:** an agent's `multiagent` roster is accepted and resolved at create/update (pinned `{id, type:"agent", version}` references, `self` to the coordinator's own id and the version the write produces, the documented constraints each a 400 — `internal/api/roster.go`), and a session snapshots it as full member definitions in `agent.multiagent`; slices 3 and 4 then made the roster load-bearing — its members spawn as real session threads the six delegation tools drive — and an advisor entry is rejected (#431, settled below). **The `resources[]` rejection is carved down three times — plan 08 slice 2 (files), plan 25 slice 1 (repos), plan 36 slice 3 (memory stores):** `type:"file"` and `type:"github_repository"` resources are accepted (materialized as `sesrsc_` mounts; a repo's write-only token seals into `session_resource_credentials`), and `type:"memory_store"` is accepted on a `cloud` session as the id-less element the reference documents, snapshotted from the store at attach time (the plan-36 entries below carry its readings) — and accepted on a `self_hosted` session the same way since plan 36 slice 6, where the BYOC worker mounts it from the per-item sessions token (its own entry below). **The `vault_ids` rejection is lifted by plan 12 slice 3:** a session now attaches vaults at create time (each id must name an existing, unarchived vault; the list round-trips and updates still reject it — see the session-attachment inference below). *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" ('Deliberate v1 rejections (documented divergences, clear errors)'); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseResourceObject / parseMemoryResource / snapshotMemoryStore; internal/api/sessions.go parseVaultIDs/validateAttachedVaults* *Tracked: #55 (delivered; files with plan 08, repos with plan 25), #50 (delivered; session vault attachment with plan 12 slice 3), #52 (delivered; memory stores with plan 36, whose slice 6 also made `self_hosted` sessions accept them).*
- **GET /v1/sessions list filter deployment_id — now a real filter (plan 37 slice 3)** — The filter no longer short-circuits to an empty page: sessions carry an optional, nullable `deployment_id` — written only by a deployment fire, emitted as an explicit null when unset, which the reference's optional key admits — and the filter is a keyset predicate over `sessions_deployment_idx`. A shape-valid unknown id is a 200 with an empty page, the rule the reference publishes for the runs list and we apply to both; a shape-invalid id is a 400, which the reference publishes for neither. The `memory_store_id` filter that shared this entry until plan 36 slice 3 has been real since then: a containment match on the session's `resources` array (the file-mount lookup's precedent) that answers every session whose element names the store — a deleted store's included, the element being a snapshot — and a 400 for a value not shaped like a `memstore_` id. *Evidence: the SDK's sessions-list `deployment_id` parameter ("Filter sessions created by this deployment ID"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaSessionListParams.DeploymentID, and its runs-list rule ("Filtering by a non-existent deployment_id returns 200 with empty data"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betadeploymentrun.go BetaDeploymentRunListParams.DeploymentID; `BetaManagedAgentsSession`'s 17 properties, 16 required, `deployment_id` the exception, string-or-null, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsSession.properties.deployment_id; `DeploymentID` (`api:"nullable"`) and the memory_store_id param, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsSession.DeploymentID and BetaSessionListParams.MemoryStoreID; internal/api/sessions.go listSessions, internal/api/deploymentruns_test.go TestSessionsListFiltersByDeployment, internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionListFiltersByMemoryStore*
- **Anthropic prebuilt skills — operator-imported, date-versioned** — The reference hosts its prebuilt skill catalog itself (short-name ids `docx`/`pdf`/`pptx`/`xlsx`, date-based versions); a self-hosted platform has no Anthropic-run catalog to reach, so the controlplane's run-once `-import-anthropic-skills` mode provisions them instead: the operator points it at a local checkout of github.com/anthropics/skills, each directory is validated exactly like an upload, and rows land as `source='anthropic'` with the SKILL.md name as the id and the checkout's last commit date (`YYYYMMDD`, `-import-version` overrides) as the version — this platform mints those versions; the reference's own version values for the same skills will differ. `display_name` is likewise set to the short-name id (`xlsx`, not a marketing name) — the reference's actual `display_name` for these skills is unrecorded and may be human-readable (the 2026-09-04 recording listed the four prebuilt skills but exported only the key set, not their values). Anthropic skills are not API-manageable here (`POST …/versions` on one is a 400, and so is `DELETE` of the skill or a version — an accidental delete must not empty a catalog only the importer can refill), matching the reference's server-managed stance. The version create's 400 carries the reference's words since #540, "Invalid skill_id format: xlsx" (2026-09-04 batch1 `rec81.versions.create.anthropic-skill`) — on the reference a format refusal of an id that is not a `skill_` one, here the refusal of an imported short name; a short name the catalog lacks is still this platform's 404, where the reference's format check would answer 400. The two delete refusals were never recorded and keep ours. *Evidence: docs/plan/06_skills.md design decision 4 + slice 3; cmd/controlplane (runImport), internal/api/skillsimport.go; managed-agents/skills docs (anthropic skill ids and date versions)* *Tracked: #78 (the reference's own display_name for these skills stays unrecorded); landed for #54.*

- **Skills materialization — sentinel idempotence instead of the reference's re-extract-every-item** — The reference's agent toolset re-downloads and re-extracts every skill each work item, clearing each destination and removing the per-skill directories it downloaded at the end, its workdir being host-shared — and the platform-managed sandbox extracts the same way but mounts the archives rather than fetching them, a split the recording paragraph below draws out; our per-session sandboxes instead keep a `.materialized` marker — the resolved `{skill_id: version}` set (never the landing directory) plus an integrity generation (`skills.SentinelVersion`, now 2 = every recorded archive was digest-verified where a digest existed) — and skip rewriting only when the marker is of the current generation, is an exact bijection with the freshly resolved set, and every skill's directory still holds its SKILL.md; a marker of a weaker generation never satisfies a stronger pass (one re-materialization per live sandbox at upgrade). The agent-writable workdir means the marker is never trusted for anything load-bearing, and three tampering residuals are accepted rather than closed by abandoning the skip as the reference's every-item re-extract does, which clobbers such edits every item: an in-place content edit; a partial deletion leaving the root SKILL.md the probe checks as a proxy for the tree; and — the landing directory being the skill *name*, shared across versions — a forged marker version over an older version's files, suppressing that one in-session upgrade. All three are one irreducible class rather than three unfixed bugs — an agent that controls the sandbox filesystem can always present chosen bytes under `skills/<name>/` — each persists only until the resolved set changes, and none reaches past the agent's own single-tenant session. We also rewrite in place without first clearing the directory (the sandbox seam has no delete primitive), so a file dropped between versions can linger as an orphan beside the new version's complete set — accepted, our workdir never being shared. The read verifies the archive against the sha256 recorded at upload — a further divergence from a reference that records and checks none — refusing a bit-rotted, truncated or substituted object; the mismatch is skipped per-skill under its own `corrupt` outcome (beyond plan 06's ok/not_found/failed) and a version predating the digest column is read unverified. Extraction takes zip only (the reference's tar fallbacks are dead code here) and, like the reference (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.0 — skillarchive.go zipEntryIsPlain and tarEntryIsPlain), materializes only regular files and directories — a Unix-host symlink, FIFO or device entry is skipped, never written out as a file holding its link target (TestExtractSkipsNonPlainEntries); three further deltas are defensive, unreachable for archives we validated: duplicate references dedupe first-wins (the reference, re-extracting each, is last-wins); an unusable version name falls back to the skill id (the reference tries `filepath.Base` first); traversal/backslash/relative segments are refused outright, where the reference absorbs them at the extraction root. Shared with the reference, not a divergence: both name the directory after the version's name, so two skills whose names collide overwrite each other under `{workdir}/skills/{name}/`. *Evidence: Skills.Versions.Download per item, RemoveAll+extract, Cleanup and filepath.Base, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.SetupSkillsFromSession and AgentToolContext.downloadSkill and AgentToolContext.Cleanup, and tar, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skillarchive.go extractTar; internal/executor/skills.go, internal/worker/skills.go; the derivations cut from here are comments in internal/skills/extract.go (SentinelVersion, ParseSentinel, SentinelMatches, Resolved, MaxArchiveBytes), pinned by TestSentinel, TestSentinelMatches, TestReadArchiveVerifiesDigest, TestExtractRefusals, TestTargetDir, TestMaterializePreVerificationSentinelIsNotTrusted* **A 2026-09-03 recording splits "the reference" here in two, and the first reading of it written in this entry was wrong.** Client-side, the SDK's agent toolset does re-fetch: it calls `Skills.Versions.Download` per skill per work item into `{workdir}/skills/{dirname}`, which is the sentence this entry opens with and which stands. The platform-managed sandbox does it differently — the zips arrive on a read-only rclone FUSE mount at `/mnt/skills`, the mount table being in the file-mount materialization entry below, which the same probe settled and which should stay the one place it is written down. What the recording does **not** show is the extraction going away: the Level-1 skill-injection entry, from this same wave, records `/workspace/skills/` holding the *extracted* `pdf/` and `xlsx/` trees, root-owned and not a symlink. So on neither path does the reference skip the per-item extraction, and the sentinel is not solving a problem the reference does not have — it answers whether a re-provisioned sandbox must redo that extraction, which is a live question on both. Nothing about the decision changes — a mount seam is a much larger commitment than an extraction seam — but the argument runs the opposite way from the one first written here: where the archives are a local mount, re-extracting is a copy rather than a download, so the managed side affords its clobber-every-item policy more cheaply than we would, and our skip rests on the sandbox being per-session rather than on saving a fetch. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probe `session.events.list.skill-paths`, analysed in 2026-09-03/FINDINGS2.md §H.* *Tracked: #54 (delivered).*
- **Agent skills[] — accepted and normalized while distribution and execution were deferred** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (#54).** The normalization itself never was one: entries are validated and re-normalized to `{type, skill_id, version}` with an omitted version becoming the literal "latest" — which **matches** the reference: an omitted version "Defaults to `latest` when omitted", agent create accepts the literal `version:"latest"`, the alias persists unresolved into the agent and the session's agent snapshot, and it is resolved only at skill-materialization time, in the reference's own worker — the alias reaches that worker intact, and who turns it into an id once there is what changed under us. As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion, the worker did it itself, listing a skill's versions and picking the newest on the strength of a doc comment claiming the versions endpoints reject an alias, and downloading by the token that produced. That function was later removed: the SDK now retrieves the version the alias addresses and downloads by the concrete id that call returned (`skillVersion` "may be the alias `latest`, which only the retrieve endpoint resolves"), which leaves the resolving to the server. So where it happens is unchanged and who performs it is not. **This platform already answers the newer flow**: a retrieve addressed by `latest` echoes the version row's own id — `skver_` for a row minted since GA, the legacy `skillver_` for one an upgrade retained — and the download accepts either, on the bare and `?beta=true` paths alike. **Our BYOC worker now follows that flow for the numeric too (#842)**: it downloads by the retrieve's id for the alias and for a pre-GA numeric pin alike, and an id pin addresses the download itself (plan 39 decision 5). Until #842 it carried the numeric through verbatim, which this platform's `/content` still takes for a worker released before then — the one compatibility left on the server side, in *`…/versions/{version}` — the numeric version and the legacy `skillver_` id* below. **This entry replaces an earlier CONFIRMED one claiming the reference resolves the version at create** — that claim was wrong; the entry is corrected rather than deleted so the reversal stays auditable. The divergence that correction left standing was the deferral behind the field: no /v1/skills API, no object storage, no sandbox materialization and no prompt injection existed here, where the reference had all four. The deferral is now closed: the /v1/skills registry and its object storage exist (plan slice 2), both halves materialize skills into sandboxes with use-time resolution (slice 4), and the brain injects Level-1 metadata into the system prompt at request-assembly time (slice 5 — see the injection-template entry in the INFERRED section below). **The literal the reference echoes after an omitted version is now recorded, and it is ours**: an agent created with `{type:"custom", skill_id}` and no `version` stores and returns `"version": "latest"`, as does one that sent the literal itself, and a `skver_` id is stored and echoed unchanged — the same normalization this platform has always performed, and it survives an archive. *Evidence: platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/skills ("Attach skills to an agent" field table: version "Defaults to `latest` when omitted"; create examples passing `version:"latest"`); the 2026-09-04 recording [106] (omitted → `"latest"`), [100] (the literal), [101, 107] (a `skver_` id, on create and on the later GET), [117-119] (the archive responses, unchanged); the retrieve, and the comment and the download by the id it returned, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.downloadSkill, against resolveSkillVersion, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion and absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go resolveSkillVersion; internal/api/skills.go getSkillVersion and resolveSkillVersion, internal/api/skills_test.go TestSkillLatestAliasRoundTripsToDownload, internal/worker/skills.go resolveSkillVersion (the numeric pin, converged for #842); docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/api` + `cmd/controlplane` — wire-compatible control-plane CRUD" (wire.go skill re-normalization)* *Landed for #54 and #842.*
- **POST /v1/skills — `display_name`'s derivation, its cap and its uniqueness** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (plan 39 decision 7).** The entry this replaces registered three inferences about a field it called `display_title`: that an omitted form value defaults to the SKILL.md frontmatter `name` verbatim, that uniqueness among a workspace's custom skills is enforced case-sensitively by a partial unique index exempting `source='anthropic'`, and that a duplicate is a 400. The 2026-09-04 recording answers all three, and renames the field on the way. It is `display_name`; omitted, it does derive from the frontmatter `name`; it is capped at 255 characters — 255 accepted, 256 refused with `display_name must be at most 255 characters long` — where this platform capped at 4096; and it is **not** unique, two skills having been accepted carrying the identical value. The uniqueness half was therefore not a conservative guess but a break, since refusing a create the reference accepts is harder for a client to work around than accepting one it refuses. Migration 0033 drops `skills_custom_display_title_uq`; the column keeps its 0007 spelling, only the wire field having been renamed. Nothing survives the convergence, and that closure is the record. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [2] (derived from frontmatter), [3, 4] (two skills, one `display_name`), [6, 7] (255 accepted, 256 refused with that message); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 7; internal/api/skills.go skillJSON, internal/api/skillsupload.go maxDisplayNameChars, internal/store/migrations/0033_skills_display_name.sql.* *Landed for #566.*
- **`…/versions/{version}` — the numeric version and the legacy `skillver_` id are accepted here and rejected there (plan 39 decision 4)** — GA addresses a version by its id, or by `latest` on the read routes: both answer 200, while a numeric timestamp is `Invalid version id: '1759178010641129'` and a `skillver_`-prefixed id is `Invalid version id: 'skillver_…'`. This platform's slot takes four forms — `skver_…`, `skillver_…`, `latest` on the read routes, and `^[0-9]{1,32}$` — the first and third matching GA, the other two additive and both deliberately so. Agents created before this convergence hold numeric pins, and a stored pin that silently stops resolving is a wrong answer rather than a refusal; `skillver_` is the id this platform minted for rows it does not rewrite (the prefix entry below). Where the reference refuses the alias, its own sentence is reproduced verbatim: `'latest' is not accepted when deleting a skill version. Address a specific version id: read the skill's latest_version_id, or GET /v1/skills/{skill_id}/versions/latest, and use that id.` on DELETE, and its download-worded twin on `/content`. **`/content` refuses the numeric on the reference too**, as `Invalid version id: '1759178010641129'` on both the console-cookie and the environment-key lane (2026-09-05 batch1 #46 `rec82.content.cookie.numeric-id`, #47 `rec82.content.envkey.numeric-id`). This entry said until #842 that GA `/content` accepts it, on the strength of the 2026-09-04 recording's [150], which that session's README marks as a browser-cache replay of [140] — itself a request under the dated `skills-2025-10-02` header, not GA. This platform keeps accepting the numeric on `/content` all the same, and that half is deliberate for a reason of its own: its BYOC worker downloaded a legacy numeric pin by the number until #842, so refusing it there would leave every self-hosted worker deployed before then materializing such an agent without the skill. **The worker side is converged:** it now downloads by the id the version retrieve answers with, as the SDK's worker does (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — skills.go AgentToolContext.downloadSkill; internal/worker/skills.go resolveSkillVersion), and the console downloads by a version's `id`, so nothing released since depends on the numeric there, and the divergence can retire once the older workers have aged out. The operator importer keeps a version regex of its own, so widening the slot does not loosen it. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [30, 32] (id and `latest`, 200), [34] (the numeric refused by version metadata), [38, 149] (the `skillver_` id refused), [60] (the delete sentence), [45, 136] (the download twin), [140] (the numeric served by `/content` under the dated header; [150] replays it); 2026-09-05 batch1 [46, 47] (the numeric refused by GA `/content` on both lanes); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 4; internal/api/skills.go checkSkillVersion (skillVersionIDRe, skillVersionNumberRe), internal/api/skillsimport.go, internal/worker/skills.go resolveSkillVersion.* *Landed for #566; corrected for #842.*
- **Skill version ids — `skver_` is minted, `skillver_` is accepted forever, and rows written before the convergence still render it (plan 39 decision 9)** — The reference's GA prefix is `skver_`, and its own pre-migration prefix was `skill_version_`. This platform minted `skillver_`, which matched **neither** lane, so the mismatch was always two-sided. It now mints `skver_` and keeps `skillver_` in `knownPrefixes` as an input alias, in the mould of the `session_` alias — but existing rows are deliberately **not** rewritten, because an agent's `skills[]` pins a version by id and a rewrite would dangle every stored pin. A skill version created before this change therefore still renders a `skillver_` id on the wire, where the reference would never emit one; a GA client round-trips it correctly, the id being opaque to it, and the reference refuses that spelling in its own `{version}` slot. What remains is a value a byte-comparing recording would see, not a client that breaks. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [2, 30] (`skver_` on the GA lane), [142] (`skill_version_` on the dated lane), [38] (the reference refusing `skillver_`); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md decision 9; internal/domain/id.go (PrefixSkillVersion, altSkillVersionPrefix, knownPrefixes), CLAUDE.md's prefix list.* *Landed for #566.*
- **Agent `skills[]` — a dangling custom reference is accepted at create here and refused there; an anthropic one is refused on both, but a reference to a prebuilt skill is checked for its version's form alone (confirmed 2026-09-02, 2026-09-04)** — The reference validates a skill reference when the agent is written: a version that does not resolve is ``Agent has invalid configuration: `skill_id` `…` version `…` not found`` — its answer to a numeric pin, to a `skillver_` pin, to another skill's version id, and, once the skill itself is deleted, even to the alias `latest`. A reference that does resolve is stored and echoed verbatim. This platform accepts every `type:"custom"` one of those at create and at session override, and surfaces a miss late instead, at materialization, as a logged skip counted by `skills.resolve.misses`. The entry this replaces filed the question as an inference — "whether the reference also accepts a dangling reference at create is unrecorded" — argued from the SDK typing `skill_not_found_error` as a *deployment pause* reason, i.e. late-bound. The recording refutes it: the late binding is real on the reference too, but it is not the whole of its behavior. Adopting the create-time refusal is explicitly **out of scope for plan 39**, which converges the wire shape and adds no validation, so the permissiveness is registered rather than fixed. It is the safe direction of the two — every agent the reference accepts is accepted here, so no client is refused a write it could make there — and what a client loses is only the early error: a dangling pin here becomes a session that runs without the skill rather than a 400 at create. Deleting a skill an agent already references succeeds on both sides and leaves the agent holding a dangling reference, so the *state* is reachable there as well; the reference simply refuses to let a new agent be written into it. **The check covers `type:"anthropic"` references too.** This entry called that unobserved until #842, from the one anthropic reference the 2026-09-04 session probed: it named a *custom* skill's id and was accepted and echoed with `"version": "latest"` — which shows only that the lookup goes by id whatever the source. The 2026-09-02 session had already recorded the rest: `xlsx` with no version is accepted and echoed as `latest` [418], `xlsx` at version `1` is ``Agent has invalid configuration: `skill_id` `xlsx` version `1` not found`` [419], and `no-such-skill` with no version is `` … `skill_id` `no-such-skill` not found`` [421], the version-less sentence also answering a dangling custom id [420] — so the sentence parts on whether a version was named, an explicit `latest` taking the version's [110]. **Since #842 this platform runs that check on anthropic references at agent create** (checkAnthropicSkillRefs), in those words: the skill looked up by id whatever its source, its version resolved as materialization resolves it. **A reference to one of the reference's own prebuilt skills is checked for its version's form alone (deliberate):** the reference's prebuilt catalog is always there — its skills list was recorded serving `xlsx`, `pptx`, `pdf` and `docx` (2026-09-02 batch2 #415) — while this platform's is the operator's import, which may hold some of them or none, under versions the import mints from its checkout's date. So this platform cannot know which versions of those skills the reference holds, and refusing a pin it cannot judge would refuse what the reference accepts — a version it minted, `20251013` or its own `skver_` id, or any pin of a skill never imported, which automation that ran without the skill before #842 depends on. Such a reference is accepted whenever its version has a pin's form — `latest`, a number, a version id in either side's alphabet — with one Warn per create naming each one this catalog does not resolve, and left to resolve or not at materialization; only a version of no form is refused, in the version's sentence. **That accepts #419's own `xlsx` at version `1`, which the reference refuses** — the divergence this rule buys, a dangling pin rather than a refused write the reference would make. Every other anthropic reference is checked against this catalog as recorded: an unknown id, `no-such-skill` among them, is refused, an empty catalog included, and so is a version that does not resolve of a skill this catalog holds — a custom skill an anthropic entry names included. The recorded set is its own (skills.IsReferencePrebuilt), not the importer's default `-import-skills` list, so widening the import cannot widen what is left unchecked. **The check is an unlocked, best-effort read, by choice:** a skill or version deleted between it and the create's commit leaves the agent holding a dangling reference, which materialization skips as it skips one a later delete leaves. A `FOR SHARE` on the skill rows closed that window only when the check took the lock first; when a concurrent delete or upload held it, the statement waited and then rechecked the locked row against its older snapshot — letting a deleted version through, and refusing a valid `latest` an upload had just moved — and every create waited on the archive write an upload or an import holds the skill row across. And the check runs on anthropic references alone: custom ones keep the permissiveness above, unchanged by this issue, and neither an agent update nor a session's agent override runs the check, the recordings reaching only create. *Evidence: the 2026-09-04 recording [102, 103, 105] (numeric, `skillver_`, another skill's version id — each that message), [110] (`latest` after the skill is deleted), [100, 101, 106, 107] (the references that resolve), [104] (an anthropic-type reference naming a custom skill, accepted), [108, 109] (the delete that leaves an agent dangling); 2026-09-02 batch2 [418, 419, 421] (anthropic references, accepted and refused), [420] (a dangling custom id, no version); docs/plan/39_skills-ga-shape.md § "Out of scope"; internal/api/wire.go parseSkills, internal/api/skills.go checkAnthropicSkillRefs, internal/skills/pin.go ClassifyPin (the stored-pin reading the check, the brain and the executor share) and prebuilt.go IsReferencePrebuilt, internal/brain/skills.go, internal/executor/skills.go, internal/worker/skills.go (the late-bound miss); internal/api/skills_test.go TestAgentCreateRefusesAnAnthropicSkillThatNamesNothing, skillrefs_internal_test.go TestTheAnthropicSkillCheckTakesNoLock, TestTheAnthropicSkillCheckBesideAVersionUpload and TestTheAnthropicSkillCheckRefusesAVersionOfNoForm; 2026-09-02 batch2 [415] (the reference's prebuilt catalog).* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference's create-time check also runs on an agent update and on a session's agent overrides — which the recordings never probed — and with them whether this platform should adopt the refusal for custom references too); landed for #566 and #842.*
- **GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events/stream — history semantics** — The stream is a live tail from connect time: no history replay and no Last-Event-ID support. Reconnecting clients seed via the list endpoint. Safe because the reference client parses neither. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/events` + events API — append-only log, send/list, SSE stream (slice 3)" (slice-3 wire/scope decisions); anthropic-cli client behavior cited there*
- **POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — inbound `session_thread_id` (plan 35 slices 2–3; aligned for #841)** — On `user.tool_confirmation`, `user.tool_result` and `user.custom_tool_result` the field is optional, and any string is accepted (anything else is a 400 in this platform's words): the answer is stored on the thread of the call it answers, routed by the tool-use id the docs say to copy from the call, and the claim decides nothing. That is what the reference does with a tool confirmation: one naming another thread of the session, an unknown `sthr_` id, or an `sth_`-prefixed value was each answered 200, its echoed `session_thread_id` rewritten to the call's own thread (2026-09-02 batch2 #334 `sessK2.send.tool_confirmation.wrong-thread`, #335 `.unknown-thread`, #336 `.sth_-prefix`; #276 `.with-session_thread_id` is the matching claim), where this platform refused each with a 400 until #841. The echo here names the call's thread as every answer's does — a child's on the session view, none for the primary's. The two tool results follow the confirmation unrecorded (*The reference's recorded error sentences, answered where no recording reaches*, below). On `user.interrupt` the field must name a live thread of this session — the primary's id means the primary, and interrupts that between them name every live thread (the primary's on a single-agent session) cancel every live item exactly as the unnamed form does. A malformed value is a 400 in the reference's words, `Invalid session_thread_id: …` (2026-09-02 `sessK.send.interrupt.sth_-prefix` and `.unknown-thread`, #540), malformed being the shape *Malformed and absent ids — the one shape agent and thread ids are read by* (below) defines, which reads the reference's own ids as well-formed; a well-formed id naming no thread of this session is a 404 `not_found_error`, `Thread not found: sthr_…`, as recorded for another session's thread (#333 `sessK.send.interrupt.thread-of-other-session`, whose own reference-format id this platform now answers as recorded; #841) and answered alike, unrecorded, for an id no thread carries (the same entry below); an archived or terminated thread is this platform's 400. *Evidence: internal/events/inbound.go (threadClaim), route.go (RouteInbound, ThreadNotFoundError); internal/api/events.go sendSessionEvents; TestInboundThreadClaimValidation; TestSendValidationSweep; TestNormalizeInboundReferenceWording; TestNormalizeInboundDropsAnAnswersThreadClaim* *Tracked: #78 (no recording yet settles a claim on a tool result or custom tool result, an interrupt naming an archived thread, or one naming a well-formed id no thread carries); landed for #841.*
- **POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — who may post a `user.tool_result` (confirmed 2026-09-02, #662)** — **Converged for management credentials; one credential wider than the reference.** The reference admits a `user.tool_result` by the credential that signed the request: an environment key posting one on a `self_hosted` session was answered 200, and a Console session posting one was refused **403 `permission_error`** "events[0]: `user.tool_result` may only be sent with environment credentials (the self-hosted runner's Session-Instance JWT); an API key or Console session cannot post this event type" — on that `self_hosted` session and, with the identical body, on a `cloud` one. This platform gated by environment kind instead — a 400 `invalid_request_error` on a `cloud` session and no credential test at all on a `self_hosted` one, where a management `x-api-key` answering a call the worker was to answer was accepted — and registered neither half. It now refuses a management credential, the `x-api-key` and the human identity lane alike, with that 403 on any session, a `user.tool_result` anywhere in a batch refusing the whole batch with nothing appended; the session's environment key is admitted. What an environment credential meets on a `cloud` session is the INFERRED entry "POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — an environment credential's `user.tool_result` on a `cloud` session". Two differences remain, both ours. (1) **The per-item sessions token (`wtk_`) is admitted**, where the same recording refused a decoded `sessions_token` with the same 403: the reference worker sends its tool results under the item's sessions token whenever the item's secret carries one (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — worker.go EnvironmentWorker.handleItem), and this platform mints one for every item whose session attaches a memory store, so refusing it would fail every answer that worker posts on those sessions. (2) The rule is decided where the batch is normalized, after the session is read, so a management caller's result to an absent session is the 404 and to an archived one the archived 400, and within a batch the first event that fails decides the answer — a malformed event ahead of a `user.tool_result` is that event's 400; every recorded refusal was a one-event batch on a live session, so the reference's order in all three is unobserved. The message is the reference's sentence word for word since #540, its parenthetical naming a Session-Instance JWT this platform has no counterpart of; it used to name this platform's own credentials, the worker's environment key or its sessions token. *Evidence: internal/events/inbound.go normalizeOne (the `user.tool_result` arm, ErrEnvironmentCredentialRequired); internal/api/envauth.go credentialFrom; internal/api/events.go sendSessionEvents; internal/api/events_test.go TestToolResultAdmissionIsByCredential, TestToolResultRefusalOrder, TestToolResultTypeSpellingsUnderAManagementKey, TestToolResultOnACloudSession and TestSessionsTokenPostsAToolResult; internal/api/identitylane_test.go TestIdentityLaneCannotPostAToolResult.* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json, probes `sessW.send.user.tool_result.env-key` (200), `sessW.send.user.tool_result.console-auth`, `sessW.send.user.tool_result.sessions_token` and `sessT.send.user.tool_result.for-platform-call` (each 403, one body).* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference still refuses the sessions token its own worker answers under, and which it checks first against an absent session); landed for #662.*
- **Reference surface that arrived in one SDK release — advisor, budgets, list-cost usage, `session.usage`, `redacted`, `session.budget_reached` — registered, not built (plan 35 slice 0)** — The SDK bump of plan 35 slice 0 found every managed-agents type change in one release (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.62.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsAdvisorParams and BetaManagedAgentsBudgetLimit and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUsageEvent) and none of it in the platform's scope; each is filed and left unbuilt — the advisor and `redacted` are the two whose refusals are settled rather than pending, below — and where a client can reach it the platform rejects rather than silently drops. **Advisor** (#431): the `{type:"advisor", model}` roster entry is reachable since plan 35 slice 1 lifted the `multiagent` rejection (the entry above) and is **400** `entry type must be "agent" or "self"` (`internal/api/roster.go` parseRosterEntry) — the platform's roster admits the two entry kinds it runs. That 400 is the settled answer, not a placeholder (#431): an advisor is a model the *primary thread* may consult mid-turn — which this platform's brain could do — but the reference gates which models may serve as one against a spec a self-hosted deployment cannot consult (the advisor's `model`: "must be permitted as an advisor for this agent's model", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsAdvisorParams.Model), and nothing has asked for a platform-run advisor. So it stays unbuilt, and refusing beats accepting an entry the platform would silently ignore. The three response unions that admit an advisor do render — a thread's `agent` on every session, an agent's and a session's `multiagent` wherever a roster exists — always in their agent arm; it is the advisor *variant* that never appears. **Budgets** (#432): `budget` on session create/update is **400** via the unknown-key rejection — the CLI's `--budget` lands there; `BudgetLimit`/`BetaMonetaryAmount` have no platform type; the `budget_reached` stop reason and the **`session.usage`** event (list, stream and thread-stream unions) are never emitted (a client POSTing `session.usage` gets the 400 every type outside the client set gets, `Failed to parse request: events[0].type: "session.usage" is not a valid value`); session `usage` carries the token buckets only, none of `active_seconds` / `list_cost` / `server_tool_use`; deployment `budget` is now its own CONFIRMED entry below rather than a deferral (a create or update carrying it is a 400), and the `session.budget_reached` webhook is undelivered like every other webhook (#261). **`redacted`** (#430): the inbound half is conformance, not a divergence — a `user.message` carrying `{type:"redacted"}` is **400** `content block type "redacted" is not allowed here`, and the reference rejects one the same way ("a user event that includes one is rejected with a 400 error"); `blocksUserMessage` admits the text, image and document the reference documents for that event, and nothing else. The emit half is the divergence, and like the advisor's it is settled rather than pending: the reference emits `{type:"redacted"}` as a placeholder for content withheld by Anthropic model policy, this platform runs no such policy and nothing has asked for one, and no Messages API response it can receive carries the block — it exists only in the SDK's managed-agents files. The generated types are a trap pointing the other way: the *inbound param* union registers a redacted arm for a request the reference itself 400s, so the inbound vocabulary must never be widened from them. What the reference renders for a session that has no budget was the one shape-question left, and a 2026-09-12 recording answered it with `null`; the INFERRED entry below now waits on the code rather than on the shape. *Evidence: AdvisorParams and its roster arm, BudgetLimit, the create/update budget, SessionUsage's new fields and SessionUsageEvent, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsAdvisorParams and BetaManagedAgentsMultiagentRosterEntryParamsUnion.OfBetaManagedAgentsAdvisors and BetaManagedAgentsBudgetLimit and BetaManagedAgentsBudgetLimitParam and BetaSessionNewParams.Budget and BetaSessionUpdateParams.Budget and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUsage.ActiveSeconds and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUsage.ListCost and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUsage.ServerToolUse and BetaManagedAgentsSessionUsageEvent; RedactedBlock, the inbound param union's redacted arm, budget_reached and the agent.message content union, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsRedactedBlock and BetaManagedAgentsRedactedBlockParam and BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParamsContentUnion.OfRedacted and BetaManagedAgentsSessionBudgetReached and BetaManagedAgentsAgentMessageEvent.Content; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betawebhook.go BetaWebhookSessionBudgetReachedEventData; platform.claude.com managed-agents/reference § "Event types" → Agent events (fetched 2026-09-07 — "Redacted blocks appear only in content the platform emits; a user event that includes one is rejected with a 400 error"), and its User events tab giving `user.message` as "text, image, or document content"; internal/api/sessions.go rejectUnknownKeys, internal/events/inbound.go block vocabularies; docs/HISTORY.md (the SDK bump's wire-schema verification record of 2026-08-19)* *Tracked: #432 (until budgets are built, `budget` is rejected on create/update, no `budget_reached` stop reason or `session.usage` event is emitted, and session usage carries the token buckets only).*
- **provider/openai adapter — lossy conversion gaps** — Documented, non-silent lossy gaps confined to internal/provider/openai: Anthropic thinking and redacted_thinking blocks are dropped (no Chat Completions equivalent); a tool_result's is_error boolean is dropped (the error text in content is still forwarded); image blocks are unmapped and fail loudly; a tool_result's `search_result` blocks (a web_search answer) flatten to text — title and source URL on a header line, then the hit's text content — because an OpenAI tool message is a string. The rendering itself (`provider.SearchResultText`) lives in the parent `internal/provider` package, not this one, so provider/anthropic's opt-in `flatten_search_results` route flag (entry below) can share it rather than duplicate it. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/provider` — config-driven model access (slice 4)", docs/history/2026-07.md § "OpenAI-compatible adapter (`internal/provider/openai`)" ('Documented lossy gaps (dropped by design, not silently)')*
- **provider/anthropic adapter — opt-in `flatten_search_results` route flag for an endpoint that rejects search_result in tool_result content (#565)** — Near-zero-conversion by default (CLAUDE.md principle 4): the adapter passes search_result blocks through unchanged. A route may set `flatten_search_results: true` to rewrite each into a text block via `provider.SearchResultText`, shared with provider/openai's flattening above; everything else — sibling blocks, string-form content, a tool_result's own fields, a block the rendering cannot parse — stays untouched. Only title/source/content survive: a search_result's `citations` (a `{enabled}` config) has no text-block equivalent — a text block's own `citations` is a list of citation objects, a shape mismatch — so it and the block's boundaries are dropped. What that costs is model-visible now that `web_search` emits `citations.enabled: true` (#548): the flattened turn carries the text but not the permission to cite it, so this flag — and every `protocol: openai` route, which flattens unconditionally — can move the answer text on a session a conformant Anthropic route would answer differently. Accepted as the price of a turn the endpoint would otherwise refuse. Default off, since a conformant endpoint accepts search_result verbatim; MiniMax's `https://api.minimaxi.com/anthropic` (docs/HISTORY.md § "Web-tools slices 2+3 acceptance") does not, answering `400 invalid params, invalid tool_result content (2013)` when replaying a web_search tool_result. Rejected at config load on protocol: openai, which already flattens. *Evidence: internal/provider/provider.go (NewRegistry's protocol:anthropic-only check); internal/provider/anthropic/searchresult.go; internal/provider/searchresult.go (SearchResultText); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsSearchResultBlockParam and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — message.go TextBlockParam.Citations; docs/HISTORY.md § "Web-tools slices 2+3 acceptance"; issue #565*
- **provider/openai adapter — stop_reason mapping** — stop_reason is forced to tool_use whenever the stream carried any tool call, regardless of finish_reason (some OpenAI-compatible servers end tool turns with stop/length; honoring that would strand the tool). With no tool calls, finish_reason maps length -> max_tokens, everything else -> end_turn. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/provider` — config-driven model access (slice 4)", docs/history/2026-07.md § "OpenAI-compatible adapter (`internal/provider/openai`)" ('stop_reason is tool_use whenever the stream carried any tool call')*
- **user.interrupt — no span.model_request_end for the turn it ends** — An interrupt that lands while a model request is in flight cancels the turn's work item, so the brain's settlement — which carries the lease as its ownership proof — rolls back in full, and with it the span.model_request_end event that settlement would have committed. The reference emits one: the `ant` CLI's `--event-deltas` help says a model request that ends early "(an error or interrupt) produces no final event — its terminal `span.model_request_end` closes the preview". Ours leaves the span.model_request_start unclosed, the same residue a brain crash leaves (entry below). Closing it from the interrupt's own transaction was evaluated and rejected: it cannot be made airtight (a turn that opens its span *after* the interrupt commits is unreachable from it), and the usage it would report is invented. *Evidence: internal/api/events.go (the interrupt case's CancelSession); internal/brain/brain.go (settlement carries the lease proof); internal/brain/interrupt_test.go (TestInterruptedTurnCommitsNothing pins the log an interrupted turn leaves); checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betasessionevent.go betaSessionsEventsStream.* *Tracked: #68 (delivered).*
- **POST/history `processed_at` for inbound events and delivered messages** — Pending input retains `processed_at: null`; the reference recordings can omit that field instead. No recorded event carries it as a present null — the 2026-09-02 echoes of `user.message`, `user.interrupt` and `user.tool_confirmation` (batch2.json, `sessT.send.interrupt` among them) and pending events in listed history all leave it out — but adopting that needs a coordinated console change, since the console's event schema requires the key (#78, #674). Tool results and approvals now receive a processing timestamp when the thread's ordered processor consumes them, and POST echoes that stored value. The order follow-up records a timestamp in an early POST response for beta while a later GET still omits it, then a new processing timestamp once alpha arrives. We keep one truthful, monotonic processing timestamp, without reproducing the reference's POST/history discrepancy or publication delays. Monotonic here means that within one commit the stamps that commit writes never run backwards through its list order (#793, #539), and two places adjust a stamp to keep it so. A row the commit leaves null is outside that promise: a delivered message is stamped later, when the request that reads it starts, so its stamp can fall after those of rows listed behind it. `Log.AppendInTx` raises a non-null stamp that is earlier than the one listed ahead of it to that one, on every append — a turn's `span.model_request_end`, stamped when it is built and listed after the `agent.*` rows stamped at insert, takes their stamp. And a send's answers, stamped by the settlement that runs after the append, are clamped into their list slot, no earlier than the rows listed ahead of them and no later than the first listed behind. Only non-null values move: a pending (null) row is never touched, by either. An adjusted stamp takes a value another row of the same commit holds, and nothing reads the difference: no reader compares two stamp values, every one keys on `processed_at` being null or not. An input a model request consumes — a `user.message`, a `user.define_outcome`, a `system.message`, an `agent.thread_message_received` — is stamped when that request starts, in the commit of its `span.model_request_start`, at the start's `processed_at` minus 1 µs: the stamp the reference writes on 139 of the 140 distinct inputs the recordings show consumed (#793; the exception, 2026-09-02/batch2.json sessT idx 78, is 58.8 ms earlier, still after the in-flight request's results). The start's `processed_at` is taken on the brain's clock once the session lock is held, so an input that commits while the start waits on the lock is never dated before the start was free to run, and the request's inputs, its start, its reply and its `span.model_request_end` are all stamped on that one clock. `created_at` is the database's clock, and an answer's `processed_at` is that of whichever process took it, so either can still differ from these by the skew between the hosts. No recording carries a `system.message`, so its stamp is ours. A `user.interrupt` is no request's input and is stamped at receipt, whatever it reaches, in its send's commit after the results it synthesizes and before its idle pairs, as the reference processes one (2026-09-02/batch2.json `sessT.events.after-outcome` idx 36 is stamped 106 ms before its idle pair, with no request between); so is the one the dream runner writes when it interrupts the session it owns. An answer the ordered processor held behind an earlier call of its thread, whose own call an interrupt or a child's archive then answers — in practice a confirmation — is stamped in that commit, with the stamp its call's result is written with, since no later walk reaches it. A build before #793 left both to its settle-time stamp, which is gone, so a request's start also repairs its thread's rows below it that only that stamp would have processed: a null `user.interrupt` — one written for "the primary's next turn", as an older API replica still writes it during a rolling deploy — and a null answer whose call already has a processed result (the legacy and rolling-deploy repair; an answer to a call still open is left to the walk). So a POST echo is null even for a waking message, and a message posted while a request is in flight stays null until the next request starts. The delivered `agent.thread_message_received` is written null too and stays so until its target's next request starts — the reference stamps each of the 22 recorded ones 43–212 ms after its target's running event and 1 µs before the start that consumes it (2026-09-12-child-lifecycle, 2026-09-19-self-hosted-latest-cli/setup.json among six recording sets). The null is ours twice over: no recording lists a received row before it is consumed, so its pending shape is inferred, and the SDK types the field as required (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentThreadMessageReceivedEvent.ProcessedAt), where `user.message`'s is nullable (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEvent.ProcessedAt); a typed client may reject it, while the console's schema accepts a null. A `user.define_outcome` is in the same position: echoed and stored null until the request that reads it starts, on a field the SDK also types as required (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserDefineOutcomeEvent.ProcessedAt), so a typed client may reject its POST echo too. A message delivered to a thread that never runs again — an archived child, or one out of retries that no delivery wakes — stays null for good. So, for a while, does a child's ending notice on the primary that a terminal grading settlement idled past, since it chains nothing (#801): it is stamped when the primary's next request reads it ("An outcome's last cycle, and what a terminal grading settlement chains on", among the inferences). *Evidence: recordings commit 16d24be5acdb0ee4729b8051b5363ce17991ed06, 2026-09-19-custom-order-followup/setup.json [77,83,84,87]; internal/api/events.go (sendSessionEvents, the answer clamp), internal/events/log.go (AppendInTx) and internal/events/orderedtools.go; internal/api/processing_order_test.go (stampsRunForward) and answerclamp_test.go; internal/events/span.go StartModelRequestOn and internal/events/log.go AppendOptions.Consume (the stamp at consumption); internal/events/span_test.go TestModelRequestStartStampsWhatItConsumes; internal/api/interruptstamp_test.go (an interrupt at receipt, a superseded answer); internal/events/stamper_test.go (one stamper per inbound type). Tracked: #78 (field omission, endpoint timestamp differences, and the present null on the required field of a delivered message and of a define_outcome).*
- **GET /v1/sessions/{id}/events — list filters and order keyed on created_at/seq, not processed_at** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.61.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams, the SDK re-worded the list params: each `created_at[gt/gte/lt/lte]` filter is "Compared against the event's `processed_at` value" and `order` is "ordered by the event's `processed_at`" (the param names and wire shape are unchanged). Ours compares the filters against the `created_at` column and orders by `seq`, the receipt order (≡ created_at order — appends serialize per session). The reference sorts by `processed_at`, which is the consumption order: all 228 distinct recorded lists do (#793), and an input not yet consumed sits at the tail with no `processed_at` and moves once it is — 2026-09-12-console-141/api-fixtures.json lists `user.message` `sevt_01C9bDZQjtPsLTqxCmq1YvKT` pending at idx 21 of `observation.session.approval.events`, and at idx 23 of `session.approval.final-events`, after the running pair its wake wrote and 1 µs before the `span.model_request_start` that consumed it. The two orders differ for an input consumed later than it was received: a `user.message`, `user.define_outcome` or `agent.thread_message_received` that landed while a model request was in flight, which we list at its receipt seq and the reference after that request's end and results: 1 µs before the next `span.model_request_start` (2026-09-03/batch2.json `conflict.turn3` idx 18, `dead-five-turns` idx 30), or 58.8 ms before it (2026-09-02/batch2.json sessT idx 78, the exception the entry above names); and a pending input, which we list at its receipt seq rather than at the tail. Our `processed_at` is now stamped at consumption, as the reference's is (the entry above), and replay and the grader and dream transcripts render such an input where it was consumed (internal/events/consumption.go), so what differs is the list and stream position alone; within one commit the log is already written in processing order (docs/plan/56_processing-order.md). A full processing-order log was rejected there: it would re-key seq, the SSE cursor and paging. Keying the filters on `processed_at` would also hide every input not yet consumed from a filtered list, since a null fails every comparison — a message posted mid-request included. Inference caveat: what the deployed service compares the filters against, and whether a filtered list shows a pending input, is unrecorded, and the processed_at wording is single-source spec-side (the SDK doc comment and the CLI usage string are generated from the same OpenAPI descriptions — one witness). *Evidence: the BetaSessionEventListParams doc comments, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams, the file's only change between the copies checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.59.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.61.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams, comment-only, proven by the empty non-comment diff; unchanged as far as the copy checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams, whose whole diff is empty against the copy checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaSessionEventListParams, the file byte-identical across that range; identical generated wording, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betasessionevent.go betaSessionsEventsList; internal/api/events.go listSessionEvents (created_at[…] → ListQuery.Created*); internal/events/log.go List (created_at comparisons, ORDER BY seq; "seq order and created_at order agree because appends serialize per session"); internal/api/events_test.go (filters pinned against the created_at column; default order chronological); 2026-09-12-console-141/api-fixtures.json (the pending message above)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle what the deployed service actually compares these filters against, and whether a filtered list shows an input not yet consumed).*
- **Event log — brain crash-window residue** — agent.thinking events and span.model_request_start commit mid-stream, so a brain dying mid-turn can leave a duplicate thinking event or a dangling span start on the log. Replay renders neither; accepted as cosmetic. A dangling start still bounds a consumption window, which the thread's next start closes, and it keeps the `processed_at` it stamped on the inputs it consumed (the processed_at entry above): the retried request replays them from their rows under a start of its own, which stamps nothing new, so a client reads them as processed from the dead request's start (ours). *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/brain` + `internal/queue` + state machine — the orchestration loop (slice 5)" ('Known crash-window residue (cosmetic, accepted)')*
- **Environment networking 'limited' (sandbox egress)** — **Carved down by plan 12 slice 4: on the Docker backend with the egress gate opted in (`CONTROLPLANE_URL` + `EXECUTOR_GATE_IMAGE` on the executor), `limited` now means the wire's "only allowed_hosts"** — the sandbox's egress rides the injected `HTTP(S)_PROXY` through the per-session gate, which admits CONNECT and plain-HTTP requests on the environment's `allowed_hosts` and whose owner-match firewall drops everything that bypasses the proxy. Plan 12 slice 4 completed on **both backends** — the K8s provider gained the gate as a native sidecar (slice 4d, PR #198), so a gate-opted-in limited or vault-attached K8s pod now means the same wire "only allowed_hosts" as Docker. The original stricter-than-wire fail-closed posture remains only where the gate does not run: an un-opted-in Docker executor still gives a limited sandbox NetworkMode none (no route out), and a K8s pod *without* the gate opt-in keeps the route-flushing init container. Both `limited` widening flags are now honored, the package-registry one only as far as a recording sized it — see the INFERRED gate-policy entry. *Evidence: internal/sandbox/sandboxtest/egress.go (GatedLimitedEgressOnlyThroughGate: allowed host reachable through the gate, non-allowed refused, direct egress dropped — on a real daemon, both backends); internal/sandbox/docker/docker.go networkMode + internal/sandbox/k8s/k8s.go (ungated limited still none / route-flush); docs/history/2026-07.md § "Vaults plan — archived" and § "Vaults acceptance"* *Tracked: #50 (delivered; BYOC gate delivery = #165).*
- **Built-in file/search tools — path confinement** — No workdir confinement: relative paths resolve against the workdir; absolute paths and absolute glob patterns are accepted. The reference runs tools on the host and canonicalizes/rejects escapes; here the container is the boundary, so a lexical check would be theatre. *Evidence: docs/history/2026-07.md § "`internal/toolset` — the built-in tools (slice 6, third part)" (slice-6 toolset decisions, divergences from the SDK's tools/agenttoolset)*
- **grep built-in tool — implementation strategy: the platform's own static ripgrep, installed in the sandbox** — grep runs ripgrep, as the reference does (the SDK's client-side toolset runs `rg -n --no-heading … -e PATTERN -- PATH` when one is on the PATH and a Go walker when none is), but always the same one: rg 15.2.0, upstream's statically linked musl builds for linux x86_64 and aarch64, embedded in the executor and the worker and written into a sandbox at `/tmp/.map-ripgrep/` the first time it greps there, chosen by the machine bash was built for, `${BASH_VERSINFO[5]}` — which bash sets itself, read-only, so no environment changes it, as an image's `ENV` or a vault credential can set `$HOSTTYPE`, and no child process prints it, so a banner an `ENV LD_PRELOAD` library prints in one cannot corrupt it — and by `uname -m` only where that machine is none rg is shipped for (a 32-bit bash on a 64-bit kernel); that fallback is a child, and a banner a loader prints in a child lands inside the frame of any platform script, which the image contract forbids rather than the frame keeping it out (docs/self-hosted-security.md). Every search first asks that binary for its `--version` in the same exec, and installs it again when the model has removed or replaced it — replacing a directory or a link the model left at its path, which `mv` would otherwise move the binary into. An install first proves, with an empty probe file, that the sandbox will execute a file under `/tmp/.map-ripgrep/`, so a noexec `/tmp` is a tool error before the 5 MB binary is carried in, on every call that meets it; it works in a directory of its own there, which it removes as it exits, and it first removes any such directory an earlier install left more than ten minutes before by the platform's clock — an exec killed at its deadline, an executor gone mid-upload — and nothing else. That sweep never reaches through a link: what stands at `/tmp/.map-ripgrep` and is not a directory — a link the model or an image put there, or a file — is removed first (the link itself, never what it names) and the directory made afresh, and the sweep and the install after it run from inside it on names relative to it, so a link swapped in while the binary is carried in — at that path or at the install's own directory — makes the install refuse rather than read or remove what the link points at. The upload is not held to that: the platform writes the binary into the install's directory by its absolute path, so a link a sandbox process swaps in there, or at `/tmp/.map-ripgrep`, before the upload lands takes that write wherever the link points inside the sandbox, and the install that follows refuses all the same. That is no boundary the platform owes: the model writes anywhere in its own sandbox itself. This reverses the earlier choice — one implementation over the image's GNU grep, find and awk, matched to rg as an oracle — by the owner's decision on #827: rg is now the implementation, so ripgrep's own walk applies (`.gitignore` inside a git repository and `.ignore`/`.rgignore` anywhere honoured, binary files skipped under a directory and answered with rg's notice when named outright, Rust regex syntax), with the reference's `--hidden` beside it (hidden files searched, the six version-control directories globbed out; the INFERRED grep entry), where the emulation read no ignore file and pruned `node_modules` instead, and the SDK's walker skips `.git` and `node_modules` by name. Unlike the SDK's rg it passes no `--max-filesize` (the SDK's is 8 MB), so a larger file is searched too — the output caps and the spill bound what comes back. There is no second implementation behind it: where rg cannot run — a machine other than x86_64 or aarch64, a `/tmp` the sandbox user cannot write or that is mounted noexec, a build made without `make ripgrep` — grep is a tool error naming the cause. The image needs no grep, find or awk for it: beyond `/bin/bash` and what every write already needs, the install runs `mkdir`, `cat`, `chmod`, `mv` and `rm` (and `uname` where bash was built for a machine rg is not shipped for and the image has one), and `head_limit`/`offset` run `head` and `tail`, and `cat` beside either. The binary a model finds there is its own to tamper with, like everything else in its sandbox: the version check catches a binary that is missing, broken or another program, not one built to answer it. *Evidence: internal/toolset/grep.go (query, script, prepareScript, installScript, installRipgrep), internal/ripgrep (the manifest, Open and Check); TestGrepParameters, TestGrepInstallsRipgrepInTheSandbox, TestGrepAsksUnameWhereBashNamesNoShippedMachine, TestGrepInstallSweepsWhatAnEarlierInstallLeft, TestGrepInstallNeverSweepsThroughALink, TestGrepInstallFollowsNoLinkSwappedInMidInstall, TestGrepInstallFaults, TestGrepRunsOnAMuslBusyboxImage, TestGrepInstallsRipgrepWhereTheSandboxIsNotRoot, TestGrepWithoutAnEmbeddedRipgrep, TestGrepSaysWhyRipgrepCannotRun, TestSearchesThroughAnImageBanner, TestGrepRunsTheSameInAKubernetesPod; the SDK's pair (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — search.go runRipgrep and runWalkGrep)*
- **web_fetch / web_search built-in tools — executor-process execution, pluggable backends** — Implemented (plan 15, #47): both tools execute **in the platform executor's own process on both deployment modes** — never in the sandbox, never on a BYOC worker (the official client toolset implements only the six sandbox tools, so a self_hosted web call reaching a real `ant` worker would fail unknown-tool), and deliberately **not** through the plan-12 egress gate: the reference documents that the environment's networking policy "does not affect the allowed domains for the web_search or web_fetch tools", so a `limited` environment does not constrain them and web egress originates at the executor. Routing is a third internal work kind, `web_exec` — claimed by the executor for **both** environment kinds; `Poll` never serves it (the kind taxonomy is internal, not wire). The backends are config-driven per design principle 4 where the reference's are a managed black box: Tavily-protocol search and Jina-Reader-protocol fetch behind `internal/webtool`'s Searcher/Fetcher seam (`TAVILY_API_KEY`/`JINA_API_KEY`/`WEBSEARCH_BASE_URL`/`WEBFETCH_BASE_URL`); an enabled tool whose backend is unconfigured answers an `is_error` result naming what is missing rather than being hidden from the definitions (`TAVILY_API_KEY` for search; `JINA_API_KEY` **or** `WEBFETCH_BASE_URL` for fetch — the Reader protocol works keyless, but a bare install must not silently egress model-chosen URLs to a public default). Results: `web_search` → one `search_result` block per hit, matching the SDK's `BetaManagedAgentsSearchResultBlock` field-for-field (round-trip-tested; `citations.enabled` is emitted **`true`**, which is what a 2026-09-02 recording caught the reference sending on every one of ten result blocks; it is emitted unconditionally, the agent's own citations config not being surfaced to vary it, and it is model-visible rather than cosmetic, because the flag is what tells the model it may cite the block), zero hits → a single "No results found." text block (the shape the Messages-API web_search docs prescribe for an empty result); `web_fetch` → a text block, where the same recording showed the reference answering with a **`document`** block carrying the page title and a `source` of `type: text` / `media_type: text/plain` — a mismatch, owned by **#547**, and a reviewer must not clear it on this entry's authority (the mixed-turn ordering is still an INFERRED entry below; the model-facing definitions are no longer one — the notes entry on them above — and nor is the block choice). *Evidence: internal/executor/webwork.go; internal/webtool; internal/toolset/definitions.go; internal/queue/queue.go (WebExec); internal/toolset/searchresult_test.go (SDK round-trip, whose fixture keeps the zero `citations.enabled` on purpose — that is what leaves its respjson presence assertion load-bearing, and it pins the block's shape, not the driver's value); internal/executor/webwork_test.go (TestWebSearchAnswersWithSearchResultBlocks pins the emitted `true`, on every block); the six-tool client toolset, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — agenttoolset.go BetaAgentToolset20260401; platform.claude.com managed-agents environments doc (the networking sentence)* *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json, entry idx 362 (`sessT.events.after-outcome`) — ten `search_result` blocks, every one `citations.enabled` true, and the only entry in the archive carrying the block at all.* *Tracked: #547 (the recorded `web_fetch` document-block mismatch, settled by the 2026-09-02 recording rather than left to a future one); landed for #47, #548.*
- **Web tools — the reference's per-tool domain lists refused; the fence is operator-side here (plan 36 slice 0)** — As checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsWebFetchToolConfig and BetaManagedAgentsWebSearchToolConfig, the SDK put the web tools' configuration on the wire: `web_fetch`'s config gained `allowed_domains`, `blocked_domains` and `max_content_tokens`, `web_search`'s gained `allowed_domains`, `blocked_domains` and `user_location`, each list documented as plain hostnames — `web_fetch`'s "no scheme, port, or path", `web_search`'s "no scheme or port; an optional path suffix is accepted" — at most 64 entries, an empty list rejected, and the two lists mutually exclusive. This platform's strict key check answers all four with a 400 — in its own words, `agent_toolset_20260401: unknown field "allowed_domains" in configs[0]`, even on the agent routes that answer other unknown config keys in the reference's sentence since #540: the reference accepts these fields, so no recording holds a refusal of them to borrow — and keeps the fence operator-side: `WEBTOOL_ALLOWED_DOMAINS` on the executor, entries in the wire's `allowed_hosts` grammar matched by the shared `egress.HostSet` and validated at startup, a blocked `web_fetch` host answered as a tool error before any fetch, a search hit outside the list dropped. Refusing beats accepting-and-ignoring here for the reason the field exists: a silently dropped `allowed_domains` reads to its author as a fence in force. The operator list is also the outer bound any per-agent list would sit inside, so honoring the wire fields is additive work — #481. **This entry replaces the inference the bump falsified.** It read "per-tool allowed domains exist in the reference, configured by no wire field", inferring a console-side mechanism because no field in the then-pinned SDK carried them; it is rewritten rather than deleted so the reversal stays auditable. The environments doc's sentence it rested on — networking "does not affect the allowed domains for the web_search or web_fetch tools" — now has a field behind it instead of a console. What the reference's own list holds is still unobserved; how it answers a fenced call is not, and the comparison against our text and our drop is drawn below. *Evidence: the response shapes, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsWebFetchToolConfig and BetaManagedAgentsWebSearchToolConfig; the params' documented grammar, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsWebFetchToolConfigParams.AllowedDomains and BetaManagedAgentsWebFetchToolConfigParams.BlockedDomains and BetaManagedAgentsWebSearchToolConfigParams.AllowedDomains and BetaManagedAgentsWebSearchToolConfigParams.BlockedDomains; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserLocation; the single struct that carried no domains field, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.63.1 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolConfig, and that the union split deleted, absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaagent.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentToolConfig; platform.claude.com managed-agents environments doc (Networking); internal/toolset/definitions.go rejectConfigKeys; internal/egress/hostset.go ValidateHostEntry (the one grammar the vault API and this list share), internal/executor/webwork.go:308 (the blocked-host tool error); internal/executor/webwork_test.go TestWebFetchOutsideAllowedDomainsAnswersIsError, TestWebSearchFiltersHitsOutsideAllowedDomains* **Recorded — a blocked domain answers the model as an ordinary `is_error` tool result, and the reference's own text names the field that blocked it.** The fetch is permitted first: the `agent.tool_use` for `web_fetch` on the blocked host carries `evaluated_permission: "allow"`, so a per-tool domain list is not a permission decision but a failure inside the call. It lands as one `agent.tool_result` with `is_error: true` and a single text block — `Web fetch error: url_not_allowed — example.com is a domain this agent is blocked from fetching (blocked_domains). Retrying this or other URLs on that domain will not work.` — and no accompanying error event: the turn continued, the model quoted the text back, and the session settled idle in the same turn. The refused call left `usage.server_tool_use.web_fetch_requests` at `0`, and a later pass measured a *successful* unfenced fetch at `0` too, so that counter distinguishes nothing about fetch; its search twin does move, reading `1` on a fenced search as on an unfenced one. **The event shape this platform already produces is the same one; its cause deliberately is not** — the executor answers a fenced host with an `is_error` text result before the fetcher is called, but the fence is the operator's `WEBTOOL_ALLOWED_DOMAINS` rather than a per-agent list, so the message names that variable and carries neither the reference's `url_not_allowed` code nor its futility hint. The recorded text is what #481 should emit once the wire fields are honored, not a correction to make while they are refused. The same probe family also confirmed on the wire the three list constraints the SDK only documents — the two lists mutually exclusive, an empty list refused as ambiguous, a 65th entry refused — each an `invalid_request_error` 400. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-03/batch2.json, probes `agent.create.web_fetch-blocked`, `session.create.blocked-domain`, `session.events.send.blocked-domain` and `session.events.list.blocked-domain`, with the three refusals `agent.create.web_fetch-both-lists`, `.web_fetch-empty-list` and `.web_fetch-65-entries`. A `web_search` agent carrying `allowed_domains` was created and never run in that pass.* **Recorded — the two tools do not answer a fence the same way.** A fenced `web_search` filters silently: `is_error: false`, no `url_not_allowed`, the turn ends `end_turn`, and the fence shows only in the hit list — `allowed_domains` narrowed ten search blocks to three, all on the listed domain, and `blocked_domains` kept ten while dropping every hit on the listed one. So the two behaviors #481 must produce are an error result for a fenced fetch and a shortened list for a fenced search, not one shape for both. A fenced search is also a counted request, `web_search_requests` reading `1` there as on the unfenced baseline. The same pass recorded the accepted side of the list bound — exactly **64** entries on both lists and both tools, each echo retaining all 64 — and a positive `max_content_tokens` in force: `100` returned a 339-character exact prefix of the uncapped 49,096, with no truncation flag, marker or error. Which stage of the search implementation drops the hits is not located by this, and the fenced-search URLs are search-provider output rather than pages verified reachable. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-12-followups/web-runtime.json, events idx 19 (unfenced baseline), 20 (`allowed_domains`), 21 (`blocked_domains`), [15..18] (the 64-entry acceptances), [22, 23] (the capped and uncapped fetch), with web-summary.json for URL lists, usage and content hashes.* *Tracked: #481 (accepting and honoring the four wire fields — the recorded blocked-fetch text and the recorded silent search filtering are the two answers to match); enforcement landed for #225.*
- **OpenAI-protocol routes — the built-in tools' schemas lose `format`, `minLength` and `additionalProperties`; the route's protocol decides, not its backend (owner decision on #682)** — The anthropic adapter hands each tool's `input_schema` to the endpoint as the platform built it, so an Anthropic-protocol model sees the built-in tools' recorded keywords as the reference sends them. The OpenAI adapter, the platform's lossy seam, removes all three keywords — and `unevaluatedProperties`, which closes an object as `additionalProperties` does and which no built-in carries yet — from the built-in tools' `parameters` alone. Which tools those are is provenance, not a name: the brain marks the definitions the `agent_toolset_20260401` expansion supplied (`provider.Request.BuiltinTools`), so a custom tool that takes a disabled built-in's name, and every MCP tool, reaches the endpoint with every keyword its author wrote — unchanged in content, the request's encoding compacting it as it always has — a contract no platform check stands behind, and one it carried before #682.
