- **Vault, agent, and session surfaces — documented limits enforced; environments document none (plan 12 D7; #66, #287, #289, #291, #665)** — The confirmed, deliberate divergence recorded here is the *asymmetry*: the vault/credential caps the public vaults guide documents are enforced on vaults, the agent caps the SDK's create/update params document are enforced on agents (#66), the whole-spec ones (tools and mcp_servers) on every session's resolved agent too — create-time resolve and the update patch's merged result alike (#287) — and the session metadata caps the same SDK bounds with the identical sentence ("Maximum 16 pairs, keys up to 64 chars, values up to 512 chars", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaSessionNewParams.Metadata) are enforced on session create and update (#289; the SDK documents them on the create params only — its session *update* params are silent, unlike agents' which restate the bound, so binding the post-patch bag extends the create-time doc rather than quoting an update-param one) — while **environments** enforce nothing because nothing documents caps for them; recorded once rather than silently harmonized in any direction. The vault caps: metadata ≤16 pairs / keys ≤64 / values ≤512 (create and after every patch — the same numbers the SDK documents for agent and session metadata, so the check is one shared helper), vault display_name required 1–255, credential display_name ≤255, ≤16 allowed_hosts, ≤20 credentials per vault. The agent caps: ≤128 tools, ≤20 mcp_servers with unique names and every server referenced by an `mcp_toolset` in the resulting tools, and the shared metadata caps; beside them `name` ≤256, `description` ≤2,048 and `system` ≤100,000 characters, which bind only a value a create or update supplies — never a stored one, and so never at resolve (#665; the spec's `maxLength`, which the Go doc comments drop — the agent-caps entry under INFERRED). The shared metadata caps are not sessions' only documented bounds: the create-time `initial_events` rules the public sessions page documents (50 events, 100 file-sourced document blocks, one define_outcome — the SDK states only the first, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaSessionNewParams.InitialEvents) are enforced too — recorded in the define_outcome entry below — and the 100,000-character ceiling on an `agent_with_overrides` replacement `system` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentWithOverridesParams.System) is enforced as well (#291) — the entry after this one records both `system` bounds' recorded rejects. Enforcement is our own — the *reference's* exact reject statuses and strings for exceeding a cap are, beyond those `system` rejects and agent create's metadata pair count (2026-09-02 `agent.create.metadata-17-keys`, whose sentence the agent routes answer since #540), unobserved and recorded under INFERRED below (the first vault entry, and the agent-caps entry, which covers the session metadata rejects too — the same helper, in our words off the agent routes); this entry claims only that we enforce them and that environments do not. *Evidence: platform.claude.com vaults guide (limits table); platform.claude.com managed-agents/sessions (fetched 2026-09-24 — "accepts a maximum of 50 events", and the create-request rejection table's rows for more than one `user.define_outcome` event and more than 100 file-sourced `document` content blocks across the whole list); the create/update param doc comments (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaagent.go BetaAgentNewParams and BetaAgentUpdateParams; checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaSessionNewParams and BetaSessionUpdateParams and BetaManagedAgentsAgentWithOverridesParams.System); internal/api/wire.go (validateMetadataCaps, validateAgentSpec); internal/api/sessions.go parseInitialEvents; internal/api/vaults_test.go TestVaultValidation; internal/api/agentlimits_test.go; internal/api/sessions_test.go TestSessionMetadataCaps* *Tracked: #50 (delivered; the vault caps are plan 12's, the agent and session caps the issues named above).*
- **`system` past 100,000 characters on agent create and on `agent_with_overrides` — the recorded rejects, words included, and the override's code-point unit (confirmed 2026-09-02; #291, #665; aligned for #540)** — The override's bound is its doc comment's ("Up to 100,000 characters", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsAgentWithOverridesParams.System); agent create's is the spec's `maxLength` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateAgentParams.properties.system), which the Go doc comment drops — why the override's landed for #291 and the agent's not until #665. **Recorded, and matched here:** agent create refused 100,001 ASCII characters with 400 `invalid_request_error` and accepted 100,000 `é` (200,000 UTF-8 bytes), which rules out bytes but cannot tell code points from UTF-16 units, `é` being one of each; the override refused 100,001 ASCII characters with 400 `invalid_request_error` and accepted 50,001 astral characters (100,002 UTF-16 units), so the override counts **code points**, as `utf8.RuneCountInString` does. **Matched since #540, the messages**: the reference's two validators do not agree with each other — `system: maximum string length is 100000` on agent create, `agent.agent_with_overrides.config.system: must be at most 100000 characters` on the override — and each is now answered in its own words, where this entry used to keep ours on the argument that a client branches on the type. Agent update's, never recorded, takes the create sentence under the `agent.` prefix the reference's update route puts on its paths (*Agent create/update caps* under INFERRED). **Ours:** that the override check binds only what the override supplies, never counting `system:null` (clear) or a preserved stored value. What stays inferred on the agent side — its unit past the BMP, update, stored values — is the agent-caps entry's under INFERRED. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json, probes resolved by name — `agent.create.system-100001-ascii` (400), `agent.create.system-100000-eacute` (200), `session.create.overrides.system-100001-ascii` (400), `session.create.overrides.system-100000-eacute` (200), `session.create.overrides.system-50001-astral` (200).* *Evidence: internal/api/wire.go (maxAgentSystemRunes, capRunes); internal/api/agents.go (parseAgentSpecFields); internal/api/sessions.go (overrideSystemMaxRunes, resolveAgent); internal/api/agentlimits_test.go TestAgentSystemCap; internal/api/sessions_test.go TestSessionOverrideSystemCap*
- **Vault placeholders — `vltph_` opaque tokens, ours to mint (plan 12 D4)** — The sandbox-visible stand-in for an environment-variable vault secret is an "opaque placeholder" the reference defines no format for; we use `vltph_`-prefixed tokens (the prefix plus 128 bits of hex). Both the prefix and the format are ours (deliberate). The token is **derived deterministically per `(session, secret_name)`** (SHA-256), not minted at random: `sandbox.Spec.Env` is bound at container create and kept across a session's idempotent re-provisions, so every executor pass and the egress gate must recover the exact token already in the sandbox rather than drift to a fresh value the gate could no longer substitute; a rotated credential keeps its placeholder. Determinism is safe because the placeholder is opaque, not a secret — the per-session gate substitutes only a session's own placeholders and only for a host the credential's `allowed_hosts` admit, so the security boundary is the gate and the host allow-list, never placeholder secrecy. Placeholder minting, injection into `Spec.Env`, and egress-time substitution are all live: the per-session gate substitutes a session's own placeholders on admitted plain-HTTP egress on both backends (Docker gate-pair, K8s native sidecar), leaving in-sandbox HTTPS placeholders literal until the #166 TLS gap closes. This never affects wire compatibility: a placeholder never crosses the management API — it lives only inside the sandbox and the egress path — so the reference's own placeholder shape is unobserved and irrelevant. *Evidence: internal/egress/engine.go (PlaceholderPrefix, Placeholder); internal/egress/engine_test.go TestPlaceholder; internal/vaultresolve/vaultresolve.go; plan 12 D4* *Tracked: #50 (delivered; in-sandbox HTTPS substitution of the placeholder is the open #166).*
- **Internal gate-config endpoint (`GET /internal/v1/gate/config`) (plan 12 slice 4)** — A control-plane endpoint **outside the public `/v1` wire**, authenticated by a per-session `gtk_` gate token (`Authorization: Bearer`) on its own lane in `dispatchAuth` — selected by path, so a `gtk_` token never reaches a management handler and an `x-api-key` never reaches this one. It returns the session's environment networking policy and its resolved, **decrypted** vault credentials (placeholder, secret, the credential's own `allowed_hosts` arm, injection locations, and non-secret `credential_id`) — the two inputs `internal/gate` needs. The endpoint and its client (`internal/gateconfig`) are entirely ours: the reference's gate is a managed black box, so a self-hosting gate that fetches its own policy and secrets has no reference equivalent and observes no reference wire shape — a settled, reference-less deliberate divergence (nothing here awaits a recording). Plaintext secrets cross this internal boundary in the response body only; the resolution error carries credential ids, never secret or ciphertext bytes (`vaultresolve.Credentials`). A 401 (revoked token / archived session) is the gate's unambiguous fail-closed signal; a transient error leaves it on last-known-good. *Evidence: internal/api/gateauth.go (requireGateToken, isGateConfigPath), internal/api/gateconfig.go (getGateConfig), internal/gateconfig (Client, Config, ErrUnauthorized); internal/gatetoken; plan 12 slice 4* *Tracked: #50 (delivered; the endpoint shipped with plan 12's gate and serves only it).*
- **Model gateway admin API (`/admin/v1/`) (plan 59 slice 1)** — The model gateway, a server of its own beside the control plane, is managed through `/admin/v1/`: providers and their sealed credentials, deployments, aliases, per-key model grants, and, read-only, the compiled-in vendor profiles and the usage ledger with its daily rollups (slice 2). The reference runs no self-hostable model gateway, so the surface is entirely ours and observes no reference wire shape — a settled, reference-less deliberate divergence (nothing here awaits a recording): its paths, its `{"data": [...]}` lists — unpaginated but for the ledger's, which pages newest first by `before` with `has_more` — and its ids, whose prefixes `gwprov_`, `gwcred_` and `gwdep_` sit deliberately outside `internal/domain`'s wire list. Errors use the platform's envelope. Its auth departs from the control plane's management lane on purpose: `x-api-key` (or a Bearer) admits only the bootstrap key, compared in constant time and never looked up in `api_keys`, so every key an application issues is refused — one named `bootstrap` included — since a key that could edit key policies could lift its own limits; an operator token, verified under the control plane's `IDENTITY_*` configuration, reads with viewer or developer and writes with admin. No binary serves it until slice 2. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway/admin (package comment, authenticate, isBootstrap); internal/modelgateway/store (newID); internal/modelgateway/admin/admin_test.go (TestAuthByKey, TestAuthByRole, TestAuthBehindAProxy); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway inference (`/v1/messages`, `/v1/messages/count_tokens`, `/v1/models`) (plan 59 slice 2)** — The gateway answers Anthropic Messages for a platform API key and passes each request through to a vendor's Anthropic-compatible endpoint, so its wire is the Messages API's but for these deliberate differences. An answer's `model`, whole or in `message_start`, is the name the caller sent — the alias — not the upstream model id. `count_tokens` is answered as the upstream answers it, so a vendor that serves none answers its own 404 and a client falls back to estimating, as Anthropic's gateway compatibility guide describes. `/v1/models` lists aliases rather than models: the chat aliases the key's policy covers, never the `*` wildcard, in name order, with `after_id` and `before_id` compared as names, so a cursor need not still exist. Its `capabilities` answer image input and thinking (as `types.enabled`) only where every target deployment records them, and false for every capability a deployment does not record; `max_input_tokens` and `max_tokens` are the smallest every target records, and zero when one records none; `created_at` is the alias's creation. The root path answers that shape only to a request carrying `anthropic-version`, as every Anthropic SDK sends, or under the `/anthropic` prefix; other callers get OpenAI's (the next entry). Every response carries the gateway's own `request-id` header, and every error the inference routes write — the error event that ends a stream included — its `request_id`; an upstream's own error, a body or an `error` event, keeps its status and content with the call's credentials removed and, in Anthropic's envelope, the gateway's `request_id` in place of the upstream's, which goes to the gateway's log. Two upstream errors are not relayed as sent. A vendor refusing the gateway's own account (HTTP 401, 402 or 403, or an `authentication_error`, `permission_error` or `billing_error` event) answers 502 `api_error`, so a caller never reads it as its own key failing. A stream whose first event, after any keep-alives (comments, `ping` events and unnamed events with no data, held back up to 64 KiB), is an error answers as that error's HTTP response — `overloaded_error` as 529, `rate_limit_error` as 429, and so on, and a type the gateway does not know as the 4xx or 5xx its body states, as MiniMax's states `error.http_code` (a 401, 402 or 403 being the vendor refusing the gateway's own credential), else 500 — since the caller has seen nothing yet. Before a stream begins, an upstream that stalls answers 504 `timeout_error`, and one that cannot be reached, answers a redirect, or breaks off a whole answer it had begun, 502 `api_error`. Once a stream has begun the status is already sent, so a failure — a stall, a broken connection, or a stream that ends before `message_stop` — is an `error` event of those types; the relay ends at `message_stop` or an upstream's own error event, and nothing follows either. A repeated credential header is refused as ambiguous. So is, with 400 `invalid_request_error`, a `stream` that is not a boolean or null, or a top-level key that differs from `stream` only in case (`count_tokens` excepted, as it never streams): a lenient or case-insensitive upstream could read either as asking for a stream, which the gateway would relay as a whole answer whose usage it cannot read; this holds on the Chat Completions route too. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway (gateway.go, auth.go, messages.go streamFlag, models.go); the model list checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — model.go ModelInfo and ModelCapabilities, and the error envelope checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — shared.go ErrorResponse; internal/modelgateway/models_test.go (TestModels); internal/modelgateway/messages_test.go (TestMessagesPassThrough, TestMessagesStream, TestCountTokens); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway OpenAI surfaces — Chat Completions passthrough, OpenAI's model list and error envelope (plan 59 slice 4)** — The gateway answers OpenAI's Chat Completions at `/v1/chat/completions`, also under an `/openai` prefix, for a platform API key, and passes each request through to a vendor's OpenAI-compatible endpoint, at its `/chat/completions`, with the provider's key as a Bearer and none of the caller's headers. The reference serves no OpenAI surface, so the wire is OpenAI's but for these deliberate differences. The answer's `model`, whole and in every chunk, is the name the caller sent. A stream is asked for its usage — `stream_options.include_usage` set true, the caller's other stream options kept, and `stream_options` that is not an object or null, or a key differing from `stream_options` or `include_usage` only in case, refused with a 400, for the reason a `stream` the gateway cannot read is (the previous entry) — and the chunk carrying it (no choices, usage set) reaches only a caller that asked; a vendor that reports usage on a content chunk unasked, as DeepSeek does, still shows it there. A stream ends at `[DONE]`, at an upstream chunk carrying `error` or an event named `error`, which is relayed with the call's credentials removed, or once every choice it began has finished and none has gone on after its finish (`n` may ask for several), however the upstream then closes it — MiniMax-M3 never sends `[DONE]`, and the gateway does not add one; after a choice whose index is no whole number, only `[DONE]` ends it. An event whose data is not a JSON object — an error that does not parse, an empty data line, a diagnostic, under any event name, a ping's included, since an OpenAI client reads every event's data — is not relayed: the gateway's own `api_error` chunk takes its place and ends the stream. One that opens with an error, named or not, has not begun the answer, as a Messages stream that does has not: it is retried or falls back, and answers, when nothing serves, as the HTTP response the error would have been — a 502 `api_error` of the gateway's own when its data is not a JSON object. One whose only chunk the upstream cuts off at its end is relayed completed. Closed before any of these, or failing partway, it ends with a `data: {"error": {…}}` chunk of type `api_error` or `timeout_error`, which openai-go reports as the stream's error. `/v1/models` answers in OpenAI's shape on the `/openai` prefix and at the root for a request without `anthropic-version`: `{"object": "list", "data": [...]}`, unpaginated, each model's `id` an alias, `created` the alias's creation in seconds and `owned_by` `modelgateway`, listing every alias the key may use, embedding and rerank aliases included, never `*`. Every error the gateway writes on these routes is in OpenAI's envelope, `{"error": {"message", "type", "param": null, "code": null}}`, with Anthropic's error types, which name the failure in either; an upstream's own error body keeps its status and content, its credentials removed, whatever its envelope — MiniMax answers in Anthropic's even on its OpenAI endpoint (probed 2026-10-08). Routing, retry, fallback, limits and the ledger are the Messages routes'; a ledger row's `protocol` is `openai` and `endpoint` `chat_completions`, and its input excludes the cache reads, which both vendors count inside `prompt_tokens` and report as `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens` (DeepSeek also as `prompt_cache_hit_tokens`), and the cache writes, which Kimi's kimi-k3 counts there too and reports as `prompt_tokens_details.cache_write_tokens`. No thinking provenance applies: `reasoning_content` carries no signature, and goes as sent. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway (gateway.go ServeHTTP and writeError, chat.go, messages.go chatStream, models.go listOpenAIModels, usage.go chatUsageOf); the error envelope checked against github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 — internal/apierror/apierror.go Error, the model list against model.go Model, the usage chunk against chatcompletion.go ChatCompletionStreamOptionsParam, and a stream's end against packages/ssestream/ssestream.go; internal/modelgateway/chat_test.go; internal/modelgateway/live_chat_test.go (TestLiveChatCompletions, against DeepSeek and MiniMax); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway conversion — a Messages request served by a Chat Completions upstream (plan 59 slice 4)** — A credential allowed only on its provider's OpenAI endpoint serves a Messages request by conversion: the request goes to the vendor's `/chat/completions` as Chat Completions, and the answer comes back in Messages. A credential that can pass through always does, so conversion is each credential's fallback, never a preference. A converted attempt sends none of the caller's `anthropic-*` headers, `anthropic-beta` among them, which neither stop a request from converting nor change it, and the answer's `id` is the upstream's (`chatcmpl-…`) rather than a `msg_` id. The reference converts nothing, and what Chat Completions cannot carry is handled as follows, deliberately. Every top-level field `MessageNewParams` names is mapped, dropped where a Chat Completions vendor would ignore it (`top_k`, `cache_control`, `metadata`, `service_tier`, `inference_geo`, `container`, a tool result's `is_error`, `thinking.budget_tokens`, a tool's `cache_control`, `eager_input_streaming` and `input_examples`, and `thinking` itself where the vendor names no `thinking.type` for it), or refused (`output_config.format`, a server tool, a document block, an image outside a user turn, `thinking.display` `omitted`, since the reasoning comes back as text, a tool's `defer_loading: true`, which needs tool search, or `allowed_callers` without `direct`, a final assistant turn, which Messages continues and Chat Completions would answer, and a field the pin does not name, at the top level, in a tool or in `thinking`, where `budget_tokens` goes beside `enabled` alone and `display` beside `enabled` and `adaptive`); a request the conversion refuses goes to the alias's passthrough attempts, and with none it is answered 400 `invalid_request_error` naming the field. So is a request without `max_tokens`, which Messages requires and Chat Completions would let go uncapped. A text block whose `text` is absent, not null, is read as empty, as the brain stores one, and an empty `tools` array as none. `count_tokens` is never converted: with only conversion attempts it answers 404 `not_found_error`, as for a vendor without it. What a vendor ignores is judged on the converted request by the Chat Completions rules (the vendor profiles entry below), the refusal naming the field as the caller wrote it (`tool_choice.disable_parallel_tool_use`, `stop_sequences`, `tool_choice.type`). The answer's `reasoning_content` becomes a thinking block signed with the provenance wrapper around an empty value, `mapgw1.<deployment id>.`, so it goes back only to the deployment that produced it and only on a conversion attempt — as `reasoning_content`, which DeepSeek requires in a tool loop — never to that deployment's Anthropic endpoint, which could not verify it, while a signed block never goes to an OpenAI endpoint, and the deployment's attempts on the protocol that produced the newest thinking are tried before its others; an assistant turn of reasoning alone goes back with empty content. `length` is `max_tokens`, a tool call it cut short included, which a whole answer leaves out; `content_filter` is `refusal`, as is an answer that refused, which OpenAI ends with `stop` and a `refusal` in place of content (a whole answer's text is its content, then its refusal), its tool calls included, since a refusal is terminal and a caller that stops for one runs none of them; otherwise a tool call stops for `tool_use` whatever the finish, and every other finish is `end_turn`, DeepSeek's `insufficient_system_resource` among them; `stop_sequence` is never set, as Chat Completions does not say which matched. The usage is the ledger's reading, input without the cache reads and writes, which `cache_read_input_tokens` and `cache_creation_input_tokens` count. A stream follows the Messages event flow: `message_start` carries zero usage and `message_delta` the counts; the gateway pings in place of the upstream's keep-alives and after 15 seconds with nothing written, and its own pings are not the upstream's sign of life, so a silent upstream is still cut off at its stall budget; and a stream the upstream closes after its finish without `[DONE]`, as MiniMax-M3 does, still ends with `message_delta` and `message_stop`. A streamed chunk the conversion cannot carry — a second choice, `function_call`, a tool call resumed after another began, a tool call's name or id arriving after its block opened, a `content`, `refusal` or `reasoning_content` that is no string, since Messages names a tool once, at `content_block_start` — ends the stream with an `api_error` event, nothing after it, while the gateway reads the upstream on to its end or its error for the usage it reports after its finish, which the ledger records. A whole answer it cannot carry — no choice, `function_call`, a tool call whose arguments are not a JSON object where the token limit did not cut it short, a `content`, `refusal` or `reasoning_content` that is no string — is the gateway's 502 `api_error`, not retried once the upstream has answered, its tokens counted. A streamed tool call's arguments go as `input_json_delta` fragments, unchecked, as a passthrough stream's are. A tool call's arguments sent as a JSON object, which Z.ai's chat-completion reference documents, are read as its JSON, and must be the call's only fragment, a streamed call failing the stream otherwise; any other value that is no string is refused. An upstream error is in Anthropic's envelope with the gateway's `request_id`, its type the upstream's where it is one of Anthropic's and otherwise the one Anthropic gives its status (a 422 is `invalid_request_error`); MiniMax's, already in that envelope, is kept. MiniMax-M3 returns its reasoning inline, as `<think>…</think>` in its content, rather than in `reasoning_content` (live tier, 2026-10-08), so a converted answer carries it in the text block and the caller's next turn sends it back as text. The ledger row keeps the caller's protocol and endpoint, `anthropic` and `messages`; the attempt's client span names `modelgateway.upstream.protocol` `openai`. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway/convert (request.go Request, messages.go Messages, answer.go Answer and StopReason, stream.go Stream); internal/modelgateway (converted.go, messages.go inference and attempt, edits.go honoring, thinking.go keptFor); internal/modelgateway/catalog/route.go upstreamProtocol; the request's fields checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — message.go MessageNewParams, the event flow checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — messageutil.go Message.Accumulate, and the chunks checked against github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 — chatcompletion.go ChatCompletionChunk; internal/modelgateway/converted_test.go; internal/modelgateway/live_convert_test.go (TestLiveConversion, against DeepSeek and MiniMax); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway Responses — stateless, served as Anthropic Messages (plan 59 slice 6)** — The gateway answers OpenAI's Responses API at `POST /v1/responses`, also under the `/openai` prefix, for a platform API key, by converting the request to a Messages one and serving it as `/v1/messages` is — routing, retry, fallback, thinking provenance, limits, and, for a credential allowed only on its provider's OpenAI endpoint, the conversion to Chat Completions (the previous entry), so a request may cross two conversions each way. No v1 vendor serves Responses and the reference serves no OpenAI surface; what follows is deliberate. Nothing is stored: `previous_response_id`, `conversation`, `prompt` and an `item_reference` input item, which name stored state, are refused with a 400 saying so, as is `background: true`; `store` is accepted and ignored; and a stored response's paths under `/v1/responses/` — retrieve, delete, cancel, `input_items` — answer 404 `not_found_error` saying the API is stateless, as `input_tokens` and `compact`, stateless but not served, do saying that. A request carries its whole conversation in `input`: a string is one user message; message items become Messages turns, consecutive items of one role joined, and a `system` or `developer` message's text joins `instructions` in `system`, a blank line apart; `function_call` and `function_call_output` items become `tool_use` and `tool_result` blocks, the results leading their user turn, and a call a client records beside its output rejoins the assistant turn it came from when its id is one the gateway wrote naming that turn's Response, so the turn keeps its thinking — a client that drops the ids, or assigns its own, gets a turn per call; a `reasoning` item becomes the thinking block it came from (below). Only function tools are served, `strict` carried, and parameters stating no type typed `object`, as Messages requires; a request offering no tools drops `tool_choice` `none` or `auto` and `parallel_tool_calls: false`, which ask nothing of it; a built-in tool, `allowed_tools`, a `reasoning.context` other than `auto` or `all_turns` (the gateway sends the model every reasoning item the input carries), a `reasoning.mode` other than `standard`, `prompt_cache_options` with `prewarm` true (it asks for no answer) or a `comparison_response_id` (a stored response), an `input_file` part, an image by `file_id`, an item type github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 does not name, `text.format` `json_object`, `truncation: "auto"`, `top_logprobs` above 0 and an `include` naming logprobs are refused, and `text.format` `json_schema` goes as `output_config.format`. `reasoning.effort` `none` turns thinking off, and any other asks for adaptive thinking at that effort, `minimal` as `low`, since a request for an effort asks a reasoning model to reason and MiniMax-M3 thinks only when sent `adaptive` (probed 2026-10-09; DeepSeek's two models and MiniMax's two take it on both endpoints); with no effort the model decides; and where a Chat Completions upstream's profile names no thinking toggle (`openai-generic`), `none` reaches it as nothing, as a Messages request's disabled thinking does (the previous entry), so its model reasons as by default. `reasoning.summary` and `generate_summary` are dropped, as are `prompt_cache_options`' other fields and a part's fields a block has no place for — an image's `detail`, an `output_text`'s `annotations` and `logprobs`; `text.verbosity`, a way of writing the answer a Messages model has no knob for; `max_tool_calls`, which counts built-in tool calls, and those are refused; `stream_options`, `service_tier`, `prompt_cache_key`, `prompt_cache_retention`, `safety_identifier` and `user`, an OpenAI account's knobs; an `include` other than logprobs, as a reasoning item always carries `encrypted_content`; and `top_logprobs` 0. `metadata` is echoed and not sent, as Messages' `metadata` takes a `user_id` alone, and `context_management`, `moderation` and `access_programs`, which ask of the answer what a Messages model does not do, are refused. `max_output_tokens` is `max_tokens`, any positive integer, as Messages takes it — a floor OpenAI's API may set, which github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 does not state, is not enforced — and left out the gateway sends the alias's `max_tokens` as `/v1/models` gives it, or 8,192; `temperature` goes as sent, though Messages takes at most 1 where OpenAI takes 2, and the upstream judges it. A refusal of the converted request names the field as the caller wrote it (`max_output_tokens`, `parallel_tool_calls`, `reasoning.effort`, `input`), and every error is in OpenAI's envelope, an upstream's type and message carried into it from either envelope, with the `param` and `code` OpenAI's carries, the call's credentials removed from each — on the conversion path too, where a Messages caller is given the Anthropic type the previous entry describes, a body stating no type taking that entry's type for its status. The Response's `id` is `resp_` and the gateway's request id, its `model` the alias, and it echoes the request's `instructions`, `metadata`, `tools`, `tool_choice`, `parallel_tool_calls`, `temperature`, `top_p`, `reasoning`, `text`, `truncation` and `max_output_tokens`, with the defaults openai-go requires where they are unset. Each text block is a `message` item of one `output_text`; a thinking block is a `reasoning` item whose `summary` is one `summary_text` holding the thinking itself, not a summary of it, and whose `encrypted_content` is the signature as the gateway returns it (`mapgw1.`-wrapped), whatever `include` says; a `redacted_thinking` block is a `reasoning` item with an empty `summary` and its data as `encrypted_content`; a `tool_use` block is a `function_call` whose `call_id` is the block's id. Sent back as input each becomes its block again, so a tool loop keeps the vendor's thinking, and a `reasoning` item without `encrypted_content` is dropped. `max_tokens` and `model_context_window_exceeded` stop the Response `incomplete` with reason `max_output_tokens`, `refusal` `incomplete` with `content_filter`, and anything else `completed`; the item an incomplete Response stopped in — its last in output order — is `incomplete` too, its streamed `output_item.done` held until the answer's end, whose last stop reason decides it. The usage's `input_tokens` counts the cache reads and writes, which `input_tokens_details` gives as `cached_tokens` and `cache_write_tokens`, and `output_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` is the upstream's `output_tokens_details.thinking_tokens`, which MiniMax reports, and 0 where it reports none, as no conversion from Chat Completions carries one; a count below zero or past 2^32 is left out, as the ledger leaves it out. A server tool's block has no Responses item: a whole answer carrying one is the gateway's 502 `api_error`. A stream is `response.created`, `response.in_progress`, each item's `output_item`, `content_part` or `reasoning_summary_part`, delta and done events, then `response.completed` or `response.incomplete`, each event named and numbered from 0 by `sequence_number`, with no `[DONE]`; keep-alives are SSE comments. A stream failing partway — an upstream error event, a stream cut short, a block the conversion cannot carry, a `message_stop` arriving with a block still open, whose content the Response would leave out, an event the gateway's rewriting grew past the 16 MiB bound on one event, block starts and deltas past the gateway's bound on a whole answer (64 MiB), which everything the stream holds for its last event comes from — ends with `response.failed`, the error inside its Response with code `rate_limit_exceeded`, `invalid_prompt` or `server_error`, since openai-go aborts on any event whose data carries a top-level `error`; the upstream is read on for its usage. The ledger row's `protocol` is `openai` and its `endpoint` `responses`. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway/convert (responses.go ResponsesRequest, responses_answer.go ResponsesAnswer, responses_stream.go ResponsesStream); internal/modelgateway (responses.go, messages.go serve and call.fail, gateway.go ServeHTTP); the request's fields checked against github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 — responses/response.go ResponseNewParams, the Response against Response and ResponseStreamEventUnion, a stream's end against packages/ssestream/ssestream.go; internal/modelgateway/convert/responses_test.go; internal/modelgateway/responses_test.go; internal/modelgateway/live_responses_test.go (TestLiveResponses, against DeepSeek and MiniMax on both endpoints); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway embeddings and rerank (`/v1/embeddings`, `/v1/rerank`) (plan 59 slice 4)** — The gateway answers OpenAI's Embeddings at `/v1/embeddings`, and rerank at `/v1/rerank` in the shape Gitee AI, Zhipu, Jina and Cohere share (`{model, query, documents, top_n}` in, `results[{index, relevance_score}]` out), which neither OpenAI nor Anthropic serves; both also under `/openai`, for a platform API key. Each passes through to an alias of its own kind, `embedding` or `rerank`, on a vendor's OpenAI-compatible host at `/embeddings` or `/rerank`, with the provider's key as a Bearer and none of the caller's headers. The reference serves neither, so the wire is the upstream's but for these deliberate differences. The body goes upstream as sent but for `model` — `input`'s strings, token arrays or multimodal objects, `encoding_format`, `dimensions` and `top_n` included — and a stream is refused: `stream` must be false, null or absent, and is read as on the chat routes (the inference entry above), since neither API streams and an upstream that honored one would answer in a shape whose usage the ledger and the TPM limit cannot read. The answer is relayed as it arrives, byte for byte as the upstream wrote it but for its top-level `model`, which, whatever it held, becomes the name the caller sent: nothing in it is held or decoded, so a vector, float or base64, reaches the caller as the upstream wrote it — an upstream that answers floats to a base64 request, as Gitee does (probed 2026-10-08), reaches the caller so — and it is cut off past 512 MiB, several times the largest batch the vendors' caps allow, its ledger row then `api_error`. An answer that breaks off partway leaves the caller the cut-off body and its ledger row the `api_error` it was. No batch is split: an upstream's cap answers with its own 400, its credentials removed, and the gateway's only bound of its own is the 32 MiB it takes in any request body, past which it answers 413 `request_too_large` before an upstream sees the batch — a few hundred images sent as data URLs reach it — so a caller sizes its batches under both. An alias of another kind is refused with 400 `invalid_request_error` naming the kind it serves, here and on the chat routes alike, and the wildcard alias `*` catches names on its own kind's routes alone, so a name no alias matches is 404 on the others; errors use OpenAI's envelope (the Chat Completions entry above). A ledger row's `endpoint` is `embeddings` or `rerank`, its input the `usage.prompt_tokens` the upstream reported, else its `usage.total_tokens`, all of such a call's tokens being input, and it records no tokens where the upstream reports neither, or a usage past 64 KiB, the whitespace between its tokens aside — Gitee's rerank reports zeros as `totalTokens` and `promptTokens`. A provider on the `gitee` profile sends each request on a connection no other request has used, from a transport that keeps none (`Connection: close` on HTTP/1.1), so it never takes one another provider on the same host left idle. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway (messages.go routes, inference and attempt, vectors.go relayVectors, answerRewriter and vectorUsageOf); internal/modelgateway/upstream (NoReuse); internal/modelgateway/profile (profile.go, the gitee profile); the answer an OpenAI client reads checked against github.com/openai/openai-go/v3 v3.73.0 — embedding.go CreateEmbeddingResponse; internal/modelgateway/embeddings_test.go; internal/modelgateway/vectors_test.go; internal/modelgateway/live_gitee_test.go (TestLiveGiteeEmbeddingsAndRerank, against Gitee AI); docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*
- **Model gateway vendor profiles — `search_result` flattened, requests a vendor would ignore sent elsewhere (plan 59 slice 2)** — A provider's profile edits what reaches its vendor's Anthropic endpoint, after thinking provenance and deterministically, so a conversation's prefix stays stable. For DeepSeek and MiniMax, which refuse a `search_result` block (DeepSeek with a 422 in every position, MiniMax with a 400, probed 2026-10-07; #565), each one in a `tool_result`'s content is sent as the text block `provider.SearchResultText`, the rendering the brain's `flatten_search_results` uses, makes of its fields read by their exact keys, its citations config dropped and its `cache_control` kept. A `search_result` anywhere else, or one with a title or source that is not a string or an inner block that is not text, goes as sent, for the vendor to refuse; a null title, source or content counts as absent. A request setting what a vendor documents, or the live tier shows, it ignores while the answer depends on it — DeepSeek's `tool_choice.disable_parallel_tool_use: true`, MiniMax's non-empty `stop_sequences`, and `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` on MiniMax's M2.x models, and, on the live tier's evidence (docs/HISTORY.md, "Model gateway live tier (plan 59 slice 2g) — acceptance record, 2026-10-08"), a `tool_choice` of type `any` on DeepSeek and of type `any` or `tool` on MiniMax, which answer without the tool call it forces, and MiniMax's `tool_choice.disable_parallel_tool_use: true` — or, on Chat Completions (slice 4, on its own probes: docs/HISTORY.md, "Model gateway Chat Completions (plan 59 slice 4a)"), `parallel_tool_calls: false` on either vendor, and MiniMax's non-empty `stop`, a `tool_choice` of `required`, naming a function, or `allowed_tools` in `required` mode, and `thinking: {"type": "disabled"}` on its M2.x models, as on the Messages routes, the rules a Messages request the gateway converts is also judged by (the conversion entry above) — is not sent to that vendor's deployments: the alias's other deployments serve it, and with none left the gateway answers 400 `invalid_request_error` naming the field — or, when the deployments were dropped for different fields, each of them — where the Messages API, or OpenAI's, would take the request. `count_tokens` is routed alike but never refused, since what a vendor ignores leaves the count unchanged. Fields are read by their exact keys. On the Messages routes the key goes to MiniMax and Moonshot as `Authorization: Bearer`, which MiniMax recommends and Moonshot requires, and to every other upstream as `x-api-key`; on Chat Completions it goes to every upstream as a Bearer. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway/profile (profile.go, each edit with its vendor's documentation or the live tier's finding), internal/modelgateway (edits.go, profiles_test.go, live_vendor_test.go).*
- **Model gateway thinking provenance — signatures returned wrapped, history thinking filtered (plan 59 slice 2)** — Every thinking block's `signature` and every `redacted_thinking` block's `data` the gateway returns is wrapped as `mapgw1.<deployment id>.<the upstream's value>`, naming the deployment that produced it; both fields are opaque to a client, which returns them verbatim. In a stream the wrapper rides on each block's first non-empty value — its `content_block_start`, or its first non-empty `signature_delta` — and the rest pass unchanged, so for blocks streamed one after another, as Anthropic streams them, the fragments concatenated, as the SDK assembles them, equal the whole answer's value. A block whose value is empty, whole or streamed, comes back as the upstream sent it: naming no producer, it is sent back to no upstream — and so does every thinking block after it in the same answer, whose signature covers it; in a stream, a thinking block that starts while the one before it is still unsigned ends the wrapping the same way, as do an empty `redacted_thinking` block and an empty start that names no index. On a request, the producer of the newest block among the alias's targets is tried first, whatever its priority, and each deployment is sent only its own blocks, unwrapped: any other thinking block — another deployment's, an unwrapped one, one naming a deployment outside the alias — is removed, an assistant message the removal empties goes with it, and the user turns it separated go as one; `count_tokens` is filtered alike. A 400 whose message names a thinking block's signature, a `redacted_thinking` block, a thinking block, or a thinking field of one of the request's blocks — not DeepSeek's "must be passed back" or Anthropic's "must start with a thinking block", which ask for thinking the request lacks, nor one about a parameter that configures thinking — earns the request, when the attempt carried thinking, one more attempt with every thinking block removed: a retry like any other, within the deployment's attempt budget and after the backoff, so not made for a caller that has left. Strip mode holds for every attempt after it, its answer's first thinking block, when signed, is wrapped `mapgw1r.` instead, and later requests drop every thinking block ahead of the newest such block. A Messages answer whose thinking no upstream checks against a prefix — a converted one, whose blocks the gateway signed, or a passthrough answer from a vendor whose profile says so (DeepSeek, probed) — carries `X-MAP-Thinking-Prefix: unchecked`, a header the gateway writes itself and the brain reads (plan 61); no other answer carries it, an upstream's never. *Evidence: internal/modelgateway/thinking.go; internal/modelgateway/messages.go (inference, relayStream, messagesStream); internal/modelgateway/thinking_test.go (TestThinkingReturnsToItsProducerAlone, TestAStreamedAnswerWrapsAsTheWholeAnswerDoes, TestAStreamWrapsInBlockOrder, TestAnUnsignedBlockEndsWhatAnAnswerWraps, TestARefusedThinkingIsStrippedOnce, TestStripModeHoldsThroughFallback); internal/modelgateway/thinking_prefix_test.go (TestTheGatewaySaysWhenThinkingMayGoBackUnderAnyPrefix); internal/modelgateway/live_test.go (TestLiveThinkingRoundTrip, against DeepSeek and MiniMax); the fragments' assembly checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — messageutil.go Message.Accumulate; docs/plan/59_model-gateway.md*

- **Vault credential create, update and `mcp_oauth_validate` (deployment-configured) — a `gcpkms` deployment bounds secret material at the key's ceiling: 64 KiB, or 8 KiB on an HSM key** — With `SECRETS_BACKEND=gcpkms`, credential secret material whose sealed plaintext — the JSON object built from all of a credential's secret fields, so the bound is on their sum — exceeds that ceiling is refused with a **400 `invalid_request_error` naming the limit**, where the identical request on the identical build succeeds under the `local` or `openbao` cipher; which of the two ceilings binds is a property of the key version, not of the code, and a resource name does not reveal it. Three request paths answer this way: create (`POST /v1/vaults/{id}/credentials`) and update (`POST …/credentials/{cid}`), where the oversize material came from the request body, and `POST …/credentials/{cid}/mcp_oauth_validate`, where it came from the token endpoint's refresh response rather than from the caller at all — a 4xx is an imperfect fit there and is used anyway, so its message says where the material came from instead of implying the request carried it (`resealFailed`'s comment holds that argument). No other request path reaches the ceiling: the only other seal on one, a `github_repository` resource's `authorization_token`, is capped by the API at 8 KiB first. So this is a property of one cipher rather than of the wire — the API bounds the length of no credential secret field and admits a 4 MiB body (`maxBodyBytes`) — and the 4xx is deliberate: an unclassified `Cipher.Encrypt` failure renders as a generic 500 whose useful text reaches only the server's log, so the backend returns `secrets.ErrPlaintextTooLarge` and the vault-credential handler classifies it into the `errInvalid` family. Envelope encryption would remove the ceiling and is deferred. *Evidence: internal/secrets/gcpkms/gcpkms.go — its package doc and the MaxPlaintextBytes/MaxPlaintextBytesHSM comments carry the measured ceilings and the fail-closed protection-level derivation this entry no longer restates; internal/secrets/secrets.go (ErrPlaintextTooLarge); internal/api/vaultcredentials.go (sealFailed, resealFailed); docs/plan/20_gcp-deployment.md Decision 3 and its 2026-08-01 ground-truth probes; gcpkms TestTheCeilingIsExactAndClassified / TestAnHSMKeyGetsTheSmallerCeiling / TestEveryProtectionLevelResolvesItsCeiling, api TestOversizeCredentialSecretIsRefusedWithAFourHundred / TestAnOversizeRefreshedTokenIsRefusedWithAFourHundred* *Tracked: #242.*

- **The deliverables harvest on `self_hosted` environments — never, on either trigger (plan 21 slice 4, plan 38)** — A cloud session snapshots `/mnt/session/outputs/` into the files registry twice over: when a grading cycle begins, and whenever the session folds idle. A self_hosted session does neither — its grading settlement schedules the grading turn directly and the grader judges from the transcript (plus any rubric) alone, and its idle folds schedule nothing at all, so `GET /v1/files?scope_id=` never holds an output for the life of the session — only the copies its file mounts mint (#578). The exclusion lives at all six enqueue sites (the grading branch and the five idle folds), never in the queue's claim predicate. Deliberate: the sandbox lives on customer compute the platform cannot reach, the wire work API serves only `tool_exec` (a harvest kind would be a new wire surface the reference does not have), and shipping deliverable bytes back over the worker wire is a data-boundary decision the customer has not made. The reference is cloud-only on this axis, so there is no reference behavior to diverge from — the divergence is that the *same platform* grades the two environment kinds from different evidence. A future opt-in worker-side harvest could close the gap. *Evidence: internal/brain/grader.go (settleEndTurn's environment-kind routing, and enqueueIdleHarvest's identical guard shared by the five idle folds); internal/queue/queue.go (Poll's tool_exec-only scope); docs/plan/21_outcomes.md Decision 8; docs/plan/38_idle-outputs-harvest.md decisions 4 and 6; internal/brain/grader_test.go TestEndTurnOnSelfHostedNeverHarvests, internal/brain/brain_test.go TestPermissionAskIdleEnqueuesOutputsHarvest; BetaSelfHostedWork is session-scoped work carrying a state enum, with no per-tool or outputs_harvest work kind, so no harvest is ever routed to a BYOC worker (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSelfHostedWork and BetaSelfHostedWorkState)* *Tracked: #77 (delivered).*

- **`github_repository` resources on `self_hosted` environments — nothing is materialized, and the agent is not told otherwise (plan 25 slice 2)** — A repository attached to a cloud session is cloned into its sandbox by the executor; the same resource on a self_hosted session is materialized by nothing, and the brain omits the "Mounted repositories" block there rather than describing a checkout that does not exist. This half is **CONFIRMED against the reference**, though not by a sentence in the docs — the public GitHub guide describes mounting a repository "to your session sandbox" and never mentions environment kinds at all. It is confirmed by what the reference *ships*: the only component that can write into a customer-hosted sandbox is the customer-run worker, and the reference's worker is a container ENTRYPOINT that executes `agent.tool_use` events and contains no repository, git, or clone handling whatsoever; nor is it given anything to clone with, since a self-hosted work item's data is `{id, type:"session"}` — it names a session and carries no materialization payload and no credential. A repository therefore cannot be materialized on self_hosted by the reference either, and the platform's silence matches. What is *ours* is the shape of the gap on a self-hosting deployment: the resource is accepted, sealed, listed, and rotatable on a self_hosted session, and simply never appears in the sandbox, with no event saying so. Deliberate for v1 rather than an oversight — a BYOC clone means shipping the customer's token to customer compute, or teaching the worker wire a materialization kind, and neither is a decision this plan makes. The brain gate is what keeps the omission honest: a block naming `/workspace/repo` to a model whose sandbox has no such path is worse than no block at all. *Evidence: docs/plan/25_git-repo-mounting.md decisions 8-9 (whose "CONFIRMED-by-docs" shorthand is narrowed here — the guide is silent, the shipped client is not); platform.claude.com managed-agents/github (fetched 2026-08-07 — no mention of environment kinds); BetaSessionWorkData = `{id, type:"session"}` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSessionWorkData); anthropic-cli's worker command ("Attach to a session and execute agent.tool_use events locally. Intended as a container ENTRYPOINT." — no repository handling anywhere in pkg/cmd/worker.go; checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — worker.go workerRunCommand); internal/brain/repos.go (resolveReposBlock's environment-kind gate); internal/executor/repos.go (materializeRepos, reached only by the platform-managed executor)* *Tracked: #322 (the session half — a repository accepted, sealed and rotatable on a self_hosted session that nothing ever materializes).*

- **Session update (`POST /v1/sessions/{id}`) — the documented idle requirement is not enforced** — The reference docs state a session "must be in an `idle` state" to be updated; our update handler applies the change regardless of status — a `running` or `rescheduling` session's title/metadata update succeeds where the reference documents a refusal. A known pre-existing gap, registered when plan 24 slice 1 added the running-session archive/delete guard next door: the update predicate is *stricter* than running-only (idle-only would also refuse `rescheduling`, `terminated`…), so it is not the same guard and needs its own decision — enforce idle-only, running-only, or leave open — plus the refusal's unobserved wire shape. Registered, not fixed, in that slice. Unlike its neighbors this entry is **not an accepted design**: it sits here so the wire-compat rung sees the mismatch flagged rather than silently absent, and #312 owns the decision — a reviewer must not suppress an update-while-running finding on this entry's authority. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/session-operations ("Updating session properties", fetched 2026-08-05); internal/api/sessions.go (updateSession — no status check)* *Tracked: #312.*

- **Human authentication (`/v1` management paths and the `/api/` console namespace) — the deployment IdP's JWTs are accepted as `Authorization: Bearer`, and no `POST /v1/oauth/token` is served (plan 31 slice 2)** — The reference API host serves `POST /v1/oauth/token` (authorization_code + refresh_token + jwt-bearer) and its console authenticates humans with the tokens that endpoint mints; this platform serves no such endpoint, so `ant auth login`, OAuth profiles, and OIDC federation against it are unsupported. In their place, a deployment that configures `IDENTITY_MODE` accepts a JWT issued by **its own** IdP — verified against that issuer's JWKS, never minted here — on the same management paths. Neither surface is on the wire any documented CLI or SDK flow uses: management rides `x-api-key` and workers ride environment keys, and both are unchanged, byte for byte, whether identity is configured or not — with two deliberate exceptions in every mode: a *repeated* `x-api-key` field, which HTTP permits and no documented client sends, is now refused as ambiguous rather than resolved by header order; and an environment key offered as a Bearer on a management route draws the reference's own refusal, a revoked key's sentence or a live key's recorded answer, when no human credential rides beside it (#840; *A revoked environment key on a management route*). The `x-api-key` lane wins outright when a key is present, so a Bearer can never vouch for a management call. With `IDENTITY_MODE` unset or `disabled` — the default — no JWT is accepted anywhere and a Bearer on a management path draws the same missing-key 401 it always has, an environment key's own answers aside. *Evidence: `ant auth login` and its two token grants (checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — cmd_auth.go authLogin and exchangeCode and refreshAccessToken), the reference's token endpoint and grant types (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — federation.go TokenEndpoint and GrantTypeJWTBearer and GrantTypeRefreshToken); internal/api/server.go (dispatchManagementAuth: x-api-key first, human second, an environment key's answer third, today's 401 when none), internal/identity/verifier.go (Verifier.Credential, Verify); internal/api/identitylane_test.go (TestIdentityDisabledIsUnchanged, TestManagementKeyWinsOverAHumanCredential); docs/plan/31_console-sso-rbac.md* *Landed for #56's SSO/RBAC half (plan 31 slice 2).*
- **Console roles — three (`admin`/`developer`/`viewer`), bound to IdP claims rather than a platform user store (plan 31 slice 2)** — The reference console has seven roles over its own account/organization membership. This platform has three, ordered `viewer < developer < admin`, and holds **no** authority of its own: a request's role is computed per request from the claim the deployment's IdP asserts, through the operator's `IDENTITY_ROLE_MAP`. The `principals` table records who has signed in (issuer, subject, email, display name) for audit and for `created_by`, and deliberately carries no role column — a stored copy would be a second, staler answer to a question the IdP already answers, and revoking a group at the IdP must take effect on the next request rather than after an operator remembers to mirror it here. A token that verifies but maps to no role is authenticated with no authority and is refused by every role-gated route. *Evidence: internal/identity/identity.go (Role, roleRank, AtLeast), internal/identity/claims.go (strongestRole), internal/store/migrations/0022_principals.sql (the no-role-column argument), internal/api/identitylane.go (requireRole); internal/api/identitylane_test.go (the role-column assertion, the unmapped-role 403); docs/plan/31_console-sso-rbac.md* *Landed for #56's SSO/RBAC half (plan 31 slice 2).*
- **`IDENTITY_MODE=trusted_proxy` — a mode with no reference analog (plan 31 slice 2)** — The reference is a hosted service and is never deployed behind a customer's identity-aware proxy; this platform is meant to be, so it accepts a proxy's signed assertion header (the shipped `gcp-iap` preset reads `x-goog-iap-jwt-assertion` against Google's global key set) as the human credential. The assertion is verified exactly as an OIDC token is — same allowlist, same issuer/audience/expiry discipline — never trusted because it arrived from a proxy. In this mode `Authorization: Bearer` is **never** read as a human credential, so a caller cannot present a token the proxy never vouched for; Bearer stays what it already was, the worker's environment key. The rule is closed from the configuration end too: naming `Authorization` or `x-api-key` as the assertion header is a **startup** error, since either would invert the rule from the other side — the API layer would read a raw `Authorization` value as an assertion and hand a worker's environment key to the verifier. A *repeated* assertion header is no credential at all, as a repeated `x-api-key` is refused as ambiguous: which copy the proxy set cannot be told from the request. *Evidence: internal/identity/preset.go (gcpIAPPreset), internal/identity/config.go (configureGCPIAP, configureCustomProxy), internal/identity/verifier.go (Verifier.Credential's trusted_proxy branch); internal/api/identitylane_test.go (TestTrustedProxyModeReadsOnlyItsHeader); internal/identity/verifier_test.go (TestCredential); docs/plan/31_console-sso-rbac.md* *Landed for #56's SSO/RBAC half (plan 31 slice 2).*

- **No `/v1/organizations/api_keys` — creation withheld as the reference withholds it, read/update omitted as Admin-API surface (plan 32 slice 2)** — Management keys are issued, listed and updated only on the console namespace: issued and listed at `/api/console/organizations/{org}/workspaces/{workspace}/api_keys`, and updated at `POST /api/console/organizations/{org}/api_keys/{id}` — the reference's own update route, with no workspace segment, answering every other method 405 as it does (2026-09-05 batch5 `rec86.keys.update.*`, `rec86.keys.method.*`) — or at `…/workspaces/{workspace}/api_keys/{id}`, the path served first and kept as an alias the console still drives. Two different absences are folded into one line here, and they have different characters. **Creation** has no public endpoint on the reference either, deliberately and for the stated reason — its Admin API FAQ reads "new API keys can only be created through the Claude Console for security reasons", and its API reference lists only Retrieve, List and Update — so withholding it is *mirroring*, the same move plan 30 made for environment-key issuance. **Read and update** do exist publicly, as `GET /v1/organizations/api_keys` and `POST /v1/organizations/api_keys/{id}`, and we do not serve them: they belong to the Console Admin API, authenticated by an `sk-ant-admin…` credential class this platform has no analog of, and outside the Managed Agents wire-compat target this project scopes itself to. A consequence worth stating plainly, because it is the shape a client would notice: the same resource has three collection shapes on the reference — the console's **bare array**, the Admin API's `{data, first_id, has_more, last_id}`, and this platform's own `{data, next_page}` wire envelope — and we implement only the first, on the only surface that carries it. One console collection we do not serve: the reference also lists and issues at `…/organizations/{org}/api_keys`, with no workspace segment (batch5 idx 0), where its create needs a `workspace_id` or a `principal_id` in the body (`rec86.create.name.whitespace` names the requirement); we serve the workspace-segment listing and create it also serves (batch5 `rec86.keys.list.workspace.spelling`, batch9 `setup.keyC.create`), whose workspace is the path's. *Evidence: the Admin API FAQ and reference index; the live recording of 2026-08-13 in [#378](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/378), where `GET …/api_keys` returned a bare array; managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch5 idx 0–12; internal/api/server.go (the console routes); TestAPIKeyUpdatesOnTheRecordedRoute, TestAPIKeyErrorsCarryTheRecordedDetails.* *Landed for #820 (the update route without its workspace segment).*
- **Client-supplied `expires_at` on management keys, against a server-fixed TTL on environment keys (plan 32 slice 2)** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (#389).** The one mismatch this entry records is a refusal this platform has since lifted. `POST …/api_keys` takes an optional absolute ISO-8601 instant and stores it verbatim; **absent means never**, which is what the reference's own console sends for its *Never* choice — it omits the field rather than nulling it, and the response then reports `expires_at: null`. There is no duration vocabulary on the wire at all: the dialog's "3 hours / 30 days / Custom *N* units" is client-side sugar resolved to an instant before sending. This is a deliberate asymmetry with plan 30's environment keys, whose lifetime is the platform's fixed `EnvironmentKeyTTL` and not the caller's to choose, and it is the reference's own asymmetry: an operator issuing a management credential picks its lifetime, while a worker credential's lifetime is infrastructure. An instant already in the past is **accepted**, and mints a key already reporting `expired`: this platform refused it until [#389](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/389) probed the reference, which answers 200 to an `expires_at` of 2020. The refusal had been the safer-looking guess — a validation message beats debugging an authentication failure — and it lost to the rule that governs here. **The `name` rule is the reference's, recorded** (2026-09-05 batch5 `rec86.create.name.*`): 1–500 characters, counted as characters, and taken as sent — nothing is trimmed, so a whitespace-only name is accepted (`rec86.create.name.whitespace`, `.tab-nl`) and stored and echoed as sent (2026-09-24 `api-key`, a key named three spaces) — while 501 and 1,000 characters are refused with the recorded message, "name: String should have at most 500 characters". It was the environment-key surface's 1–128 after trimming, a local pick made while this bound was unprobed; that surface keeps it (*The console-API dialect's edges*). A rename is held to the same rule, which no recording shows (*`/api/console/…` — `principal_id` on key create, a rename's name rule, and the workspace read*). *Evidence: the `Never` capture and the `{"name":…,"expires_at":…}` capture, both in [#378](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/378); managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch5 idx 13–23 and 2026-09-24-api-key; `apiKeyName` in `internal/api/consoleapikeys.go`; TestAPIKeyNameIsTheRecordedBound.* *Landed for #389 (the expiry) and #820 (the name rule).*
- **`can_manage` omitted from the listing while the surface stays admin-only (plan 32 slice 2)** — The reference's console rows carry a `can_manage` boolean the created resource does not: a **per-row authorization hint**, the server telling the console whether *this viewer* may act on *this row*, rather than the console deriving it from a role. Every row in the recording returned `true`, because that account's user is an admin; its falsy case is unobserved. We omit the field. These routes are gated whole at `admin` (plan 31's role mechanism), so the value would be a constant `true` — a column reserved for a permission split this platform has not built, and one whose only honest value today carries no information. It is worth keeping in view rather than merely dropping: it is the reference's answer to "how does a console grey out the buttons it must not offer", which is a question our own RBAC will eventually have to answer too. *Evidence: the row-shape capture in [#378](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/378).* *Tracked: #78 (a recording made from a non-admin console account would settle `can_manage`'s falsy case, which an admin account cannot show).*
- **`sk-map-api01-` as the issued-key prefix, and `principal`/`api_key` actors where the reference says `user` (plan 32 slice 2)** — Issued management keys read `sk-map-api01-` plus 43 base64url characters, beside plan 30's `sk-map-env01-` and against the reference's `sk-ant-api03-`. The silhouette is modeled on it; the middle is deliberately ours, for the reason plan 30 gives: a credential that reads like an Anthropic key invites being pasted into `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and nothing parses the prefix — to every consumer the key is an opaque `x-api-key` value. The resource's actor fields diverge for a harder reason: the reference renders `created_by` and `principal` as `{"id": "user_…", "type": "user"}`, and this platform has no `user_` id to give, because sessions and credentials here are deliberately not bound to an end user (design principle 5). `created_by` therefore renders `{"id": "principal_…", "type": "principal"}` for a human who issued the key over SSO, `{"id": "apikey_…", "type": "api_key"}` for a machine credential that did, and `null` for the key seeded from `CONTROLPLANE_API_KEY` — which nobody issued. `workspace_id` and `principal` are emitted `null`, which is mirroring rather than reserving-by-guess: both came back null on the reference's own single-tenant account, so [#56](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/56) can populate them without a shape change. *Evidence: the created-resource capture in [#378](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/378); `internal/api/consoleapikeys.go`.*
- **A key seeded from `CONTROLPLANE_API_KEY` is listed but not console-mutable (plan 32 slice 2)** — The console lists every management key, including the one the environment variable seeds — hiding the credential the caller is authenticating with would misdescribe what can reach this API — but `POST …/api_keys/{id}` refuses to change its status or its name, naming the variable in the 400. The row `BRAIN_API_KEY` seeds under the name `brain` (plan 59 slice 5) is held the same way, and its 400 names that variable. The reference has no analog to diverge from: it has no env-var-managed credential class. The rule is ours and it is structural. That row's lifecycle already has an owner, rotation-by-restart, so a console disable would be silently undone by the next boot; and renaming it would break that rotation outright, since `EnsureAPIKey` archives the incumbent **by name** — a bootstrap row renamed out from under it would survive the next rotation and leave two live credentials under one name, which is precisely the race `api_keys_one_live_unissued` exists to prevent. *Evidence: `internal/api/consoleapikeys.go`'s `updateAPIKey`; `TestAPIKeyUpdateGuardsTheEnumAndTheEnvManagedRow`, whose negative control observes the control plane's own key 401 once the guard is removed.*

- **The lifecycle refusals — the reference's behaviour and, since #540, its words (plan 32 slice 2, measured by [#389](https://github.com/OpenSDLC-Dev/managed-agent-platform/issues/389))** — **Converged, no longer a divergence (#540).** The two state guards on `POST …/api_keys/{id}` matched the reference's *behaviour* exactly once #389 measured it, and this entry kept their *wording* ours on purpose: `"api key <id> is archived, and an archived key cannot be updated"` and `"api key <id> has expired; an expired key can only be archived, not renamed or re-activated"`, for naming which key of a batch was refused, and for saying "archived" where the reference's "deleted" is a console verb for a state this API spells `archived` and serves no DELETE for. The owner's decision on #540 — the reference's words wherever its body was observed — reverses that, so the guards now answer `"Archived API keys cannot be updated."` and `"Expired API keys can only be deleted, not renamed or reactivated."`, the strings the probe measured. The envelope and the HTTP status were already mirrored (`400`, `invalid_request_error`). What survives is the reading of "deleted": the expired-key sentence names an operation this API spells `status: archived`, as the code comment beside it says. *Evidence: internal/api/consoleapikeys.go updateAPIKeyIn (the two guards); the measured strings in [docs/HISTORY.md](HISTORY.md)'s #389 probe record; internal/api/consoleapikeys_test.go TestAPIKeyExpiryIsClientSuppliedAndDerived and TestArchivingAnAPIKeyIsPermanent.* *Landed for #540.*
- **The dial-address floor — what a customer-URL connection may reach (plan 29 slice 2)** — Five production paths resolve a host a customer or an agent named, and all five go through `internal/dialguard` (plan 44): the vault-credential validate probe, the MCP client's `DefaultClient` (which the executor's discovery driver selects by supplying no client of its own), the executor's dial-time token-endpoint refresh, which redials long after the probe last vetted the address, the JWKS fetch, and the per-session egress gate. The guard refuses loopback, link-local (cloud metadata), the unspecified address and multicast on the **resolved** IP of every dial, and re-checks the embedded IPv4 target inside six specification-fixed IPv6 transition forms; an address it cannot read at all is refused rather than admitted, so the natural `IPAllowed(net.ParseIP(host))` call shape cannot fail open. **None of this is reference behavior**: the reference documents reachability but no per-address policy, and a refusal on its side would be indistinguishable from an ordinary `mcp_connection_failed_error` — so both the list and its consequence are ours. **RFC 1918 is deliberately allowed**, the reading most likely to look like an omission: this platform is self-hosted by premise, so an MCP server on the operator's own private network is the normal case, and this guard is the floor beneath an agent's egress policy rather than the policy itself. One neighbouring dial is deliberately *not* behind it, and reading the guard as universal misplaces it: the `github_repository` clone is pinned to the literal `github.com` by a create-time grammar rather than an address check. Inside the gate the floor is per admission class rather than per dial, and after plan 45 exactly one class is exempt — a host in the operator's own `allowed_hosts`, where naming a private address *is* the vouching. `unrestricted` is no longer exempt (#570), which is the reference's own answer; what remains outside the floor entirely is a session provisioned no gate at all (#620). The decoding is hardening on the one prefix an attacker can count on being routed, not a general answer to NAT64: a Network-Specific Prefix carries nothing that marks it as one, so a deployment translating through its own space gets no decoding, and the over-refusal this costs is real, ranges from 0% to total depending on the deployment's prefix bytes rather than only its length, and is fail-closed by choice. *Evidence: internal/dialguard/dialguard.go, whose comments carry the whole derivation this entry used to duplicate — which six forms are decoded and why, the RFC 6052 six-candidate read and the padding skip that makes it usable, the RFC 8215 §5 sentence that argues against the decoding and why a guard deciding a refusal is not a node forwarding a packet, the measured over-refusal figures, and the RFC 2529 6over4 omission taken by decision rather than oversight; internal/dialguard/dialguard_test.go pins each class and form; internal/api/vaultvalidate.go (the overridable probe seam), internal/mcp/mcp.go (DefaultClient), internal/executor/mcpwork.go (the driver that selects it).* *Landed for #45.*
- **The session delegation bound — what a session may spend when nobody is asking (#447)** — **OURS, and not an implementation of anything: the reference publishes no bound of any kind on autonomous agent-to-agent activity.** A session counts, in `sessions.delegation_turns`, every turn that called at least one delegation tool its own role was actually offered (a call to the other half's tool, or to a name the agent was never offered, is answered without counting — #567), and a claim is refused once that count reaches **625** (`maxLiveThreads × maxSettlementChain` — what every thread the session may hold could each spend under the thread-local chain cap; a 24-child coordinator over ten rounds is roughly 480 counted turns, so a rounder-looking figure would cut legitimate work). Only a demand from outside resets it — `user.message`, `user.define_outcome`, `user.tool_confirmation`, `system.message` (`domain.EventType.StartsNewWork`, deliberately narrower than `Inbound`). A tool result does **not**: on a `self_hosted` environment every tool call arrives as a client POST, so resetting there would zero the counter continuously and the bound would not exist. Nor does `user.interrupt` — a stop is not a demand, and counting it would let an operator's own stop hand the session a fresh budget. Counting is not restricted to settlement-only turns, or a model pairing one trivial tool call with each message would never move it. The refusal is at the **claim**, not the settlement: it never intervenes in a turn's fate, so it cannot idle a thread whose `tool_exec` is still in flight, and it needs no gate on `send_to_agent`, whose pinned `Message sent.` therefore stays true — the peer really is woken, and its own next claim is what is refused. A refused turn issues **no model request**: the thread idles `end_turn` carrying a `session.error` of the platform-coined type `session_delegation_exhausted_error` with `retry_status: exhausted`, a refused child delivers its coordinator the ending notice every other ending owes it, and overshoot is bounded at one model request per thread already in flight. **Why not the reference's `budget`**: it is a monetary ceiling (`max_list_cost`, a `BetaMonetaryAmount` in minor units of an ISO-4217 currency, under a discriminator whose only value is `"limit"`), and its documented recovery is to **raise the budget** — a remedy that presupposes a price to raise, where this bound's remedy is a client message. Implementing it needs a per-model list-price table that a gateway or self-hosted model cannot have (design principle 4), so a budget clone degrades to no bound at all for the deployments this platform exists for. This is therefore **not** a partial implementation of #432: `budget` still renders `null`, `session.usage` is still never emitted, `budget_reached` is still never written, and `domain.StopReasonType` is untouched. What it does **not** bound, so that nobody reads it as a runaway guard: a single agent looping `bash` forever off one client message. A pair emitting one real tool call alongside each message is bounded on a `cloud` environment but **not** on a `self_hosted` one, and the difference is which event answers the call — an executor posts `agent.tool_result`, which is stamped at write and is not pending input, so the refusal fires; a `self_hosted` client POSTs `user.tool_result`, which is, so the refusal declines rather than strand the thread. A client-executed custom tool takes the `self_hosted` lane on either kind. The cut is session-wide, so sibling threads doing real work stop beside the looping pair; and the counter is a projection no fold rebuilds (the `sessions.usage` precedent), trustworthy because exactly two statements write it and both hold the session row lock. *Evidence: `budget` is "A hard spend ceiling" on "tracked list cost" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsBudgetLimit), "Raise the budget to continue" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsSessionBudgetReached), the idle stop-reason union closed at four values, none an activity-exhaustion state (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsSessionStatusIdleEventStopReasonUnion.Type and BetaManagedAgentsSessionThreadStatusIdleEventStopReasonUnion.Type); platform.claude.com managed-agents multiagent-orchestration (structural limits only — roster 1–20, depth 1, 25 concurrent threads — and the roster entry having "no `max_uses`, `max_tokens`, or `caching` fields", a mechanism deliberately removed); internal/store/migrations/0027_session_delegation_turns.sql; internal/domain/event.go (StartsNewWork); internal/events/log.go (the reset inside AppendInTx); internal/brain/delegate.go (maxSessionDelegationTurns, runExhausted, cutExhaustedRun); TestEventStartsNewWork, TestSessionDelegationBudgetIsDerived, TestADelegatedTurnSpendsFromTheSessionBudget, TestAMixedDelegationAndToolTurnSpendsToo, TestAChildCallingTheCoordinatorsToolAlongsideBashNeverSpendsTheSessionBudget, TestAChildCallingTheCoordinatorsToolAloneHitsTheChainCapNeverTheSessionBudget, TestAClientMessageReturnsTheDelegationBudget, TestAToolResultDoesNotReturnTheDelegationBudget, TestTheClaimIsRefusedAtTheDelegationBound, TestAPeerWakeClearsTheChainCountButNotTheSessionBudget, TestPendingInputRunsTheTurnInsteadOfRefusing, TestARefusedChildTellsItsCoordinatorAndTheCascadeStops* *Landed for #447.*
- **Memory-version `created_by` / `redacted_by` — `service_account_actor` is never emitted (plan 36 slice 0; the rows land with slice 2)** — The reference's actor union is a closed set of four: `session_actor {session_id}` for an agent's write through the mounted filesystem, `api_actor {api_key_id}` for a write outside any session, `user_actor {user_id}` for the Anthropic Console, and — added inside this bump's range (since anthropic-sdk-go v1.64.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaManagedAgentsServiceAccountActor) — `service_account_actor {service_account_id}`, "a write made by a workload authenticated as a service account, for example via Workload Identity Federation". This platform has no service-account identity to name: `internal/identity` maps OIDC or trusted-proxy claims to three console roles, and no `svac_`-shaped id exists anywhere in it.
