Paging is offset / `total` / `has_more`, the recorded listing envelope rather than this platform's wire-side keyset `next_page` cursor; offset paging can repeat or skip a row across concurrent writes where a keyset cursor cannot, but an operator's per-host key list is small enough that it never surfaces, and matching the dialect wins. **Ours:** the literal `default` organization (*`/api/oauth/…` and `/api/console/…` — `default` is the organization segment we answer for*). **Inferred:** how many rows a page above 100 serves — the recording held one key, so the echo is all it shows; here a page serves up to the `limit` asked for. That the organization gate answers `/api/console/…` as it answers `/api/oauth/…` — one function serves both, and no organization probe was recorded on the former. And this dialect's `name` bound is still unprobed: ours is 1–128 characters after trimming, with a whitespace-only name rejected — a local pick sized to a hostname-or-phrase label. The management-key dialect's recorded 1–500, untrimmed, is not evidence about this one. *Evidence: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch2 `rec83.*`; internal/api/consoleapi.go (consoleOrganization, createEnvironmentKey, parseOffsetPage, environmentKeyName); internal/api/consoleapi_test.go (TestConsoleKeyIssueRejectsBadRequests, TestConsoleKeyIssuesOnCloudAndArchivedEnvironments, TestConsoleKeyListPagesAndRendersNullExpiry, TestConsoleKeyListTakesALimitAbove100, TestConsoleKeyRevokeRejectsIdsItDoesNotOwn, TestConsoleKeyRoutesRejectOtherOrganizations, TestConsoleKeyErrorsCarryTheRecordedDetails); internal/api/consoleapikeys_test.go TestAPIKeyRoutesRejectUnknownScopesAndIDs; docs/plan/30_environment-keys-console-issuance.md § "The console-API surface"* *Tracked: #78 (a listing past 100 keys would settle the page a larger `limit` serves, an organization probe on `/api/console/…` the gate there, and a probe of this dialect's own `name` bound that bound); landed for #820.*
- **A `cloud` or archived environment's key — what it reaches (#820)** — The console issues a key on any environment, as the reference does (*The console-API dialect's edges*), so a `cloud` environment's key is now routine, and that environment's work is the platform executor's, run in-process: the key has no worker to serve. **Matched:** on the work API its poll is a 400 `invalid_request_error` and its work listing a 404 `not_found_error`, both without details, and its stats a 200 whose counts are zero and whose `workers_polling` is null, archived or not — a null the pinned SDK's type does not admit, since it declares the field a required integer (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSelfHostedWorkQueueStats.WorkersPolling), so a typed client reads it as 0 — the reference's answers on its `cloud` environments (2026-09-05 batch2 `rec83.active-key.work.*.beta`, idx 37–39, and `rec83.archived-key.work.*.beta`, idx 33–35; batch8 `item2.workpoll.envkey.cloud-env-rejected`, idx 24), and since #540 in its words: the poll's "Only BYOC and bridge environments support work polling. Environment env_… is anthropic_cloud." and the listing's "Environment `env_…` not found, or does not support work listing. …". One recorded listing answered otherwise — the archived environment's, a 404 naming the archive (idx 33) — and that sentence is not ours to give: the listing consults no archival, so an archived cloud environment's key draws the kind sentence. The refusal sits in the handler, ahead of the poll's bookkeeping and its reservation, on the kind the key's own lookup read (`authenticateEnvironmentKey` joins it), above a queue whose poll serves `self_hosted` items alone (`queue.Poll`), so a cloud item stays queued for the executor's claim. The skill reads are served, as recorded: the reference served such a key a skill version's `/content` for three skills (batch2 `rec83.orphan.content.after-env-delete`, `.after-env-delete.different-skill` and `rec83.orphan.content.recheck.t2`, idx 57, 59 and 62, on idx 52's `cloud` environment), skills being workspace-global. Those reads came after that environment was deleted, which has no counterpart here: a key goes with its environment, and authentication joins the two (*A deleted environment's key*). **Inferred**, because no recording reaches it: (1) the item routes — get, update, ack, heartbeat, stop — answer a cloud environment's item with the work-item 404 that every item outside the work API's scope gets. (2) Archival is not consulted on a `self_hosted` environment: its key's poll, listing and stats, and its session, file and skill reads, are served as before, so a worker drains what the archive left queued. The reference's own refusal to delete such an environment says to archive it first "to allow the queue to drain" (*DELETE /v1/environments/{id} — the self-hosted queue refusal and force=true*), but its poll and listing were recorded on archived `cloud` environments only, and there the listing answered a 404 naming the archive (idx 33) that an archived `self_hosted` one may answer too. (3) The session lane and the file content download refuse a `cloud` key with one 404 `not_found_error` and no details — the status the work listing answers it — before any session or file is looked up (the download in the reference's "Not found", recorded for such a key's download, 2026-09-03 batch2 `envkey.files.content-original`, #540; the session lane in ours): the session read, its event list and stream, every posted event (a `user.tool_confirmation` the executor would act on, a `user.custom_tool_result` and a `user.define_outcome` included), and the download. What the reference admits a `cloud` key to on a session is unrecorded; its file content route refuses every environment key (*GET /v1/files/{id}/content — the environment-key content lane, wider than the reference's*). *Evidence: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch2 idx 33–39 and 52–62, batch8 idx 24; internal/api/envauth.go authenticateEnvironmentKey, requireEnvironmentKeyForSession, requireSelfHostedEnvironmentKey and errNotSelfHostedKey; internal/api/workapi.go pollWork, listWork and statsWork; internal/queue/queue.go PollOn; internal/queue/lifecycle.go workAPIScope; TestACloudEnvironmentKeyCannotTakeItsWork, TestAnArchivedSelfHostedEnvironmentStillDrains, TestACloudEnvironmentKeyReachesOnlyWhatTheReferenceServesIt (which requests every route server.go registers with both kinds of key), TestACloudEnvironmentKeyCannotAnswerItsSessions, TestAnArchivedSelfHostedEnvironmentKeyKeepsItsLanes.* *Tracked: #78 (an item route driven with a cloud key, a poll and a listing on an archived `self_hosted` environment, and a cloud key on its session's events would settle all three); landed for #820.*
- **A revoked environment key on a management route — the reference's revoked sentence, not the missing-key 401 (#840)** — The management lane looks up a Bearer carrying the `sk-map-env01-` prefix every minted key carries, when neither a management key nor a human credential rides beside it, and a revoked key there answers 401 `authentication_error` "OAuth access token has been revoked." on every route the lane serves, the /v1 management routes and the console namespace alike, as the key lanes answer it (*Environment key revocation and expiry*). Outside every key lane the reference was recorded giving that answer only on `GET /v1/models` (2026-09-19 custom-mixed-tools, custom-order-followup and self-hosted-latest-cli `worker-network.json` idx 170, 52 and 61), a Messages API route this platform does not serve and answers through the management lane, so there it is matched as recorded. On the managed-agents management routes it is inferred from where the reference gives it. The body is the same on three route families — the work poll, a skill version's content and `/v1/models` — and on all three it carries `"request_id": null` and no `request-id` header, where every other refusal recorded on those routes (27 of them) carries an id: an answer given before routing, by something that never looks at the route. This platform's 401 keeps its request id, as on the key lanes. The inference reaches revocation alone. A live key is answered per route, the recorded refusal where one was recorded and the missing-key 401 elsewhere (*Skill reads on the environment-key lane*). An expired or unknown key, a grandfathered pre-0021 key (its operator-chosen value carries no prefix, so the lane never looks it up, which keeps every other Bearer off the database), and any other Bearer keep the missing-key 401, so nothing about SSO is revealed. A failed lookup is that same 401, logged at Warn, since nothing authenticated the request. A human credential beside the key is served as the human: the key is only ever refused here, and a stale worker Bearer must not refuse someone a trusted proxy vouched for. That human-first order is the management lane's alone: on the routes an environment key reaches, `dualAuth` still gives a worker's Bearer the environment lane ahead of a trusted proxy's assertion, the key being the credential those routes admit. The claim is every **public** route: the internal gate-config endpoint is off the wire, is no reference route, and takes a gate token and nothing else, so it answers any other credential, a revoked environment key included, as an invalid gate token. *Evidence: internal/api/envauth.go (answerEnvironmentKey, lookupEnvironmentKey, errRevokedEnvironmentKey), internal/api/server.go dispatchManagementAuth; internal/api/envkeyrefusals_test.go TestARevokedEnvironmentKeyIsAnsweredAsRevokedOnEveryPublicRoute, TestTheManagementLaneLeavesEveryOtherBearerAlone, TestTheManagementLaneLooksUpOnlyAnEnvironmentKeyShapedBearer and TestAHumanBesideAnEnvironmentKeyIsServedAsTheHuman, internal/api/envauth_internal_test.go TestTheManagementLaneFailsClosedWhenTheKeyLookupFails.* *Tracked: #78 (a revoked environment key on a managed-agents management route, `GET /v1/agents` say, would confirm or correct the sentence there); landed for #840.*
- **`/api/console/…` — `principal_id` on key create, a rename's name rule, and the workspace read (#820)** — Three answers rest on less than a recording. **`principal_id`.** Its shape is recorded: a value that is not a `user_…` or `svac_…` id is a 400 `invalid_request_error` with `{error_visibility: user_facing}` (2026-09-05 batch5 `rec86.create.principal_id.bogus-string`), and we answer so, once the name and expiry have been judged. That order is inferred, not recorded: the 501-character name's refusal (batch5 `rec86.create.name.501`, idx 21) came from a body holding the name alone, and no recording pairs a bad name with a bad `principal_id`. Its message is pydantic's and carries no details, while the `principal_id` refusal carries them, so the first reads as the reference's schema validation and the second as its handler, which would run after. That a bare `user_` or `svac_`, or one followed by unstorable bytes, is malformed too is *Console namespace ids — what makes one malformed beyond the recorded inputs*. What a well-formed id does — link the key to that user or service account, the reference's "identity-linked key" — is unrecorded, and needs an identity this platform does not have: its humans are `principal_` rows, which the recorded shape refuses, and it has no service accounts. So a well-formed id names nothing here and is the namespace's 404 `not_found_error` with `{error_visibility: user_facing}`; nothing is linked and nothing is minted. A non-string is a 400 without details, and an explicit null is no principal at all. **A rename** is held to the create's recorded name rule (*Client-supplied `expires_at` on management keys, against a server-fixed TTL on environment keys*); no recording renames a key. **`GET …/workspaces/{id}`** answers 404 with `{error_visibility: user_facing}` for every id, `default` included, and renders no workspace. The one recorded read is that 404, for an archived workspace (batch8 `item6.after-archive.workspaceB.get`); the recorded workspace object is a `wrkspc_` row whose display colour, data residency and compartment id have no value here; and the reference's own Default workspace is no row of its workspace listing (batch9 `item5.workspaces.list.include_archived`), so what it answers a read of `default` with is unrecorded too. *Evidence: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch5 idx 21 and 26, batch8 idx 31–34, batch9 idx 0–4; internal/api/consoleapikeys.go createAPIKey, apiKeyPrincipal, apiKeyName and getWorkspace; TestAPIKeyPrincipalIDIsJudgedAsRecorded, TestAPIKeyNameIsTheRecordedBound, TestConsoleWorkspaceReadIsTheRecordedNotFound.* *Tracked: #78 (a create naming a real user or service account, one pairing a bad name with a bad `principal_id`, a rename, and a read of the Default workspace would settle all three); landed for #820.*
- **Console namespace ids — what makes one malformed beyond the recorded inputs (#664)** — Each id the recordings name on this namespace is answered as recorded (*Error envelope — the reference's nested `details` member*); the rule that sorts the ids no recording reaches is ours, read off those answers and off the reference's own error text. **Prefix and bytes, not alphabet.** An environment or API key id is well-formed when it carries its `env_` or `apikey_` prefix and a non-empty token of bytes Postgres can store, and is looked up; otherwise it is the recorded 400. The alphabet is left out because the reference's own ids use letters ours does not mint, and were answered 404 (`env_01MQbDnwtRB9MBhtuxWAHq1M`, `apikey_01ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ`). But no recording shows an id whose token neither alphabet holds: `env_NOT!VALID` is looked up and answered 404 here on that inference alone. **UUID spellings.** An environment key id is well-formed as `envkey_` plus storable bytes or as a UUID, and only the hyphenated UUID is recorded (batch2 idx 18, `rec83.edge4.revoke.unknown-uuid`, a 404). The reference's refusal of `not-a-uuid` reads "invalid character: expected an optional prefix of `urn:uuid:` followed by [0-9a-fA-F-]", which is the Rust uuid crate's message; that crate's parser takes exactly four spellings — 32 hex digits bare, 36 grouped 8-4-4-4-12, that grouping in braces, or it after a lowercase `urn:uuid:` — and refuses `urn:uuid:` before 32 bare digits (uuid 1.8.0 and 1.10.0, src/parser.rs try_parse). We accept those four, on the inference that the reference parses the key id with that crate — and the organization segment the same way, since its recorded refusal of `default` is the same crate's message (`rec83.edge6.literal-default-org`): a UUID in any of the four is a foreign organization's 401 here, and only the hyphenated one is recorded (idx 5). **`principal_id`** is well-formed as `user_` or `svac_` followed by a non-empty token of storable bytes, the prefix-and-bytes rule above; only the recorded message names the two prefixes (batch5 `rec86.create.principal_id.bogus-string`). **Which malformed id wins.** On revoke the key id's shape is judged before the environment is resolved, because the reference refuses a malformed key id as path validation (pydantic's `path.token_uuid`, no details) and a malformed environment id in its handler (with details); no recording names both malformed at once. *Evidence: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-05 batch2 idx 2–6 and 18–20, batch5 idx 7–12 and 26, batch8 idx 22; internal/api/consoleapi.go consoleIDShape, isUUID, consoleOrganization and revokeEnvironmentKey; internal/api/consoleapikeys.go apiKeyPrincipal; TestConsoleKeyRevokeRejectsIdsItDoesNotOwn, TestConsoleKeyErrorsCarryTheRecordedDetails, TestAPIKeyRoutesRejectUnknownScopesAndIDs.* *Tracked: #78 (a revoke naming a braced, a bare 32-digit and a `urn:uuid:` key id or organization, one naming both a malformed environment and a malformed key, and an issue or update naming an `env_` or `apikey_` id with punctuation, would settle all three); landed for #820 (the organization segment and `principal_id`).*
- **`self` recursion versus the depth-1 rule** — The reference admits at most one `{"type":"self"}` roster entry, which it says allows "recursive self-invocation", while its depth rule is phrased as a property of the agents a roster references — they "must not themselves have `multiagent` set" — and `self` resolves to an agent that does. The docs add only that session-level overrides "apply to the coordinator and its `self` copies", saying nothing about whether a copy inherits the roster. If it does, depth 1 does not bind the one topology that can recurse. Ours is unaffected either way, and by a different mechanism than the rule: `resolveRoster` exempts `self` from the depth check (read literally the rule would forbid the documented feature), but the six delegation tools are injected **by role**, so a child thread is never offered `create_agent` whatever its own snapshot carries — a `self` copy is a child and cannot spawn. So the inference is about the reference alone, and it matters because a reference that does let a `self` copy recurse bounds depth only by its 25-thread cap. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents multiagent-orchestration (the `self` entry, "recursive self-invocation", the depth sentence, and the session-override sentence); checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaManagedAgentsMultiagentParams.Agents; internal/api/roster.go (the documented `self` exemption); internal/brain/mcptools.go (resolveTools, hasRoster)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of a session whose roster names `self`, read back from a spawned copy's own agent snapshot, would settle whether the reference lets that copy spawn in turn).*
- **Session ID prefix parsing (all endpoints accepting a session id)** — Canonical prefix is `sesn_`; the alternate `session_` form is accepted on input and normalized to `sesn_` before lookup. Whether the reference **accepts** that spelling is unobserved: every concrete session id in the SDK and the `ant` CLI, all of them test fixtures (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession_test.go TestBetaSessionGetWithOptionalParams; checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — betasession_test.go TestBetaSessionsRetrieve), and on the public sessions pages is `sesn_`, and the only reference-side `session_` anywhere is the work-data schema's description of its own `data.id`, which the SDK's worker feeds straight back into `/v1/sessions/{id}/…` — so if the reference ever emits that spelling it must accept it, and a lenient parse is the safe side of an unrecorded question. **The entry this one replaces asserted the accept as settled** — "mirroring the reference's dual form" — on evidence that was this repo's own wire-compatibility rule restated plus two history sections that no longer carry the sentence; it is corrected rather than deleted so the reversal stays auditable. *Evidence: BetaSessionWorkData.ID's description spells it `session_` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironmentwork.go BetaSessionWorkData.ID) against "a `sesn_...` value" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaManagedAgentsSessionActor.SessionID) and the SDK's own fixture, which emits `sesn_test` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — poller_test.go workJSONWithSecret); `sessionID := work.Data.ID`, fed straight into the per-session calls (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — worker.go EnvironmentWorker.handleItem); internal/domain/id.go (altSessionPrefix), internal/api/sessions.go (normalizeSessionID); platform.claude.com api/beta/sessions/retrieve, fetched 2026-08-23 (the Get Session example answers `sesn_011CZkZAtmR3yMPDzynEDxu7`, and the page documents no alternate spelling — its `session_id` path parameter is a field name, not a prefix)* *Tracked: #78 (a recording would settle whether the reference accepts a `session_` session id on input at all, and which spelling its work-poll `data.id` actually carries).*
- **Memory-store update — an empty body is the recorded 400; an empty metadata bag, an empty name's wording, the order of the refusals and a create's null bag are ours** — The recorded part is matched: `POST /v1/memory_stores/{id}` with `{}` answered **400** `invalid_request_error`, "at least one of name, description, or metadata must be provided", with no `details`, and this platform now gives that answer where it used to answer 200 and leave the store alone; a request with no body at all, or a JSON `null` for one, reads as `{}` here, as on every route, and draws the same 400, which no recording sent. What counts as naming a field is the key, not its value: `{"description": null}` was answered 200, so a body of nulls is not empty, and each null takes the recorded answer the entry *Memory-store update — an explicit JSON null on `name`, `description` or `metadata`* gives it. Four readings past the recordings are ours. (1) **An empty metadata bag names metadata**: `{"metadata": {}}` is a 200 that changes nothing, `updated_at` included, because the key is present; the reference might count an empty bag as nothing provided, and no recording sent one. (2) **An empty name takes the null's wording**, `name cannot be empty`, the one this route was recorded using for a cleared name; the 1–255 bound is documented, the reference's create answered `{"name": ""}` with `name: minimum string length is 1` — which this platform's create answers since #540, with `name: Field required` for an absent name (`store.create.no-name`) — and its update was never sent one. (3) **The body is judged before the store is looked up**: the empty body and the null metadata bag, a parse refusal on the reference, are refused before the lookup, as the memory update's own empty-body rule and this route's unknown-key refusal already are, so either one naming a well-formed id with no store behind it, or an archived store, draws its 400 rather than the 404 or the archived refusal; so does every other shape refusal, all parse errors on the reference — a name or description that is not a string, a metadata bag that is not an object of strings and nulls — while a null name or description is a value, judged after the lookup with the empty name. The reference was recorded sending these bodies only to a live store. (4) **A create's null metadata bag is `{}`**: the update's recorded parse refusal names a field type, so the reference's create may refuse a null bag too, but none was sent to it, and this platform's create reads one as `{}`, as every create here does. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `store.update.empty`, `store.update.name+description` and `store.create.empty-name`) and the 2026-09-03 recording (2026-09-03/batch1.json, probes `store.update.description-null` and `store.update.metadata-null`); internal/api/memorystores.go updateMemoryStore; internal/api/memorystores_test.go TestMemoryStoreEmptyUpdate, TestMemoryStoreUpdateSemantics and TestMemoryStoreCreateReadsANullBagAsEmpty* *Tracked: #78 (would settle the four readings: on update, `{"metadata": {}}`, `{"name": ""}`, a bodiless request and a `null` body; `{}`, `{"metadata": null}`, `{"metadata": "x"}` and `{"name": null}` against a missing and an archived store; and `{"name": "n", "metadata": null}` on create); landed for #817.*
- **Content over 100 kB, or content that is not valid UTF-8, is a 400 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The reference documents the 100 kB (102,400-byte) ceiling as a property of the memory object — "Maximum 100 kB" on `content` — and never says what a request exceeding it answers. It has a 413 `request_too_large` family, which this platform mirrors for its own uploads (`errors.go`'s `errTypeRequestTooLarge`, used by the file and skill upload paths), so a 413 is entirely plausible here; the platform answers 400 instead because the reference frames the bound as validation of the memory rather than of the request. The reference's worker cannot tell the two apart either — it treats a 400 and a 413 on an upload identically, remembering the SHA so the same bytes are not re-sent — which is why the choice costs no compatibility today. **The size half is recorded now, and it matches**: a create carrying 102,401 bytes answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "content must be at most 102400 bytes" (2026-09-02 free_batch1 `mem.create.102401-bytes`), the sentence this platform answers since #540. The UTF-8 half is inert on this route (a JSON string decodes to valid UTF-8 by construction) and load-bearing on the sync path slice 4 adds, where the bytes come from a file; so is the check beside it that the content holds no U+0000 — legal UTF-8 that Postgres text refuses, already a 400 on every route through the body check above, and on the sync path a refusal remembered by digest rather than a settlement that fails the whole store on every run. *Evidence: "Maximum 100 kB (102,400 bytes)" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsMemory.Content and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryNewParams.Content); `case (isStatus(err, 400) || isStatus(err, 413)) && localSHA != ""` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go SessionMemoryStores.upload); internal/memsync/memsync.go ValidateContent, internal/api/errors.go errTypeRequestTooLarge (the 413 family this deliberately does not join); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryContentRules; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (content that is not valid UTF-8 — the size half of recording item 9 is answered, the encoding half was never sent).*
- **The memories list — byte-wise path order, `path_prefix` defaulting to `/` and matched literally, and any `depth` but 0 or 1 refused (plan 36 slice 2, decision 5)** — Three readings in one place, because they are one endpoint's contract. **Order**: the reference promises "a stable, server-defined order" and says prefixes "interleave with `memory` items in path order" without saying which collation, so this platform sorts bytes, spelled `COLLATE "C"` on the column, its unique index, the `ORDER BY` and the cursor comparison — the executor already pins `LC_ALL=C` shell-side for the same reason, that `postgres:16-alpine` and Cloud SQL do not agree on `en_US.utf8`. **A 2026-09-02 recording refutes the premise**: the reference returned `/x/w.md`, `/x/y/z.md`, `/ä`, `/a`, `/A` — strict newest-first by `created_at`, not path order at all — so the collation question never arises there, and this platform's byte order is a mismatch rather than one reading of an underspecified promise. **#549** owns it. **`path_prefix`**: documented as "Must end with `/` (segment-aligned)", with no stated default and no stated answer for a value that does not; this platform defaults it to `/` (which every path starts with, so an omitted filter is no filter) and matches whatever arrives literally rather than refusing it — so `/notes/` cannot reach `/notes-archive/`, and a prefix of `/a_b` or `/100%` means those bytes, not a pattern. **The recording refutes this half too**: the reference answered a `path_prefix` of `/x`, without the trailing slash, with 400 "path_prefix must be segment-aligned and end with /", where this platform accepts it literally — also #549's. **`depth`**: documented as `0` (or omitted) and `1` with no bounds given; anything else is a 400 rather than a silent clamp, since a caller asking for `depth=2` wants something this endpoint cannot give. **This one the recording confirms**: `depth=2` answered 400, "depth must be 0 or 1 when provided" — the words this platform answers since #540, as it does the list's two `limit` refusals the reference was recorded making (2026-09-05 batch4 `rec85.memories.list.limit-*`): above 1,000 "limit: must be greater than or equal to 1 and less than or equal to 1000", from 101 to 1,000 "limit: value must be greater than or equal to 1 and less than or equal to 100". It adds one nuance that is ours to reconcile — with a `path_prefix` present the reference refuses `depth=0` too ("depth: must be greater than or equal to 1"), which this platform accepts. *Evidence: the list params' documentation of `path_prefix` and `depth` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.PathPrefix and BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.Depth) and BetaManagedAgentsMemoryPrefix, "interleaves with `memory` items in path order" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryPrefix); internal/store/migrations/0029_memories.sql (the `COLLATE "C"` column), internal/api/memories.go listMemories, internal/executor/harvest.go (the LC_ALL=C precedent); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryList / TestMemoryListPrefixAndDepth; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 5* *Tracked: #549 (the recorded newest-first order, the refused non-segment-aligned prefix, and the `depth=0`-with-prefix refusal — all three recorded 2026-09-02; the `depth=2` bound is confirmed and needs no further recording, and the implied default prefix stays unprobed).*
- **A memory at `/.anthropic-memory-store`, or under it, is created like any other and never lands in a mount; a rename onto the path itself is refused with a 400 (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4; #669)** — Every store directory a worker mounts carries a marker file of that name at its root, whose bytes are `"version 1\n<memory_store_id>"` and whose absence or alteration makes a worker stop trusting the directory. **The create half is recorded, and this platform now matches it**: the reference answers a create at that path with 200 and lists the memory as an ordinary row, applying no reserved-path rule at the API. This platform refused it with a 400 until #669 — an inference the recording refuted, as its documented path table (empty, `.` and `..` segments; no reserved name) already hinted. **Keeping such a memory off the marker is the consumers' job, and ours mirror the reference's own client**, whose worker skips a listed memory at that path with a warning rather than write it over the marker, and never uploads the marker. Both of ours do the same — the BYOC worker in its one listing loop, the executor where it reads a store's rows to land them and to settle a sync — so the memory is never written over the marker (which would leave the directory untrusted and every later write of the session withheld as pull-only), never pulled, and never recorded in a baseline, where its absence from the directory would read as a local deletion. It stays in the store and counts toward the 2,000-memory cap. **Ours skip a memory *under* that path too, and that step is ours alone**: the reference client's test is the exact path, and lets `/.anthropic-memory-store/x.md` through, yet such a memory can never land either — it needs a directory where the marker file is, so on a fresh mount the landing batch fails at the marker and on a landed one every batch that pulls it fails, taking the store's other pulls with it. The API accepts both a create and a rename at such a path, as it accepts any other: the reference's answer to either is unrecorded, and a refusal would be a divergence with nothing to argue it. **The rename half is our choice, unobserved**: no probe renamed a memory onto that path, and this platform keeps refusing one — 400 `invalid_request_error`, `path /.anthropic-memory-store is reserved for the memory store's marker file` — judged once the memory is read, so a missing memory answers its 404 first; an update naming the path the memory already holds is no rename and is not refused. Nothing else is involved: `/x/.anthropic-memory-store` is an ordinary memory that both workers sync, each excluding the marker by its root-relative path rather than its basename — the reference's by comparing root-relative keys, ours with `find . -type f ! -path './.anthropic-memory-store'`. *Recording: managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/free_batch1.json, probes `mem.create.marker-root` and `mem.create.marker-nested` (both 200) and `mem.list.view=full` (both listed as ordinary rows); no probe renames onto the marker's path.* *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/self-hosted-sandboxes ("Each store directory contains a marker file named `.anthropic-memory-store`… the worker does not sync a directory whose marker is missing or altered"); `const MarkerPath = ".anthropic-memory-store"`, the skip on a listed memory at it, and the marker's exclusion from the upload (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — memories.go MarkerPath and SessionMemoryStores.listMemories and SessionMemoryStores.scanMarker); the spec's BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams.path, the documented rules, which name no reserved path (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateMemoryParams.properties.path); internal/memsync/memsync.go IsMarkerPath, ShadowsMarker and ValidatePath, internal/api/memories.go updateMemory, internal/executor/memory.go materializeStore and settleStore, internal/worker/memory.go listMemories, internal/memsync/tree.go HashTreeCommand, internal/sandbox/bulkwrite.go BulkPrepareShell and BulkRenameShell (the two refusals a memory under the marker meets); internal/memsync/memsync_test.go TestValidatePath / TestIsMarkerPath / TestShadowsMarker, internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryPathRules, internal/executor/memory_test.go and internal/worker/memory_test.go TestMemoryShadowingTheMarkerIsSkipped; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (a rename onto `/.anthropic-memory-store`, and a create or rename under it, none of which a recording has probed: the status of each); landed for #669.*
- **The memory-versions list defaults to 20 items per page (plan 36 slice 2, decision 5)** — Its `limit` parameter carries no documentation at all in the SDK ("Query parameter for limit") and no default in the guides, unlike its siblings: the memories list documents "Defaults to 20 when omitted", and so do the store and agent lists. Twenty is therefore taken from the siblings rather than observed, along with the 1–100 range this platform applies to every list. The ordering beside it is not an inference — the reference does state "ordered by `created_at` descending (newest first), with `id` as tiebreak". *Evidence: the list params, `limit` undocumented (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememoryversion.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryVersionListParams.Limit) against "Defaults to 20 when omitted" (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betamemorystorememory.go BetaMemoryStoreMemoryListParams.Limit); the spec's BetaManagedAgentsListMemoryVersionsResult, the documented order (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsListMemoryVersionsResult.properties.data); internal/api/memoryversions.go listMemoryVersions, internal/api/page.go defaultLimit; internal/api/memoryversions_test.go TestMemoryVersionList* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 8 — `GET …/memory_versions` with no `limit`: the page size it returns, and the shape of its `next_page`).*
- **The 2,001st memory is refused with a 400 `invalid_request_error` (plan 36 slice 2, decision 4)** — The memory guide says a store holds at most 2,000 memories and that "writes to new memories fail" past it, and stops there: no status, no error type. This platform answers 400 `memory store … holds 2000 memories`, counted under the store row's lock so two concurrent creates cannot both squeeze in. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the 2,000 limit); internal/api/memories.go createMemory (`memoriesPerStore`); internal/api/memories_test.go TestMemoryStoreCapacity; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 4* *Tracked: #78 (not among the plan's fifteen items: a 2,001st create against the reference would settle the status and type).*
- **The memory element has no id, and `GET`/`DELETE …/resources/{rid}` answer 404 for it (plan 36 slice 3, decision 7)** — The response variant carries `memory_store_id, type, access, description, instructions, mount_path, name` and nothing else (`instructions` only when the attachment supplied one, the recorded shape the element entry among the divergences above describes) — no `id`, `created_at` or `updated_at`, where the file and repository variants carry all three — so nothing in the element can serve as a `{rid}`. Yet the get and update response unions of `…/resources/{rid}` list the memory variant, so the reference has some handle that returns one; which is unrecorded. This platform's get and delete admit only `sesrsc_` ids, so a `memory_store_id` in the path is the shape-check 404 like any other non-`sesrsc_` value, and the attachment can be neither read alone nor removed: attach is create-time only (the SDK's add endpoint is typed file-only), so the session's lifetime is the attachment's. The resources list serves the element in place and pages across it by a key of its own (`memstore:<memory_store_id>`, unique per session by the same-store-twice rule) inside the cursor, which stays opaque. *Evidence: the three variants' key sets, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaManagedAgentsMemoryStoreResource and BetaManagedAgentsFileResource and BetaManagedAgentsGitHubRepositoryResource; the unions carrying the memory variant, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaSessionResourceGetResponseUnion and BetaSessionResourceUpdateResponseUnion; add is file-only, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betasessionresource.go BetaSessionResourceAddParams and BetaSessionResourceAddParams.MarshalJSON; internal/api/sessionresources.go checkResourceID / resourceKey; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachment / TestSessionResourceListPagesAcrossMemoryElements* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 3 — `GET`/`DELETE …/resources/{rid}` with the `memory_store_id` and with a `sesrsc_` id: which handle, if any, returns the memory element).*
- **Stores whose slugs collide all attach, each later one suffixed `-2`, `-3`, … — recorded for a pair, ours beyond it (plan 36 slice 3, decision 8)** — Nothing published says what two stores that slug alike — "notes" and "NOTES", one mount path — do in one session. The 2026-09-02 recording answers it for a pair, twice: `session.create.case-colliding-stores` ("notes", "NOTES") and `session.create.five-stores` ("notes", then "(Notes)") each answered 200, the earlier store at `/mnt/memory/notes` and the later at `/mnt/memory/notes-2`. This platform refused such a create with a 400 naming both stores — an unregistered divergence, and one that failed every fire of a deployment carrying such a pair — and now attaches both the same way. The rest of the rule is ours, because a pair cannot show it. "Earlier" is request order: in both probes the earlier store was also the earlier created, so creation order fits the recording as well, while the store ids' lexical order does not (the five-stores pair runs against it). Every store claims its own slug before any suffix is handed out, so a suffix never displaces a store whose name slugs to it: beside "notes" and "NOTES", a store named "notes 2" keeps `notes-2` and "NOTES" takes `notes-3`, in whichever order the three are attached. The stores that lost then take the first of `-2`, `-3`, … that nothing has claimed. A suffixed slug is held to 255 bytes, the Linux NAME_MAX, since a longer mount directory would fail every write under it — a 255-character name slugs to 255 bytes, so the slug is cut short enough for its suffix, a hyphen the cut leaves at the end dropped, and the cut candidate is checked against the claimed slugs like any other; no unsuffixed slug needs the cut, a name being at most 255 characters that each slug to at most one byte and the id fallback 33 bytes. And the whole-id fallback is a slug like any other, so a store named after an all-symbol store's id collides with it. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.case-colliding-stores` and `session.create.five-stores` in `batch2.json`; the stores' creation order, `store.create.notes` and `store.create.(Notes)` in `free_batch1.json`, `store.create.NOTES` in `batch2.json`); internal/api/sessionresources.go suffixCollidingMemoryMounts and memoryMountNameMax; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreSlugCollisions; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 8* *Tracked: #78 (three stores that slug alike, a store named "notes 2" beside two named "notes", a colliding pair attached against their creation order, and two 255-character names that slug alike — recording item 7 took only the pair); landed for #671.*
- **The same store attached twice, and a ninth store, are each a 400 (plan 36 slice 3, decision 7)** — The cap is documented ("8 stores per session"); what exceeding it answers is not, nor what two attachments of one store answer. The second is recorded: `session.create.same-store-twice` answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "resources contains duplicate memory_store_id: …", which this platform matches on status and type and, since #540, on session create in those words too (a deployment's resources keep ours, `memory store … is attached more than once`, never recorded there). The ninth is unrecorded and refused the same way here. Both are judged before any row is read. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.same-store-twice`); platform.claude.com managed-agents/memory (the per-session limit); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseResourceInputs; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachmentRejections; docs/plan/36_memory-stores.md decision 7* *Tracked: #78 (a ninth store — not among the plan's fifteen items, and would need a call of its own).*
- **Attaching a well-formed memory store id that names no store is a 404 `not_found_error` (#668)** — The spec leaves an unknown id's status unstated. The entry this replaces answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, on the `vault_ids` precedent; this platform now answers 404 `not_found_error`, and since #540 in the words the reference's one recorded unknown-store attach used, "Memory store `memstore_…` not found." (below). What the reference does here is inferred rather than recorded. Its one recorded attach of an unknown store, `session.create.unknown-store`, did answer **404 `not_found_error`** ("Memory store `memstore_…` not found."), but that id's token is 25 characters where every real one is 24, so it cannot tell "absent" from "malformed". Since #841 that id answers the same 404 here: on session create a `memstore_` id whose token lies outside this platform's id alphabet — as that one's does, and as every reference id's does — goes on to the store lookup, a text compare through a bind parameter, rather than being refused on shape, so a well-formed absent id and the recorded one take one path (a token that is not storable text is still the 400). The deployment routes, which look no store up until a fire, dream create's input store and the sessions list's `memory_store_id` filter keep the full shape and its 400 (ours, unrecorded). The well-formed case rests on siblings: a 24-character absent id answered 404 `not_found_error` on `GET /v1/memory_stores/{id}` and, as an `environment_id`, on session create itself (2026-09-05), and a deleted file mounted at session create answered 404 (2026-09-02) — so an id naming nothing is a 404 on every resource and route the recordings reach, while a row that exists but is archived is a 400 (the archived-store entry among the divergences above). A deployment fire still records the vanished store as `session_resource_not_found_error` (*A missing memory store at fire time settles session_resource_not_found_error*, among the notes above): the 404 is wrapped with that type, so a manual run settles it on a 200 and a scheduled fire pauses on it. Dream create applies the same rule to its input store (the dream-create entry below). The `vault_ids` entry this one borrowed from stays an inference with its 400: no unknown vault was recorded. *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`session.create.unknown-store`, `sessF2.create.deleted-file-mount`) and the 2026-09-05 recording (`item1.read.absent-store-control`, `item1.create.session-absent-env`); internal/api/sessionresources.go parseMemoryResource and snapshotMemoryStore; internal/api/memorystores.go; internal/api/sessionmemory_test.go TestSessionMemoryStoreAttachmentRejections / TestSessionMemoryAttachLocksTheStoreRow; internal/api/deploymentscheduler_test.go TestDeletedMemoryStoreFailsAFireAsClassified* *Tracked: #78 (a session create attaching a well-formed, absent memory store id — the one recorded attach used a malformed id, so the 404 rests on its siblings); landed for #668 and #841.*
- **The Memory-stores block — its placement, and the rendering of a store archived since attach (plan 36 slice 4, decision 9)** — The block sits after the Mounted-repositories block and before any runtime `system.message` text, and a store archived since its attachment renders `(read-only, archived)`. The 2026-09-02 recording that settled the block's structure could observe neither: its session carried five memory stores and no files or repositories, and no store was archived after attach. *Evidence: internal/brain/replay.go buildRequest (placement); internal/brain/memory.go renderMemoryBlock* *Tracked: #78 (a session with an uploaded file, a repository and a store, asked for its system prompt's sections in order; and one whose store is archived after attach — neither among plan 36's fifteen items).*
- **Cron day-of-month and day-of-week — the POSIX union reading is recorded as wrong; the frequency floor is still unprobed (plan 37 slice 1)** — The published dialect fixes the grammar precisely — five fields, DOW 0-7 with both 0 and 7 Sunday, no seconds or year field, no L / W / # / ?, no @daily — but says nothing about the classic POSIX rule that a restricted day-of-month and a restricted day-of-week are OR'd rather than AND'd, and the strongest evidence for the union was the dialect's own name: both schemas call it a "5-field POSIX cron schedule", and union is what POSIX specifies. **A recording refuted it** (2026-09-02): the reference does not union the two fields, it refuses the expression outright — `0 0 1 * 1` answered 400 `invalid_request_error`, "`schedule.expression` cannot restrict both day-of-month and day-of-week; use `*` for one" — so this platform accepts at create what the reference rejects, and then fires on the union. **That is a bug, not a divergence**, so it has no entry of its own: #543 owns it, with the recorded message, and a reviewer must not clear it on this entry's authority — note that `internal/cron/cron_test.go` TestDayOfMonthUnionDayOfWeek currently pins the very behavior #543 will change. What stays inferred is the frequency floor: none is published, so an expression matching every minute is accepted here, and no probe covered it. The reference's other two create-time schedule refusals do match this platform, and are recorded rather than inferred now — an unsatisfiable expression is 400 on both sides, and since #540 in the same words ("`schedule.expression` does not produce any occurrences in the next year", answered by `deploymentparse.go`'s empty-`Upcoming` check), as is a 6-field expression ("… is not a valid 5-field cron expression: expected 5 fields, got 6"). *Evidence: the 2026-09-02 recording (`deployment.create.dom+dow-cron`, `deployment.create.unsatisfiable-cron`, `deployment.create.6-field-cron`); the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule` and `BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams` (both descriptions open "5-field POSIX cron schedule"; their expression descriptions carry the dialect verbatim and identically), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams; the same sentence, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsSchedule.Expression; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — "Maximum granularity supported is at the minute level"); internal/cron/cron.go matchesDay, internal/api/deploymentparse.go, internal/cron/cron_test.go TestDayOfMonthUnionDayOfWeek.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference enforces any floor on schedule frequency stays unprobed — create a deployment that fires every minute and read upcoming_runs_at; #543 owns the recorded day-of-month/day-of-week refusal).*
- **`upcoming_runs_at` returns five occurrences whenever five exist (plan 37 slice 1)** — The schema says "up to 5" and publishes when the array is non-empty and when it is empty; it does not say when fewer than five come back for a live schedule. We return five whenever five exist within a twelve-year search bound — twelve rather than a round four because a leap-day expression has an eight-year gap across the non-leap year 2100, and a shorter bound would return an empty array for a live schedule the same schema requires to be non-empty. An expression with no occurrence inside that bound is refused at create and update rather than stored, which is the entry below. The two behaviors the schema does state — non-empty for active and paused deployments, empty once archived_at is set — are implemented as published and are not inferences. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.upcoming_runs_at`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.properties.upcoming_runs_at; the same sentence, and no api: tag, making the key optional, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaManagedAgentsSchedule.UpcomingRunsAt; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-29 — its create example answers a weekly schedule with three occurrences, not five, which is an example rather than a rule but is the closest thing to an observation of the reference's count); internal/api/deployments.go fillScheduleTimestamps, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentScheduleComputesItsTimestamps.* *Tracked: #78 (compare a weekly and a per-minute schedule's arrays on one recording).*
- **A cron expression with no occurrence is refused at create and update (plan 37 slice 1)** — An expression naming 31 February parses, every field is in range, and that date never arrives; the reference publishes no validation rule for such an expression, only that `upcoming_runs_at` is non-empty for an active deployment — which is unsatisfiable together. We answer 400 rather than storing a schedule that can never fire, searching the same twelve-year bound `upcoming_runs_at` uses. Twelve years rather than four because a leap-day expression has an eight-year gap across 2100, which a shorter bound would misreport as unsatisfiable. The refusal itself is recorded now — the reference answered `0 0 31 2 *` with 400 `invalid_request_error`, "`schedule.expression` does not produce any occurrences in the next year" (2026-09-02 `deployment.create.unsatisfiable-cron`), the sentence this platform answers since #540, with the twelve-year search reported to the operator's log instead. What stays inferred is the horizon: the reference's sentence suggests it searches one year, which would refuse a sparse but satisfiable expression this platform stores. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams.expression` (the dialect, with no validation rule), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronScheduleParams.properties.expression, and `BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.upcoming_runs_at` ("non-empty for active and paused deployments"), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.properties.upcoming_runs_at; internal/cron/cron.go SearchYears, internal/api/deploymentparse.go parseDeploymentSchedule, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentScheduleRejections and TestDeploymentScheduleRefusalsLogWhatTheSentenceDrops.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference's search stops at one year — post an expression that fires only on 29 February and read the status code).*
- **Update, pause and unpause on an archived deployment are 400s (plan 37 slice 1)** — Archive is terminal and the reference publishes no status code for acting on an archived deployment through any path; the spec attaches no operation-specific errors to any deployment endpoint. We answer 400 on update, pause and unpause alike, matching how this platform already refuses to act on an archived resource, while GET and the list with include_archived still return the row. Update and pause are recorded now — 400 `invalid_request_error`, "Cannot modify archived deployment" (2026-09-02 `deployment.update.on-archived`, `deployment.pause.on-archived`) — and since #540 all three answer in those words; unpause is the inference. Leaving update unspecified would leave hidden configuration mutable on a row nothing can ever fire. The manual-run path takes the same refusal one slice later. *Evidence: the spec's deployment update, pause and unpause operations, no per-endpoint error semantics on any of the three, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Update and BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, every error status answering the one shared error body, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaErrorResponse; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — archive described as terminal, with no status code for acting on one); internal/api/deployments.go loadDeployment, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestArchiveDeploymentIsTerminalForMutation.* *Tracked: #78 (unpause an archived deployment on the reference and read the status code; update and pause are recorded).*
- **A deployment `description` set to the empty string reads back as null (plan 37 slice 1)** — The field is in the Deployment object's required list and typed string-or-null, so null is the reference's own spelling of unset; the create params accept a string with no minimum length, so the empty string is legal input. This platform stores the column NOT NULL with an empty-string default and renders the empty string as null, which round-trips every value except one: a caller who sets description to "" reads back null. The two are not distinguishable in storage and are not distinguishable in meaning, and the alternative — a second nullable column added by a later migration to preserve a distinction nothing acts on — buys nothing. What the reference does with an explicit empty string is unrecorded. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsDeployment.description` (required, string-or-null), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeployment, and `BetaManagedAgentsCreateDeploymentParams.description` (no minLength), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateDeploymentParams.properties.description; internal/store/migrations/0031_deployments.sql (the column), internal/api/deployments.go scanDeployment.* *Tracked: #78 (create a deployment with description "" and read it back).*
- **Pause and unpause are idempotent 200s, unpause does not re-validate the cause, and a body on an action endpoint is ignored (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference documents neither a conflict for pausing an already-paused deployment nor a precondition failure for unpausing an active one, says nothing about whether unpausing an error-paused deployment is refused while the cause persists, and declares no requestBody on any of the four action endpoints. We answer 200 in all four idempotency cases, returning the resource; unpause clears paused_at and both reason columns unconditionally, and the next scheduled fire re-discovers the cause and re-pauses if it is still there; a request body is neither read nor refused. *Evidence: the spec's deployment pause and unpause operations, neither declaring a request body, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentService.Pause and BetaDeploymentService.Unpause, and neither carrying per-endpoint error semantics, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaErrorResponse; the four action params structs, which carry only headers, the beta and workspace ones, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentArchiveParams and BetaDeploymentPauseParams and BetaDeploymentRunParams and BetaDeploymentUnpauseParams; internal/api/deployments.go setDeploymentPause, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentPauseAndUnpause.* *Tracked: #78 (six calls: pause twice, unpause twice, each against an archived deployment, and one with a non-empty body).*
- **A deployment's initial_events must satisfy the same adjacency rule a posted batch does (plan 37 slice 1)** — The reference's deployment initial_events union admits user.message, user.define_outcome and system.message with no stated ordering constraint. This platform routes a deployment's list through events.NormalizeInbound, the same normalizer POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events uses, which requires a system.message to be the final event of the request and to immediately follow a user.message, user.tool_result or user.custom_tool_result. So a user.message followed by a system.message is accepted and a lone system.message is a 400 — narrower than the union states. Calling the one normalizer at every entry point beats a second copy of the rules whose ordering could drift from the first, and refusing at create beats recording a failed run every night on a list that can never normalize. *Evidence: the spec's BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentInitialEventParams (the three-type discriminated union, with no ordering constraint), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentInitialEventParams; internal/events/inbound.go NormalizeInbound (the adjacency rule), internal/api/deploymentwire.go validateDeploymentInitialEvents, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestDeploymentInitialEventsMustSurviveTheNormalizer.* *Tracked: #78 (post a deployment whose initial_events is a lone system.message, and record the status).*
- **A `user.message` whose `content` is a plain string is accepted, and echoed back as one (plan 35 slice 0, surfaced on a typed object by plan 37 slice 1)** — Every reference schema types this content as an array of blocks — `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.content` and `BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.content` alike — and the SDK's `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParamsContentUnion` has arms for text, image, document and redacted blocks and none for a bare string. This platform accepts the string form on inbound `user.message` and stores it verbatim, so the event echoes back as a string. Replay renders it as a single text block, which is why the empty string is refused there like an empty text block. The leniency long predates deployments and is left standing rather than tightened, because narrowing it would break session clients already sending it; what plan 37 changes is the blast radius, since a deployment's `initial_events` are echoed inside a schema'd response object, where an SDK client decoding the array type sees no content at all. *Evidence: the spec's `BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.content` and `BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.content` (both `type: array`), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParams.properties.content and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentUserMessageEvent.properties.content; four block arms, no string arm, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsUserMessageEventParamsContentUnion; internal/events/inbound.go normalizeUserMessage.* *Tracked: #78 (post a string-content user.message to the reference and record the status).*
- **The deployments list refuses `status` beside `include_archived` on presence, not on value (plan 37 slice 1)** — The published sentence is "To include archived deployments, use `include_archived` instead; the two cannot be combined", which settles the contradictory pair — recorded since, 400 `invalid_request_error` "include_archived: cannot be set together with status" (2026-09-02 `deployment.list.status+include_archived`), the words this platform answers since #540 — and leaves the redundant one open: `?status=active&include_archived=false` asks for something coherent, since excluding archived rows is what the status filter already does. We answer 400 to both, keying the refusal on the parameter being sent rather than on how its boolean parses — "cannot be combined" reads as a rule about sending both, and a caller who sends both has a belief about the endpoint worth correcting once rather than silently satisfying. The blast radius is small: the `ant` CLI sends only flags the user set, so `--status active` alone is unaffected and only an explicit `--include-archived=false` beside it is refused. *Evidence: the quoted sentence, with no statement about the redundant pair and the same text as the list operation's parameter description, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadeployment.go BetaDeploymentListParams.Status; unset flags omitted from the request, checked against anthropic-cli v1.30.0 — requestflag.go ExtractRequestContents; internal/api/deployments.go listDeployments, internal/api/deployments_test.go TestListDeployments.* *Tracked: #78 (send status with include_archived=false to the reference and read the status code).*
- **A deployment is fired only through POST /run and the schedule; sessions cannot name one (plan 37 slice 3)** — The reference's manual trigger context reads "The run was started manually by creating a session directly against the deployment", yet its own session-create params carry no deployment field and no /v1/deployments/{id}/sessions path exists. We take POST /run to be that path, described from the server's point of view, and keep `deployment_id` out of the session-create allow-list — where a client sending it gets the same 400 unknown-field answer it gets today. *Evidence: the spec's BetaManagedAgentsManualTriggerContext (the quoted description), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsManualTriggerContext, and create-session params (required agent and environment_id, no deployment field), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCreateSessionParams; SessionNewParams has no deployment field, absent at anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasession.go BetaSessionNewParams.DeploymentID; internal/api/deploymentruns.go deploymentSessionIn, internal/api/deploymentruns_test.go TestSessionsListFiltersByDeployment.* *Tracked: #78 (whether any request can set a session's deployment_id directly).*
- **Catch-up after downtime — at most one occurrence, inside a one-hour window (plan 37 slice 4, decision 2)** — Nothing in the spec, the SDK, the CLI or the docs describes what a scheduled deployment does about occurrences missed while the platform was down; a spec-wide grep for catch-up / backfill / misfire returns nothing. The one adjacent published rule is about pause: "Unpause resumes the schedule from the next scheduled occurrence. Missed triggers are not backfilled." We generalize it: a tick fires only the most recent due occurrence per deployment, and only if it falls inside a one-hour window (a package constant, not a knob). A day-long outage on a */5 schedule would otherwise spawn 288 sessions at once, and the reference's own (deployment_id, scheduled_at) key would admit every one of them. The same rule is what bounds a newly-tightened expression from back-firing, since no watermark is stored to reset. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — the unpause sentence); the dedup key, which bounds duplicates but not backlogs, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsScheduleTriggerContext.properties.scheduled_at; internal/api/deploymentscheduler.go.* *Tracked: #78 (what a real schedule does across a deliberate outage, and what it does when an expression is tightened; note this is the one inference a recording may never settle, since the reference's downtime is not ours to arrange).*
- **schedule.last_run_at moves only on a successful scheduled fire (plan 37 slice 4)** — The field is "Time the most recent scheduled run actually started. Null until one completes; preserved after the deployment is archived. Manual runs do not update this." The last two clauses are published and we honor them exactly; what the sentence does not say is what a fire that failed to create a session does to it. We read "started" as "started a session" and leave the field untouched on a failed fire. All three behaviors fall out of one derivation rather than three code paths: last_run_at is the created_at of the row ORDER BY scheduled_at DESC LIMIT 1 selects over this deployment's runs where trigger_type = 'schedule', succeeded_at IS NOT NULL and scheduled_at IS NOT NULL — the trigger filter excludes manual runs, archive touches no run row, and a failed fire's row has a null succeeded_at. Success is judged from succeeded_at rather than the session link, which was ON DELETE SET NULL until migration 0042 and let a deleted session pull the field backwards to an older run (#520, migration 0032); the links it nulled stay null (#663). created_at rather than scheduled_at because the field says the run "actually started" and scheduled_at is the pre-jitter cron match, which this platform fires 0-30 seconds after in steady state and up to an hour after across a restart that triggers catch-up. The row is selected by ordering on scheduled_at with an explicit scheduled_at IS NOT NULL, which is what lets the partial unique index serve it; that is not the same query as MAX(created_at), and deliberately so — under overlapping fires it names the most recent occurrence rather than the most recently inserted row. *Evidence: checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsCronSchedule.properties.last_run_at; internal/api/deployments.go fillScheduleTimestamps (the derivation).* *Tracked: #78 (let a scheduled fire fail against an archived environment, then read the deployment).*
- **session_creation_rejected_error does not pause the deployment (plan 37 slice 4)** — The paused-reason union carries fourteen of the run error union's sixteen types, omitting session_rate_limited_error and session_creation_rejected_error. The prose cuts the other way for this one — it is "rejected with a non-retryable validation error", and non-retryable is an argument for stopping the schedule — but the structure settles it: paused_reason.error "Matches the failed run's error.type", and the union contains no member this type could take, so a pause carrying it is unrepresentable — were one ever recorded, the schedule would keep firing. Nothing here records one: the fire routes every unclassified rejection through a full rollback that leaves no run row at all (the unknown_error entry above), so the store admits the type and no path emits it. *Evidence: "non-retryable validation error", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsSessionCreationRejectedRunError; "Matches the failed run's error.type", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentPausedReasonError; the fourteen, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsDeploymentPausedReasonError.discriminator.mapping; the sixteen, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsRunError.discriminator.mapping.* *Tracked: #78 (record a run of that type and read the deployment's status).*
- **agent_archived_error is recorded for a roster member, never for the deployment's own agent (plan 37 slice 4)** — The type's schema description says "The deployment's agent was archived", while the guide says an archived top-level agent archives the deployment in the same operation with no run recorded, and that this error type is what an archived subagent produces. Both hold only if the type is unreachable for the top-level case, and under decision 7 it is unreachable by construction rather than by convention: a live deployment refuses the archive of the agent it pins, so the deployment's own agent can never become archived while the deployment can still fire. *Evidence: both variants of the type, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsAgentArchivedRunError and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsAgentArchivedDeploymentPausedReasonError; platform.claude.com managed-agents/scheduled-deployments (fetched 2026-08-27 — the subagent rule and "In both cases no deployment run is recorded" for the top-level agent); internal/api/agents.go archiveAgent.* *Tracked: #78 (archive a top-level agent and a roster member in turn, and read the run list both times).*
- **Environment `packages.type` — accepted on input, never persisted, rendered on every cloud response** — The reference SDK types the field as a discriminator admitting only the literal "packages": `BetaPackagesParams.Type` (`json:"type,omitzero"`) on the create/update params, `BetaPackages.Type` (`json:"type"`) on the response — the one field among the seven the response type carries that is not tagged `api:"required"` the way its six manager-list siblings are. This platform accepts the key absent or exactly "packages" on create/update (any other value — including "", null, and a number — answers 400 `packages.type must be "packages"`), stores none of it — `normalizeEnvConfig`'s persisted config stays the six manager lists — and instead stamps `"type":"packages"` onto every cloud environment's packages object at render time, across create, get, list, update and archive alike (`packagesTypeEcho`). Whether the reference's own response always carries the field, as its typed response field suggests, or omits it the way every published request example does, is unobserved: the docs page's "Packages" section shows the key in none of its one request example's nine language variants (cURL, CLI, Python, TypeScript, C#, Go, Java, PHP, Ruby), and the page includes no response body for any environment endpoint. The one signal pointing toward round-tripping is client-side rather than server-observed: two of Anthropic's own cwc-workshops write the field — one as YAML fed to `ant beta:environments create` (production-ready-agent), one as a TypeScript SDK object literal (research-desk) — evidence a real client writes it, not that a response echoes it. *Evidence: the six manager fields, each `api:"required"`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaenvironment.go BetaPackages.Apt and BetaPackages.Cargo and BetaPackages.Gem and BetaPackages.Go and BetaPackages.Npm and BetaPackages.Pip; `BetaPackages.Type`, untagged, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaenvironment.go BetaPackages.Type; `BetaPackagesParams.Type`, `omitzero`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.66.0 — betaenvironment.go BetaPackagesParams.Type; platform.claude.com managed-agents/environments (fetched 2026-09-04 — the "Packages" section's one request example, in all nine language variants, carries no `type`, and no response body is shown for any environment endpoint on the page); production-ready-agent bin/setup.sh:40 (`ant beta:environments create < seed/environments/research.yaml`) and seed/environments/research.yaml (`packages: {type: packages, ...}`); research-desk src/lib/provision.ts:122-134 (`client.beta.environments.create({..., packages: { type: "packages", ... }})`) (cited in #382); internal/api/environments.go parsePackages, packagesTypeEcho.* *Tracked: #78 (a GET or POST against a real cloud environment with packages set, read back, would settle whether the reference's response ever carries packages.type).*
- **The `config.packages` install commands — the six the docs name, and every choice inside them ours (plan 40 decision 3)** — The docs pin the managers, their alphabetical order, one native pin example each and that an unpinned entry installs the latest; nothing about how the install is run. Here it is one `bash -c` per non-empty manager through `Sandbox.Exec` — the same seam every tool uses, so both backends behave identically and `sandbox.Spec` is untouched — shaped `set -o pipefail; { <preflight>; <install>; } 2>&1 | tail -c 8192`, so the bytes kept are the *last* of the combined output, which is the failure, where `Exec`'s own cap would keep the head. The preflight is `command -v <manager>` (for `pip`, `python3 -m pip --version`, since a slim image ships `python3` without `pip` and that failure exits 1), making a missing manager exit 127 whatever the manager. The commands, and the choices in them: `apt` opens `export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive; dpkg --configure -a` before `apt-get update -q && apt-get install -y -q`, with `-o APT::Sandbox::User=root` on both `apt-get` calls — the repair because a transaction an earlier deadline killed otherwise wedges every later `apt-get`, the agent's own included; the update because a slim image ships no package lists; and the sandbox-user override because apt otherwise drops its acquire methods to `_apt`, which needs the `CAP_SETUID` and `CAP_SETGID` this platform's own default capability drop removes, so every fetch would die with `Method http has died unexpectedly` on a default deployment rather than only a hardened one; `cargo install --root /usr/local` and `GOBIN=/usr/local/bin go install` put binaries on a `PATH` an arbitrary image includes rather than in a home directory it may not; `go` runs **one invocation per entry**, because `go install` refuses `@version` arguments from different modules in one call, and appends `@latest` to an entry carrying no `@`, because outside a module it requires a version and the docs promise the latest; `npm install -g` puts binaries on `PATH` while leaving `require()` from the workdir needing a `NODE_PATH` the sandbox does not set; `gem install --no-document`; and `pip` overrides PEP 668's externally-managed refusal with `PIP_BREAK_SYSTEM_PACKAGES=1` rather than the flag, because an older pip rejects an unknown flag and ignores an unknown variable. Every entry is single-quoted, so a list element is one argv member whatever it contains, and is otherwise passed verbatim as the docs' per-manager examples show. The pass needs a **root sandbox with a writable root** — every manager writes under `/usr` or `/var` — and one cheap probe (`id -u`, `/usr` and `/var` writability) says so before the first manager that actually execs, so a pass whose managers are all settled or refused runs no exec at all; it refuses the whole pass with reason `sandbox_not_root` or `rootfs_read_only` rather than six timeouts' worth of `Permission denied`. Probing rather than reading `cfg.Hardening` is deliberate: `RunAsUser` and `ReadOnlyRootfs` are bound at create, an adopted sandbox may disagree with this executor's current config, and an image whose own default user is non-root is invisible to the config entirely; an answer the probe cannot have produced installs anyway, with a log line rather than an invented wire reason. Budget: `EXECUTOR_PACKAGE_INSTALL_TIMEOUT` (10 minutes) is **one manager's** deadline, six managers being six budgets rather than one shared, and it joins the repository clone in the floor the executor's stall budget is held above — and the probe is bounded by the same budget, so a wedged shell answering `id -u` cannot hang provisioning on the outer lease alone. One unchanged list is attempted at most three times per sandbox, after which it is left alone until it changes, so a typo'd entry or a registry the gate refuses stops costing an install budget before every tool call. Under `limited` networking the install's egress is the session's: through the gate where one is configured, which since #597 honors `allow_package_managers` by admitting the recorded package-registry hosts (thirty of them since #594), and nowhere at all where none is — a Kubernetes or Docker deployment that has not set a gate image — so there `allow_package_managers: true` buys nothing and the install fails the same bounded way. All six managers therefore reach a registry under `limited` today **on a deployment that configures a gate** — where none is, the flag still buys nothing for any of them — with one distribution-shaped hole: the set's apt hosts are Ubuntu's, so an `apt` list on a Debian base image is refused at the gate rather than at the mirror. Whether the reference runs anything resembling these commands, or builds an image instead, is unrecorded. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/environments § "Packages" (fetched 2026-09-05 — the six managers, the alphabetical order, the per-manager pin examples and "Unpinned packages install the latest version"); internal/executor/packages.go (packageManagers, packagePreflight, quoteEntries, the command builder, packagesProbeCommand, probeSandboxForPackages, packageInstallAttempts), internal/executor/executor.go Config.PackageInstallTimeout, internal/toolset/stall.go LongestStep, cmd/executor/main.go (the stall floor's two named steps); internal/executor/packages_test.go TestInstallsEveryManagerInTheReferencesOrder, TestPackageEntriesAreOneArgvMemberWhateverTheyContain, TestTheProbeRefusesASandboxThatCannotInstall, TestAnUnrecognizedProbeAnswerInstallsAnyway.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference installs through a sandbox shell at all, with what commands, into what install root, and under what per-manager budget and retry cap).*
