- **`session.error` `environment_package_install_error` — a variant of ours, on a failure surface the reference has never published (plan 40 decision 4)** — A manager whose install exits non-zero, times out, or is refused before it starts records one `session.error` whose `error` object is `{type: "environment_package_install_error", manager, reason, message, retry_status, packages_digest}`. `manager` is one of the six and is absent on the two sandbox-level reasons, which are the sandbox's rather than a manager's; `packages_digest` is a SHA-256 of the list, absent on those same sandbox-level reasons, present so a client (and the dedupe) can tell one failing list from another — taken over the entries with every URL credential the executor could move out of band **stripped out** since #599, because this subtree is readable with an environment key while the config it digests needs a management key and an unsalted hash of the entries as written was an offline oracle for a *weak* credential — the shapes that pass leaves inline keep theirs in the pre-image too, only the published value is stripped at all, and the sentinel inside the sandbox still digests the list as written, which is what keeps a rotated credential a changed list; `reason` is `failed` (non-zero exit), `manager_missing` (exit 127), `timeout`, `invalid` (an entry the platform refuses to pass, or a list whose assembled command would overflow the single-`execve`-argument limit), `sandbox_not_root` or `rootfs_read_only`; `retry_status` is `{"type":"retrying"}` until an unchanged list has failed three times in this sandbox and `{"type":"exhausted"}` on that third attempt, and `exhausted` from the first for `invalid` and the two sandbox reasons, since nothing in the session's life makes those acceptable and promising a retry would be a lie. Events are deduped on (manager, reason, retry_status.type, packages_digest): a repeated identical failure of the same list is one event, a reason flip or a changed list is a new one, and the attempt that spends the cap re-emits under the flipped status. Since #599 a changed list is a new event because the executor says the list changed rather than because the digest differs — two lists differing only in a credential now publish the same digest, and a rotation would otherwise be suppressed as a repeat. It says so only where the in-sandbox sentinel holds a record to compare against, which the two terminal refusals never write, so those stay deduped on the digest exactly as before. The digest keys the dedupe on list identity the way the clone error keys on its resource id, so a second failing list is not swallowed by the first's history. **The session runs on** — its other managers still install, its tools still run, and the agent meets the missing package the way it would on a host, as an import error it can read; only a backend fault from `Exec` faults the item, exactly as a provision failure does — and not the one an image's startup file makes by printing past the output cap in every shell, which every retry would meet again (#860): an install whose result it pushed out is that manager's `failed` attempt, and a probe whose answer it pushed out installs nothing that pass, with a log line and no reason of its own on the wire. `message` is the manager's own kept output tail, which makes this **the one `session.error` this platform writes whose text is sandbox-controlled** (the clone error's message is fixed; the MCP path cuts its own to `scheme://host`). That is deliberate — the output *is* the diagnosis — and it is what the three bounds on the way to the log are for: every http(s) URL reduced to `scheme://host` by the MCP path's own `redactURL` and every other scheme's userinfo dropped by `anySchemeUserinfoRe`, because a `pip` or `npm` entry may legitimately carry a credential in a URL's userinfo *or* its query (`?token=…`) and a manager echoes its arguments; `toolset.SanitizeText`, because a NUL byte would fault the jsonb append and a faulted item reclaim-loops; and `toolset.TruncateRunes` at the same 8 KiB the brain's `maxFailureMessage` holds. Three residuals — a query credential in a non-http URL; one whose `http(s)://` scheme the in-shell `tail -c 8192` cut off, leaving a fragment the scheme-anchored redactors miss; and a run of trailing punctuation `redactURL` reattaches so the surrounding sentence still reads, which leaks a credential's trailing-punctuation suffix (its whole value only if the credential is nothing but those characters) — are **narrowed** by #599's out-of-band injection rather than closed by it: a credential lifted out before the command is assembled is one the manager cannot echo, but six entry shapes keep theirs (a credential in a query parameter, a transport that reads neither file the pass writes, a *manager* that reads neither — `apt`, `cargo`, `gem` — a hostname two entries disagree about, a value a bare netrc cannot carry, and a userinfo Go's URL grammar refuses), and for those the three residuals are still what stands between the credential and this text. The entry list is deliberately **not** on the event: the environment's config already holds it for a management key, while the events subtree is also readable with an environment key, and the manager plus the output tail already name what failed. All of this is ours. The reference's session-error union carries eight variants, none about packages, and what a failed install looks like on its wire — an event, a status, or nothing at all — is unrecorded; its retry-status union has a third variant, `terminal`, that this platform never writes here. *Evidence: the union's eight variants, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsSessionErrorEventErrorUnion.Type, and BetaManagedAgentsRetryStatusTerminal, a member of all eight, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betasessionevent.go BetaManagedAgentsRetryStatusTerminal; platform.claude.com managed-agents/environments § "Packages" (fetched 2026-09-05 — silent on what a failed install does); internal/executor/packages.go (packageInstallErrorType, the six reason constants, packagesDigest, packageMessage reusing mcpwork.go's redactURL, emitPackageInstallError, the assembled-command guard against sandbox.MaxCommandBytes); internal/executor/packages_test.go TestAFailedInstallSurfacesAndTheSessionRunsOn, TestARepeatedFailureIsOneEventUntilItIsExhausted, TestADifferentFailingListIsNotSuppressedByTheFirst, TestInstallFailureReasons, TestTheFailureMessageIsSanitizedAndRedacted, TestPackageMessageRedactsEveryCredentialForm, TestAnAssembledCommandTooLongForExecIsRefusedTerminally, TestABackendFaultDuringInstallFaultsTheItem, TestAStartupFloodIsNoInstallFault.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference surfaces a failed install on the wire at all, and if so under what type, reasons and retry semantics).*
- **`config.packages` entries — an empty or `-`-prefixed entry refused, a strictness the SDK points away from (plan 40 decision 6)** — Create and update answer 400 ``packages.<manager> entries must be non-empty and must not begin with "-"``, and the executor applies the same predicate again before building a command, so a row stored before the rule is refused at install (reason `invalid`) rather than handed to a manager. Two shapes are refused and only two: the empty string, which no manager can install, and one beginning with `-`, which every manager reads as an option rather than a package — `--index-url=…` passed to `pip install` is the injection no quoting can stop, because the entry is a single argument either way. Everything else — whitespace, `@`, `==`, `:` — is the managers' own syntax and passes through verbatim. One refused entry refuses its manager's whole list, since a manager's entries are one command. A manager's entries also have an aggregate size cap (16 KiB summed), because the executor hands the whole list to a single `bash -c` argument and Linux caps one `execve` argument near 128 KiB — over it the install would fault at exec startup and reclaim-loop rather than fail cleanly; whether the reference caps a list at all is unrecorded, so this too is a strict-direction divergence. The reference's own entry validation is unrecorded, and what the SDK says points the other way: "You are responsible for validating the package and version exist" puts existence on the caller and suggests a server that checks little. So this is a divergence in the **strict** direction — a create the reference may well accept is a 400 here — registered as such with that sentence as the evidence against it. *Evidence: the sentence, on the create and update params alike, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaCloudConfigParams.Packages and BetaPackagesParams; internal/domain/environment.go ValidPackageEntry (the shared predicate), internal/api/environments.go parsePackages, internal/executor/packages.go validPackageList; internal/domain/environment_test.go, internal/api/environments_test.go (the per-manager refusals and TestEnvironmentPackagesManagerTooLarge), internal/executor/packages_test.go TestAnInvalidEntryIsRefusedWithoutRunningAnything.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference refuses any entry shape at create at all, or accepts everything and lets the manager fail).*
- **The `limited` + `packages` 400 — the reference's rule, but the predicate and the message are ours (plan 40 decision 9)** — The docs give the rule and the status: "If the environment uses `limited` networking, also set `networking.allow_package_managers` to `true`; otherwise the request is rejected with a 400 error", which the networking section restates as applying "whenever the environment specifies `packages` … even if the registry hosts are listed in `allowed_hosts`". Neither the predicate behind "specifies" nor the message is published. This platform reads "specifies" as **a non-empty list**, not a present key, because every stored cloud config carries all six lists — the reference's own response type marks them required — so the key-presence reading would refuse every `limited` environment ever created here; and it answers 400 `invalid_request_error` with the message `packages require networking.allow_package_managers to be true under limited networking`, which names both remedies a caller has. The check runs on the **merged** config after both blocks are parsed, so the two halves of the offending shape cannot arrive separately: an update that adds packages to a `limited` environment, and one that switches a packaged environment to `limited`, are refused exactly as a create is. The consequence worth knowing is for rows stored before the rule — any config patch on one is refused, even a patch touching only `allowed_hosts`, because the merge carries the stored lists into the check; setting the flag or clearing the lists lifts it, and a patch carrying no config at all never reaches it. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/environments § "Packages" and its networking section (fetched 2026-09-05 — both sentences, neither with a message or a response body); the six manager lists, each `api:"required"`, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betaenvironment.go BetaPackages.Apt and BetaPackages.Cargo and BetaPackages.Gem and BetaPackages.Go and BetaPackages.Npm and BetaPackages.Pip; internal/api/environments.go checkPackagesNetworking and normalizeEnvConfig; internal/api/environments_test.go (each refusal, the allow_package_managers acceptance, and the legacy row whose allowed_hosts patch is refused).* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference reads "specifies" as a non-empty list or a present key, and what its 400 message says).*
- **When a `config.packages` install happens, and when a changed list reaches a running session (plan 40 decision 10)** — The docs say the field "pre-installs packages into the sandbox before the agent starts". Here it installs at the session's **first `tool_exec`**, because that is when a sandbox first exists — the lazy provisioning lifecycle plan 25 already recorded for repository clones — and it runs before the skills, repositories, files and memory stores materialize, inside the same session advisory lock the provision holds, so a reclaiming executor waits on a lapsed holder's pass instead of racing its `apt-get` for the dpkg lock. What a client sees is the latency: the first tool call of a packaged session waits for the install, where "before the agent starts" suggests the reference pays it earlier. A `packages` update on the environment reaches a **running** session at its next provision — a sandbox that has already settled a manager's list skips it, a changed list is a fresh attempt — which the docs neither promise nor forbid. The harvest lane installs nothing either way: a grading harvest clears the lists before it provisions, and an idle harvest only attaches to a sandbox that already exists. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents/environments § "Packages" (fetched 2026-09-05 — "before the agent starts"); internal/executor/executor.go provisionSandbox (the installPackages call inside the lock hold) and sessionForRun, internal/executor/harvest.go harvestSandbox; internal/executor/packages_test.go TestPackagesInstallBeforeSkillsMaterialize, TestTheInstallRunsInsideProvisionSandbox, TestTheGradingHarvestInstallsNothing, TestASettledManagerIsNotReinstalled.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference installs eagerly at session create, and whether a packages change reaches a session already running).*
- **An environment's `networking.allowed_hosts` entries are rejected, where the reference publishes no grammar (plan 43)** — The SDK's spec constrains `BetaLimitedNetwork.allowed_hosts` to `{"items": {"type": "string"}, "type": "array"}` (checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaLimitedNetwork.properties.allowed_hosts) — no `pattern`, no `maxItems`, no `format`, and the description says only "Specifies domains the container can reach." Its docs document no rejection for the field, while documenting one for `allow_package_managers` on the same page, so the reference knows how to say "rejected" and does not say it here. This platform holds each entry to the grammar the reference *does* publish for a credential's list — a bare hostname, an IPv4 literal, or a `*.`-prefixed wildcard — and answers 400 otherwise, because an out-of-grammar entry matches exactly the string it was written as and nothing else, and no request host can be that string: the gate splits the port off before it asks the matcher (`hostOnly` on a CONNECT authority, `url.Hostname` on a plain-HTTP one), and the one authority that survives that split still carrying a colon — a bracketed `[host:port]:443` — is refused by `http.ReadRequest` before the handler runs. So a typo reads as the operator's fence when it is really a hole in it. The cost is stated rather than glossed: an IPv6 literal (`::1`) and an underscore label (`_acme.example.com`) *did* match, and are refused at write time now. Three consequences are deliberate. The check runs on the entries a patch newly supplies rather than on the merged config, and carries through any entry whose *value* the row already holds — a value, not an occurrence, so a patch may also repeat one — so a row stored before the rule keeps what it holds and the ordinary read-modify-write is not refused on a value this API returned (plan 43 decision 4 — stored rows are not migrated). And no entry cap is imposed here: the reference publishes 16 for a credential's list and none for this one. *Evidence: BetaLimitedNetwork against BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworking, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaLimitedNetwork.properties.allowed_hosts and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsLimitedCredentialNetworkingParams.properties.allowed_hosts; internal/api/environments.go parseNetworking, internal/egress/hostset.go CanonicalEntry and ValidateHostEntry; internal/api/environments_test.go TestEnvironmentAllowedHostsAreHeldToTheEntryGrammar and TestEnvironmentAllowedHostsCheckedOnThePatchNotTheStoredList.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference rejects a malformed environment `allowed_hosts` entry at all, and what its 400 says); landed for #609.*
- **A Unicode `allowed_hosts` entry is stored, and echoed, as its A-label (plan 43)** — Neither `allowed_hosts` schema says anything about a Unicode hostname, so whether the reference accepts one, and what it echoes back, is unobserved. Here an entry written `bücher.example` is accepted and stored as `xn--bcher-kva.example` on **both** lists, and an operator therefore reads back punycode they did not type; a wildcard is converted after its `*.` prefix is cut, since an asterisk is a rune IDNA refuses. An entry IDNA itself refuses — `ä-.example`, whose trailing hyphen sits inside the label — is a 400 rather than a fallback. The reason is what `ToASCII` returns *beside* its error, which is not always the input: measured on a different entry, `idna.Lookup.ToASCII("xn--pypi-.org")` answers `("pypi.org", err)`. An entry is written once and matched forever, so acting on a refused conversion would put a domain nobody typed into the allow-list. (That second entry never reaches the conversion here — it is all-ASCII, and the grammar refuses its trailing hyphen — which is why it serves as the measurement rather than the example.) An ASCII entry is stored exactly as typed, case included, since the matcher folds. *Evidence: internal/egress/hostset.go CanonicalEntry; internal/api/environments.go parseNetworking and internal/api/vaultcredauth.go canonicalAllowedHost; internal/egress/hostset_test.go TestCanonicalEntryStoresOneAlphabetPerHost and TestACanonicalizationErrorNeverBecomesAnotherHost, internal/api/vaults_test.go TestCredentialAllowedHostsStoreTheALabel.* *Tracked: #78 (whether the reference accepts a U-label entry, and whether it echoes the U-label or the A-label); landed for #609.*
- **Dream create — exactly one `memory_store` input and exactly one `sessions` input** — The guide says a dream reads "a pre-existing memory store" and "1 to 100 sessions", and its limits table caps sessions per dream at 100, but the schema bounds neither `inputs[]` nor `session_ids[]`: the cardinality rule is prose alone. This platform reads it literally — exactly one input of each type, with 1-100 ids in the `sessions` one — and answers anything else with a 400 `invalid_request_error`: two `memory_store` inputs, two `sessions` inputs, either arm missing, an empty array, 101 ids. Two `sessions` inputs merged into one set is the reading this refuses, and it is the plausible one, since an array of a union is the shape a merging server would choose. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide and its limits table; BetaDreamNewParams — `inputs[]` with no bounds, checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — betadream.go BetaDreamNewParams, and `BetaCreateDreamRequest` (no `minItems`/`maxItems` on either array), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaCreateDreamRequest.properties.inputs and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaDreamSessionsInput.properties.session_ids; internal/api/dreams.go; docs/plan/41_dreams.md §2.4 and §5.2* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 7 — whether the reference accepts two `sessions` inputs, and if so whether it merges them or takes one).*
- **Dream create — a missing input store is a 404; an archived one and a missing input session are 400s; an archived input session is accepted** — Nothing in the spec or the guide says what a dream does with an input it cannot use *at create*; the guide speaks only of an input archived or deleted **mid-run**, which fails the dream with `input_memory_store_unavailable` or `input_session_unavailable`. This platform validates at create rather than deferring. A well-formed `memory_store_id` naming no store is a 404 `not_found_error`, the rule session create follows since #668 — no dream create was recorded, so it rests on the cross-resource evidence the unknown-store attachment entry above sets out, where an id naming nothing is a 404 on every route the recordings reach. An archived store and a session id naming no session are each a 400 `invalid_request_error`; the same evidence would point a missing session at a 404 too, which this platform has not adopted — recording item 6 would settle both. A duplicated session id is a 400 too, since a set that counts one id twice against the 100 bound would quietly shrink the work the caller asked for. An **archived** input session is deliberately accepted: an archived session is exactly the finished transcript a consolidation job wants, and refusing it would make the feature useless for any session already put away. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (the mid-run failure types, and its silence on create); internal/api/sessionresources.go snapshotMemoryStore (the rule the missing store follows), internal/api/dreams.go checkDreamInputsExist, internal/api/dreams_test.go TestDreamCreateRejections; docs/plan/41_dreams.md §5.2 and §8.2 item 6* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 6 — the status and `error.type` the reference gives at create for a missing store, an archived store, a missing session, an archived session and a duplicated id).*
- **Dream create — an empty-string `model` is a 400** — The spec types `model` as `string | {id, speed?}` and bounds the object arm's `id` at 1-256 characters, but the bare-string arm carries no `minLength` at all, so `{"model": ""}` is schema-valid. This platform refuses it, on dreams and on every surface `parseModel` serves, because an empty id can only fail later and further from the caller. The line this draws is between malformed and merely unknown: a non-empty model this deployment cannot route is accepted at create by choice (the `model` at create entry among the divergences above), and only an id naming nothing at all is refused. *Evidence: `BetaDreamModelParams` (the `oneOf` whose string arm carries no `minLength`), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaDreamModelParams, and `BetaDreamModelConfigParams` (the object arm's bound), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaDreamModelConfigParams.properties.id; internal/api/wire.go parseModel, internal/api/dreams.go; docs/plan/41_dreams.md §5.2* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 6 — whether the reference refuses `model: ""`, and with what status and type).*
- **Dream create — an explicit null `output_behavior` or `instructions` is read as unset** — The spec types `output_behavior` as a non-nullable `BetaOutputBehavior` and `instructions` as nullable, so `"output_behavior": null` is schema-invalid where `"instructions": null` is not. This platform reads both as the field left out — `output_behavior` becomes the documented default `{type: create_new}`, `instructions` null — because that is its rule on every create route: an explicit null on an optional field is the unset value (`stringField`, `parseMetadata`; agents, environments, sessions and memory stores alike, pinned by `TestNullFieldLeniency`), and a dreams-only 400 would diverge from that rule rather than from the reference, whose answer to an explicit null is unrecorded on every surface — the plan 36 slice 1 review settled the same question for memory stores. The SDK never sends a null for either field, so no client following it can tell. *Evidence: `BetaCreateDreamRequest` (`output_behavior` a bare `$ref` with no null arm; `instructions` with one), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaCreateDreamRequest.properties.output_behavior and checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaCreateDreamRequest.properties.instructions; internal/api/dreams.go parseDreamOutputBehavior; internal/api/wire.go stringField and parseMetadata; internal/api/edge_test.go TestNullFieldLeniency; docs/HISTORY.md § "Memory stores — real `ant` CLI against `/v1/memory_stores` (plan 36 slice 1, run 2026-08-25)" (the refuted null finding)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 6 — what the reference answers to `output_behavior: null` and `instructions: null` at create).*
- **Dream create — an `update_existing` target that is not the dream's own `memory_store` input is a 400, and a target the hold already holds is a 409 (plan 41 slice 4)** — The spec states the constraint in prose and gives it no status: "In EAP the store must be the job's own memory_store input, so the job consolidates the store in place." This platform answers a target naming any other store with a 400 `invalid_request_error`, `output_behavior.memory_store_id must be the job's own memory_store input`, checked before the inputs are read to exist — the platform's ordinary shape for a body whose fields contradict each other. A missing, null or empty `memory_store_id` under `update_existing` is a 400 of its own, which the schema does settle: the spec lists the field in that arm's `required` with `minLength: 1`. The 409 beside it is the spec's, not an inference — `BetaTargetStoreHeldError`'s `type` is the const `conflict_error`, its description names the release conditions, and it ends "Carried with `x-should-retry: false`", all of which this platform matches. What stays inferred is only the first status: whether the reference calls a wrong target a 400, and whether its message is this one. *Evidence: `BetaOutputBehaviorUpdateExisting` (the `required` list and `minLength: 1`), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaOutputBehaviorUpdateExisting, and `BetaTargetStoreHeldError` (`type` const `conflict_error`, `required: [type]`, the `x-should-retry: false` sentence), checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaTargetStoreHeldError, with no generated Go type for either — `shared.ErrorType` lists nine members and none is a conflict; platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (the EAP sentence, with no status); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §2.4 and §5.3; internal/api/dreams.go (the target rule and the hold's unique-violation catch), internal/api/errors.go (`errTypeConflict`, `apiErrorWithHeaders`), internal/store/migrations/0034_dreams.sql `dreams_target_hold_idx`* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 6 — the status and message the reference gives a target that is not the input).*
- **A dream's pipeline runs on an internal agent and environment that no list and no id-addressed route shows** — A session renders its `agent.id` and `environment_id` to any viewer, so whatever the reference runs a dream on has ids a client can read off the pipeline session and try to fetch; nothing published says what those fetches answer. This platform creates one agent and one `cloud` environment on the first dream a deployment runs, marks both `internal`, and hides them completely: absent from `GET /v1/agents` and `GET /v1/environments`, a 404 from every route addressing them by id, and resolvable by the runner alone — a session create, a deployment create and a roster member each refuse them with that same 404. Without the gate, any developer key could open its own session on an always-allow agent no operator created or can see. The spec's description of a thread's agent as possibly "an inline-defined (ephemeral) agent snapshot" is the hint that the reference's dream agent is equally unfetchable, and is the reading this follows. *Evidence: the thread-agent union's "inline-defined (ephemeral) agent snapshot", checked against anthropic-sdk-go v1.70.1 — spec components.schemas.BetaManagedAgentsSessionThreadAgentEntry; docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.3 and §4.4; internal/store/migrations/0034_dreams.sql (the `internal` columns), internal/api (the resolvers that refuse an internal row)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 1 — whether `GET /v1/agents/{id}` and `GET /v1/environments/{id}` resolve the ids a completed dream's pipeline session renders, and what the agent spec holds if they do).*
- **A dream's pipeline session is listed like any other, and the agent id it renders does not resolve** — The guide says a dream's session can be streamed and is archived rather than deleted when the dream ends, but not whether it appears in `GET /v1/sessions` beside the caller's own. Here it does: the session carries no hidden flag, so it is listed, retrievable and streamable exactly as the reference's is said to be — while the `agent.id` it renders answers 404, because that row is internal. A client walking the session list therefore meets one session whose agent it cannot fetch. Hiding the session too was rejected: the reference exposes it, and a `session_id` a client cannot read back would be a worse divergence than an agent id it cannot. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide ("You can stream that session's events"); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §2.7 and §4.4; internal/api (the session list, which this plan leaves alone)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 1 — whether the reference lists its pipeline session in `GET /v1/sessions`, and whether the agent id that session renders resolves).*
- **A dream's pipeline session has a null `created_by`** — The runner creates the session from a background sweep carrying no request context, so nothing attributes it: `created_by` renders null, exactly as a scheduled deployment fire's session does here. The dream row keeps the caller's own `created_by` for audit, which is where the attribution a reader wants actually lives. What the reference stamps on a session it creates for itself — a null, a service actor, or the caller who created the dream — is unrecorded. *Evidence: docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.2 step 6; internal/api (the deployment-run precedent this follows, and the dream row's own `created_by`)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 1 — the `created_by` a completed dream's pipeline session renders).*
- **A dream's transcripts are `file` rows, visible in `GET /v1/files` while it runs and deleted when it closes** — Nothing published says how the reference puts a session's transcript in front of the pipeline. This platform renders each input session to markdown and mounts it as a `file` session resource, one per transcript plus an `INDEX.md`, so the rows are listed in `GET /v1/files` under `dream/<drm>/…` filenames while the dream runs, undeletable through `DELETE /v1/files/{id}` for as long as it owns them, and deleted by the same pass that archives the session — the rows in its transaction, the objects enqueued for the drain by that same transaction, so a store that refuses loses no key (#703), bar one another session mounted, whose copy keeps that object (#578). Each mounts as itself, never through the session copy every other file mount mints (#578), so the pipeline session holds no copy that would keep a transcript's object alive past that pass. After that the archived session's `resources[]` keeps naming file ids that answer 404, the dangling reference plan 08 decision 2 already accepts and the executor already tolerates. The transcripts are derivable from the input sessions at any time, and a hundred rows per dream accumulating in a caller's file list buys nothing. *Evidence: docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.5 and §4.6; internal/store/migrations/0034_dreams.sql (`files.dream_id`), internal/api (the rows the start arm inserts and the closing pass deletes), internal/executor (a missing mount costs its own mount, never the run)* *Tracked: #78 (recording items 1 and 2 — what a completed dream's session `resources[]` holds, and whether transcripts are visible as files at all while the reference's dream runs).*
- **A `create_new` dream's output store — its name, description, metadata, and the actor on its cloned versions** — The guide says a `create_new` dream clones the input store and reports the clone in `outputs[]`, and stops there: the clone's own fields are unpublished. This platform names it the input's name followed by ` (dream <token>)`, `<token>` being the dream id's suffix, with the input's portion truncated so the suffix always survives the 255-rune bound; copies the description; leaves `metadata` empty; and writes each cloned memory as one `created` version attributed to a `session_actor` naming the pipeline session. The distinct name is for the caller — the two stores are told apart in a list, and mounted in one session each takes its own slug rather than a collision suffix. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (`create_new` clones the input store), with docs/plan/41_dreams.md §2.7 listing what it leaves unsaid; docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.2 step 4; internal/api (the clone the start arm writes)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 3 — the output store's `name`, `description` and `metadata`, and the `operation` and `created_by` on its cloned memory versions).*
- **A canceled dream's session ends `idle`, not `terminated`, wherever the interrupt could move it** — The guide says cancel moves a `pending` or `running` dream to `canceled` immediately and that its `usage` "might continue to update for a few seconds while in-flight work winds down", without saying what becomes of the session. Here cancel runs this platform's ordinary session interrupt in the same transaction — outstanding calls settled, queued work canceled, the session folded to `idle` on `stop_reason.end_turn` — and a later tick mirrors the final `usage` and archives it. The interrupt moves a `running` or `idle` session, which is every status v1 writes, so a canceled dream's session is normally an archived `idle` one; `rescheduling` and `terminated` it deliberately leaves alone, and the closing arm then archives the session at whatever status it holds once its live children have ended, which can itself move the status (#731). `terminated` is what this platform reserves for a session that ended badly and what the runner reads as an `internal_error` wherever it finds one. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (the cancel rules and the winding-down `usage`); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.1 and §4.6; internal/api (the interrupt arm the cancel handler and the runner share)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 5 — a canceled running dream's session `status` and `stop_reason`, and its `usage` over the following ten seconds).*
- **A `user.message` sent to a running dream's pipeline session is refused with a 400** — The reference invites a client to stream the pipeline session and never says whether it will also accept events on it. This platform refuses, along with every other mutation of that session while the dream owns it, for the reason argued among the divergences above under the pipeline session being read-only: a hidden always-allow agent with `bash` must not be steerable by whoever holds the session id. What is inferred is only the reference's own answer — it might accept the send and let the message join the pipeline's context, ignore it, or refuse it with a status and type of its own, and nothing published chooses between them. *Evidence: platform.claude.com managed-agents dreams guide (streaming documented, sending not mentioned); docs/plan/41_dreams.md §4.4 and §8.2 item 2; internal/api (the dream-ownership refusal in the send handler)* *Tracked: #78 (recording item 2 — a `user.message` sent to a running dream's pipeline session, and the same send once the dream is archived).*
- **The reference's recorded error sentences, answered where no recording reaches (#540, #841, #842)** — #540 gives a refusal the reference's recorded `message` wherever a recording holds one for the same route, status and type. Where the condition sits in a helper several routes or inputs share, the recorded sentence goes with the helper, so the routes and inputs below answer it too, unrecorded; that the reference words them alike is inferred from its answering each family through one validator wherever it was recorded. A sentence recorded on one route that had no such helper beneath it stays there, and the other routes keep this platform's words. Each extension, with the helper that carries it and where its sentence was recorded: **`Failed to parse request body: unknown field "…"`** (rejectUnknownKeys, rejectUnknownNested; recorded on agent create, deployment create and a deployment's repository resource, session create and its memory-store resource, vault create, credential create, and a credential update's `auth.refresh`) on agent update, and a coordinator's `multiagent` object and its roster entries on create and update; deployment update, a deployment's `schedule` and `agent` objects and its file and memory-store resources; session update, a session create's file and repository resources and a repository's `checkout`, the file resource POST …/resources adds, and a repository token rotation; the event batch's top level on POST …/events; dream create and its `model`, `inputs` and `output_behavior` objects; memory-store create and update; memory create and update and an update's `precondition`; vault update; and a credential update's top level and every credential `auth` object but the update's `refresh`. **`<path>: maximum string length is N`** (capRunes; recorded for `system` on agent create) on `name` and `description` on create and on `agent.name`, `agent.description` and `agent.system` on update — the agent caps entry below has the counting unit. **`invalid metadata: metadata map would have N entries, exceeding the maximum of 16`** (validateAgentMetadataCaps; recorded on agent create) on agent update. **`Failed to parse request: agent.model.effort: "…" is not a valid value; …`** and the `inference_geo` sentence (parseAgentModel; recorded on agent update) on agent create, as `model.effort`; and the `configs[]` refusals (parseAgentTools; recorded on agent create) on agent update. **`Failed to parse request: initial_events[i].type: "…" is not a valid value`** (errInitialEventType; recorded for `user.interrupt`) on every other type a session's `initial_events` refuses — `system.message`, the tool answers and an unknown type among them. **`Not Found`** (errUnknownPath; recorded once under each prefix, `GET /v1/deployments/{id}/runs` and `GET /v1/dreams/{id}/bogus`) on every unknown path under /v1/deployments/ and /v1/dreams/, and **`Not found`** on every other unknown path, from the handful recorded under /v1/ and the console's prefixes. **`Method Not Allowed`** (errMethodNotAllowed; recorded on a dream, the work poll, an environment key's token path and a console API key) on every route's unsupported method. **`x-api-key header is required`** (requireAPIKey; recorded once, on GET /v1/models, a path not served here) on every management-lane request that offers no key, but for a revoked environment key this platform minted and a live one on a route recorded refusing it, offered with no human credential beside them, which take the reference's own answers (*A revoked environment key on a management route*). **The roster refusals** (resolveRoster; recorded on agent create — `Agent has invalid configuration: subagent … referenced multiple times`, `… version N not found`, `… is archived`, `… is not a valid agent ID` and `… has its own subagents; maximum depth is 1`, `Failed to parse request: multiagent.agents[i]: must be a string or an object (got null)`, `multiagent.agents.i: at most one {"type":"self"} entry is allowed` and `multiagent.coordinator.agents: must contain at least 1 item`) on agent update, where the three that name a path open it with `agent.` as the update route was recorded doing elsewhere (agentUpdatePath). **`Environment env_… not found.`** (errEnvironmentNotFound; recorded on environment delete and archive, session create, and the console's token list and issue) on environment get and update, on a malformed environment id on all four environment routes, and on a deployment create or update naming an unknown environment. **`Skill not found: …`** (errSkillNotFound; recorded on skill get and delete, the versions list, a version's get and its content) on version create and delete and on a malformed skill id. **`Resource not found: …`** (errResourceNotFound; recorded on a resource's get and delete) on a repository token rotation. **`Credential not found.`** (errCredentialNotFound; recorded on credential get, update and delete) on credential archive. **`Cannot modify archived deployment`** (loadDeployment; recorded on deployment update, pause and run) on unpause. **``File `…` not found.``** and **`Session not found: …`** (errFileNotFound, getSession; each recorded for a deleted id on its GET) on a malformed id there. **The heartbeat 412's `actual was …`** (mapWorkErr; recorded only with a six-digit fraction) without a fraction on a whole second, as Python's isoformat renders one. **#841 carries the rule from sentences to statuses**, where the reference answers an input apart from the one this platform had folded it into, and each recorded answer goes with its helper the same way: **`Invalid agent ID.`** (checkAgentPathID; recorded as a 400 on agent get, for the prefix-less `undefined`) on agent update, versions and archive, and on every id the shape *Malformed and absent ids — the one shape agent and thread ids are read by* (below) calls malformed, while a well-formed id naming no agent — a reference-format one, or the runner's fixed `agent_dreamrunner` — keeps the 404; the same GET under the console's cookie lane was recorded answering `Failed to parse request body: unknown field "agent_id"` instead (2026-09-04 batch1 #85, #96, #98), and every lane here answers the API key's. **`Invalid thread ID: …`** (threadIDs; recorded as a 400 on a thread get with an `sth_` prefix) on a thread's archive, events and stream, and on every `{tid}` that shape calls malformed — the shape an interrupt's claim is held to. **`Thread not found: …`** (RouteInbound's ThreadNotFoundError; recorded as a 404 for an interrupt naming another session's thread) on an interrupt naming a well-formed id no thread carries at all, which misses the same lookup. **`Invalid work ID format: expected session_*, cse_*, or work_* prefix, got "…".`** (checkWorkID; recorded as a 400 on the update route, a POST to `…/work/poll`) on a work item's get, ack, heartbeat and stop, for an id carrying none of those prefixes nor `sesn_` — the prefix the reference's own work ids carry, which its routes were recorded taking (*Work item `id` — a `work_` id here, the session's id there*) — while an id carrying one of the four is still the 404 an unknown item gets — after a lookup for a `work_`, `sesn_` or `session_` id in this platform's alphabet, and without one for a `cse_` id or a reference-format `sesn_` one, which domain.Valid refuses, since this platform mints neither. **A tool result's and a custom tool result's `session_thread_id`** is accepted and dropped, as a tool confirmation's was recorded being (*POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — inbound `session_thread_id`*): RouteInbound routes all three answers by one lookup of the call they answer. **#842 carries it to one validator's other sentence, to the waiting refusal, and to the `x-should-retry: false` header**, each going with its helper the same way. **`reclaim_older_than_ms: Input should be a valid integer, unable to parse string as an integer`** (pydanticInt; its ≥1 sentence recorded for that parameter on the work poll, this one for the console token list's `limit`) on an unparseable reclaim window, which this platform used to read as the default. pydanticInt's reading of a string is lax pydantic's as inferred, on the console's `limit` and `offset` and the reclaim window alike: surrounding whitespace is stripped, as Python's int() strips it, so a query's `+5`, which decodes to " 5", is 5; an integer past any machine integer is still one, bounded like any other — taken (saturated, then the caller's cap) over the minimum, the minimum's sentence under it; and digits followed by anything else, a float spelling or an underscore one are not an integer, none recorded either way. **The waiting refusal** (events.CheckWhileAwaiting; recorded for one `user.message` beside one custom call) on a `user.define_outcome`, beside a pending confirmation or a `self_hosted` worker's call, and naming several calls — *POST /v1/sessions/{id}/events — a message while the primary awaits a response* has the reading. **`x-should-retry: false`** (noRetry; *`x-should-retry: false` — on the refusals recorded carrying it*) on every refusal under /api/, where every one recorded carries it; through errUnknownPath's `Not found` on every unknown path outside /v1/deployments/ and /v1/dreams/; through errSkillNotFound on a version's create and delete and a malformed skill id; through checkSkillVersion on every malformed version slot; through the skill upload's refuse on every bundle refusal, its length and symlink sentences having been recorded only where the header was not captured; through the dead environment key's 401 on every route that lane serves; through workScope's 403 on every work route; through the environment validators on an update; and through requireAPIKey on every management-lane request that offers no key. A malformed id's 404 takes it where the absent id's does — an environment's on delete and archive, a file's on GET — so the two stay indistinguishable. *Evidence: the helpers named, in internal/api (wire.go, deploymentparse.go, sessions.go, server.go, environments.go, skills.go, sessionresources.go, vaultcredentials.go, deployments.go, files.go, workapi.go, threads.go, consoleapi.go pydanticInt, errors.go noRetry, auth.go, envauth.go, skillsupload.go) and internal/events (inbound.go threadClaim, route.go RouteInbound, awaiting.go CheckWhileAwaiting), and internal/unknownkey Least; the recordings cited in each helper's doc comment; internal/api/errors_internal_test.go TestAHeartbeatMismatchRendersTheLastBeatAsTheReferenceDoes; internal/api/agents_test.go TestAgentPathRefusesAMalformedID, internal/api/threads_test.go TestThreadsPrimaryOnEverySession, internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkIDFormatIsAnsweredAsTheReferenceAnswersIt, internal/api/threadexec_test.go TestInboundThreadClaimValidation, internal/events/inbound_edge_test.go TestNormalizeInboundDropsAnAnswersThreadClaim, internal/api/workapi_test.go TestWorkPollRefusesAReclaimWindowUnderOne, awaiting_test.go TestWhatTheSendWaitsOn, noretry_test.go TestXShouldRetryFollowsTheRecordings.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording on any route or input named here would confirm or correct its sentence, its status, or its header, there); landed for #540, #841 and #842.*
- **Malformed and absent ids — the one shape agent and thread ids are read by (#841)** — Where the reference answers a malformed id apart from an absent one — an agent path, a thread path, an interrupt's `session_thread_id`, a roster member — this platform reads the id by one shape, domain.WellFormedID, and the `agent_id` filters on the sessions and deployments lists read one by it too, so they agree with the agent routes on what an agent id is: the resource's prefix, then a non-empty token of ASCII letters and digits other than `I`, `O` and `l`. Malformed is the 400, in the reference's words where they were recorded; well-formed goes on to the lookup, and absent is its 404. The recordings fix the edges. Refused as malformed: `undefined` on agent get (2026-09-02 batch2 #115), `sth_…` on a thread get and an interrupt (#337, #332), `sthr_01UnknownThreadIdXXXXXXXXX` on an interrupt (#331) and `agent_01UnknownAgentIdXXXXXXXX` in a roster (#123). Read as well-formed: every reference id they carry — `sthr_01DdMGc4KudV1Z22t2L7Y9QH` answered `Thread not found` for another session (#333), `agent_01UreT9PZKHtpNLgeSGzPCQh` read 200 (#136). #123's token is 24 characters like every real one, so the `I` both refusals carry is what decides, not the length. The character set is the union of base58, which the reference's ids carry after their `01` and which has no `0`, `I`, `O` or `l`, and the alphabet this platform mints, which needs the `0` — so every id either side mints is well-formed, the runner's fixed `agent_dreamrunner` among them, which plan 41 §4.4 needs to answer the hidden row's 404 rather than a 400. That an `O`, an `l` or any byte but a letter or digit is malformed too is the inference: only the `I` is recorded. Only letters and digits pass, so nothing unstorable reaches a bind parameter. The one recorded exception is the memory store a session create attaches: the reference looked up `memstore_01UnknownStoreIdXXXXXXXXX`, `I` and all, and answered 404 (#160), so it parses that id differently or not at all, and session create reads it by the lookup's looser shape — the prefix and a non-empty storable token, consoleIDShape, by which the console namespace already reads its ids (2026-09-05 batch8 idx 22, batch5 idx 10–12). Every other id keeps domain.Valid, the shape this platform mints, where telling the two apart changes nothing on the wire: a malformed environment, session, file or skill id, or a work item's token past its prefix, is the 404 an absent one gets, and a deployment's or a dream's memory store and the sessions list's `memory_store_id` and `deployment_id` filters keep their 400s (ours, unrecorded). *Evidence: internal/domain/id.go WellFormedID and id_test.go TestWellFormedID; internal/api/wire.go checkAgentPathID, threads.go threadIDs, roster.go checkAgentID, sessions.go and deployments.go (the `agent_id` filters), sessionresources.go parseMemoryResource and consoleapi.go consoleIDShape; internal/events/inbound.go threadClaim; managed-agents-wire-recordings 2026-09-02/batch2.json idx 115, 123, 136, 160, 331–333, 337; internal/api/agents_test.go TestAgentPathRefusesAMalformedID and TestAgentIDFiltersReadIDsAsTheAgentRoutesDo, threads_test.go TestThreadsPrimaryOnEverySession, threadexec_test.go TestInboundThreadClaimValidation, hiddenrows_test.go TestInternalRowsAreUnlistedAndUnretrievable and TestInternalRowsRefusedByEveryResolver.* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of an id carrying an `O`, an `l` or a byte outside the letters and digits, on any of these surfaces, would confirm or correct the rule); landed for #841.*
- **agent.thinking — one per thinking or redacted_thinking block the model returns (plan 60)** — The brain appends one content-free `agent.thinking` per block, `redacted_thinking` included, and a Claude 5-generation block whose text is omitted (an empty `thinking_delta`, then its `signature_delta`) counts as one. Before plan 60 the anthropic adapter dropped a `redacted_thinking` block, so it appeared as no event. Whether the reference emits an `agent.thinking` for a redacted block, and how many for a turn's several blocks, is not recorded. *Evidence: internal/brain/stream.go streamTurn (KindRedactedThinking); internal/brain/thinking_test.go TestOmittedAndRedactedThinkingReplay* *Tracked: #78 (a recording of a turn with redacted or several thinking blocks would confirm or correct the count).*
